- **Webview forms gain a "Plane" select.** `EditsPanel`'s `planeSelectField()` (populated via new `setPlanes(planes)`) offers `Custom (typed vectors)` plus each saved plane's name; picking a plane fills+disables the `planePoint`/`planeNormal` inputs with that plane's vectors and records `planeId` in the draft. `buildOpForPanel`'s `mirror`/`draft`/`splitByPlane`/`section` cases carry `planeId` through `withExprs`. Live preview (`handleOpPreview` in `provider.ts`) re-resolves the draft against fresh `readPlanes` so a `planeId` preview reflects the current plane.
- **MCP: `OP_PARAM_DOCS` entries for the four kinds now document `planeId`, and every replay-consuming `readEdits` site goes through `readEditsResolved`.** `mcpTools.ts`'s `readEditsResolved` (readEdits + readPlanes + resolvePlaneRefs) replaces `readEdits` at all pipeline replay sites; `apply_edit_ops` and `run_parametric_script`'s `compileAndApplyScript` resolve the combined `[...current.ops, ...accepted]` before `writeEdits` and `loadBRep`, so a `planeId`-only op is persisted with its cache vectors immediately.
- **`opCatalog.ts`'s `referencedEntities` now includes `planeId` (and `midplaneFaces`, a pre-existing omission) for the four kinds**, so hover tooltips correctly report "mentioned by op N" for a plane reference.

**A real defect found incidentally while using `AnnotationsModel` as the template: `clone` omitted `tolerance`.** It runs on both `push` and `list`, so a freshly-pinned band was dropped before it could be persisted, and `renderAnnotationsList`'s `a.tolerance` was therefore **always `undefined`** — the entire tolerance-band feature (persistence, the decorated label, the out-of-band colouring) was silently inert, with every automated test passing. Fixed, with a regression test verified to fail against the original code. The general lesson: any field added to `Annotation` must be copied in `clone`, and a field-by-field clone is where that obligation hides.

## Query-based selection filters (roadmap item, closed — Phase 1)

`Select ▾` now filters by shape, not just by clicking. Phase 1 (S) is entirely client-side: a registry-driven predicate dropdown whose options follow the active pick mode — faces: `Normal ±X/±Y/±Z`, `Planar`, `Area ≥/≤`, `Largest/Smallest N`; lines: `Along X/Y/Z` (chord direction, sign-insensitive), `Length ≥/≤`, `Longest/Shortest N` — plus a numeric threshold field (`argKind: "value"|"count"` drives its enable/disable and `"value"/"N"` placeholder), a `No seams` seam-exclusion toggle (`userData.smooth === true` lines dropped before any other test), and two buttons: **Select** (replace selection) / **Add** (union). Volume/point modes grey the whole form (mesh sources' dedup-by-group asymmetry is deferred with Phase 2). Deliberately the curated vocabulary future items must reuse — item 10's "tangential chain / same surface type / same producing op" context-menu entry point, and Tier 4's selector-synthesis predicate AST, are both specified to reuse this vocabulary rather than invent a second.

- **`src/webview/selectFilters.ts` (new, THREE yes, DOM no, unit-tested)**. `faceArea` sums triangulated area (exact for planar faces, **strictly under for curved** — pinned as a test: a single-quad 90° cylindrical patch facets to `√2 ≈1.414`, analytic `π/2 ≈1.5708`, faceted < analytic). `faceNormal` is area-weighted mean; `faceIsPlanar` checks every triangle normal within `DIRECTION_TOLERANCE_DEG = 5°` of that mean. Reuses `measurement.ts`'s `polylineLength`/`angleBetweenVectors` for edge helpers; `collectTargets` already `traverseVisible`s hidden-subtree exclusion, so a filter never matches a hidden part. Host never sees normals — `EncodedMesh` carries only positions/indices, but `geometryBuilder` computes vertex normals locally.

- **`viewerDom.ts`'s `#filter-group`** inside `#select-dropdown`: row 1 = `<select id="filter-pred">` + text input `inputmode="decimal"`; row 2 = checkbox + two buttons. `setupSelectionControls` in `src/webview/main.ts` populates the dropdown from `FACE_FILTERS`/`LINE_FILTERS` whenever the pick mode changes (including `setSelectableModes`'s mesh-source path, via an exposed `__syncFilterUi`), enables the arg field only when the selected option's `argKind !== "none"`, and on Select/Add collects via `collectTargets`, applies the pure predicate, and bulk-injects into `SelectionSet` (`clear()`+`add()` then one `renderHighlight()`).

- **Verified**: `selectFilters.test.ts` (15 cases) against hand-built fixtures — planarity, area exact vs under-estimate, normal-direction ±tolerance boundary (tilted 10° face misses at 5°, hits at 11°), large/small ordering, sign-insensitive axis match, length thresholds, seam exclusion, degenerate geometry → empty result.

## Align and pattern (linear/circular array) ops (roadmap item, closed)

`mate` was the closest thing to an alignment op — a two-face coincident constraint, not a min/center/max axis alignment against an absolute coordinate — and there was no way to array-duplicate an existing solid at all (every `addX` op builds new geometry from scratch; nothing takes an existing, possibly-imported solid and stamps out N transformed copies of it). Two new `EditOp` kinds close both gaps, added to the **Assembly** category in the Edits panel alongside Explode/Mate.

- **`align`** (`{targets, axis: "x"|"y"|"z", extent: "min"|"center"|"max", to: number}`) translates each targeted solid along `axis` so its OWN bbox `extent` lands at the absolute coordinate `to`. **Deliberately stores a resolved coordinate, not a reference to an "anchor" solid** — matching this codebase's established "store resolved values, not live references" convention (e.g. primitive `center` params are literal `Vec3`s, never an id) — the panel can still let a user click "use this solid's extent" to COMPUTE `to` client-side before Apply, but the persisted op itself never needs id-rebinding logic for an anchor the way a Part's entity ids do. **Every targeted solid aligns INDEPENDENTLY, even when every solid in the model is selected** — `occtOperations.ts`'s `alignSolids` deliberately does NOT reuse `transformSolids`'s existing "transform the whole shape as one unit when every solid is targeted" fast path, since that path would compute the delta from the COMBINED bbox extent of the whole shape rather than each solid's own extent, silently changing behavior based on how many solids happen to be selected. Not topology-changing (a pure rigid move, like `translate`) and not B-rep only (mesh engine support via `THREE.Box3`).
- **`patternLinear`** (`{targets, direction, spacing, count}`) and **`patternCircular`** (`{targets, axisPoint, axisDir, angleDeg, count}`) each produce `count` TOTAL instances (the original plus `count - 1` new copies) — `count` includes the original, matching how most real CAD pattern tools count "instances," not "additional copies." Both are topology-changing (new solids appended) but NOT B-rep only — patterning is fundamentally the same rigid-transform building block `translate`/`rotate` already have on both engines, just applied `count - 1` times.
  - **OCCT: a genuinely novel hybrid of two existing patterns, not a variant of either alone.** `occtOperations.ts`'s `patternSolids` combines `transformSolids`'s "resolve `targets` via `collectSolids`" with `addPrimitive`'s "`compound(existing + new)`" append — every OTHER existing op either transforms a target IN PLACE (transform ops) or appends brand-new geometry with no pre-existing source to keep (add ops); pattern is the first op that needs to keep the original untouched AND append transformed copies of it in the same compound rebuild. Reuses the exact already-verified `BRepBuilderAPI_Transform_2(shape, trsf, /*copy=*/true).Shape()` call shape `transformSolids`'s own `rigid()` helper already uses for every other transform op — no new OCCT API surface, no new probing needed, since a "copy" transform is just the SAME rigid-transform call already verified elsewhere in this codebase.
  - **Mesh: `Object3D.clone(true)` (shared geometry/materials by reference, independent transform) + `applyMatrix4`**, tagged with a fresh `pattern-{K}` id — a DIFFERENT id namespace from primitives' `prim-{K}` (its own counter, `patternCount`, threaded through `applyEditsMesh`'s fold loop the same way `primCount` already is) since one `patternLinear`/`patternCircular` OP can produce MULTIPLE new tagged objects, unlike one `addX` op producing exactly one.
  - **A new `bboxExtent(oc, shape, cleanup): {min: Vec3, max: Vec3}` helper** (private to `occtOperations.ts`) fills the one gap the existing `bboxCenter`/`bboxDiagonal` helpers left — neither exposes a per-axis min/max, only a combined centre point or scalar diagonal length. Same `Bnd_Box_1` + `BRepBndLib.Add` + `CornerMin()`/`CornerMax()` pattern every other bbox reader in this codebase already uses (`Bnd_Box.Get()` is confirmed NOT bound in this WASM build).
- **New op-panel icons (`align`, `patternLinear`, `patternCircular`) went through the REAL TikZ→SVG pipeline** (`icons/tikz/{align,patternLinear,patternCircular}.tex` → `make svg-ops/<id>.svg` for each id individually, NOT the bare `make ops-ts` which would re-render and churn all 46 existing committed `svg-ops/*.svg` files against this environment's local TeX Live/poppler version — see the "Toolbar/panel icons" section's own "rebuild only the ids you changed" warning) → `node build-op-icons.mjs` to regenerate `src/webview/opIcons.ts`. Verified the targeted rebuild touched only the 3 new SVGs plus exactly 3 new lines in `opIcons.ts` (a `git diff --stat` check), not a wholesale rewrite. `icons/build-ops/` (the LaTeX scratch directory) was missing from `.gitignore` — a pre-existing gap from before the `tikz/` ops pipeline existed, fixed alongside this change since it would otherwise show up as untracked cruft on every contributor's first icon build.
- **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM through the REAL pipeline** (`npm run mcp:smoke`, not just unit tests): a box added off-axis, aligned so its z-min lands at exactly 0 (confirmed via `inspect`'s bbox, and that x/y stayed unchanged — align only touches the requested axis); `patternLinear` count:3 on that same box appends exactly 2 new solids evenly spaced 10 units apart along x (confirmed via each instance's `inspect`-reported centre); `patternCircular` count:4 on a fresh box appends exactly 3 new solids, each verified to stay the exact same radial distance from the rotation axis and the exact same z (rotation about a z-parallel axis never should move z). Pure-function coverage (`editOps.test.ts`'s validation branches, `meshEdits.test.ts`'s `applyMeshAlign`/`applyMeshPattern` — headless, no WASM, THREE.js objects only) covers the parts that don't need live OCCT.

## Transform gizmo (roadmap item, closed)

Every transform used to be typed into text fields with no visual feedback until Apply. `Viewer` now embeds three.js's OWN `TransformControls` (from `three/examples/jsm/controls/TransformControls.js`, already shipped as part of the `three` dependency — no new package) as a **live preview only**: dragging never itself pushes an edit op, exactly preserving the "webview owns the op stack, host stays dumb" invariant — Apply is still the only thing that commits. Two existing decisions this had to respect rather than revert, both confirmed untouched: `onSelectPointerUp`'s 4px drag-tolerance click detection, and the numeric fields staying `type="text"` for parametric expressions.

- **A real API-version trap, caught by checking the ACTUAL installed source rather than trusting memory/docs.** Older TransformControls releases are widely documented as dispatching a literal `"dragging-changed"` event with an `{value: boolean}` payload — this codebase's installed version (three@0.185.1) has NO literal `"dragging-changed"` string anywhere in its source, which first looked like the event had been removed. It hadn't: `dragging` (like `camera`/`object`/`mode`/`axis`) is registered through the class's own generic `defineProperty(propName, default)` helper, whose setter unconditionally dispatches `{type: propName + '-changed', value}` on every value change — so `this.dragging = true/false` (assigned internally on pointerdown/pointerup) DOES genuinely fire `"dragging-changed"` with a real boolean `.value`, just constructed dynamically rather than appearing as a source literal a naive `grep` would find. The standard integration pattern (suspend `OrbitControls` while dragging) applies unchanged. Also verified this way: `.camera` is a genuinely reassignable accessor (needed for `setOrthographic()`'s perspective/ortho camera swap) and `getHelper()` returns the visual `Object3D` to add to the scene once.
- **The gizmo is always attached to a dedicated, permanently-scene-resident, geometry-free proxy (`Viewer.gizmoProxy`) — never directly to a real model object.** A drag typically needs to move a WHOLE selection (possibly several solids) as one rigid group about their shared bbox centroid, which is a capability TransformControls' native single-object `attach()` doesn't have; `attachTransformGizmo(pivot, mode)` resets the proxy to `position=pivot, quaternion=identity, scale=(1,1,1)` on every (re)attach, which is what makes `getGizmoDelta()` trivial — the proxy's CURRENT transform after any drag directly IS the delta (only position needs the pivot subtracted back out; rotation/scale need no subtraction since their base is always identity/(1,1,1) by construction).
- **Per-target delta application is pure, DOM-free math in a new `src/webview/gizmoTransform.ts`**, unit-tested headless (mirrors `cameraControls.ts`'s "pure Three.js math lives in its own file" convention — `THREE.Vector3`/`THREE.Quaternion` have no DOM dependency). `applyRotateDelta`/`applyScaleDelta` both revolve/scale a target ABOUT THE PIVOT, not about the target's own position — an off-centre target's position changes too, not just its orientation/size (verified via a target exactly AT the pivot, where the position term correctly reduces to zero). The scale formula (`pivot + scaleDelta·(basePosition − pivot)`, applied component-wise) is the identical affine `occtOperations.ts`'s own non-uniform-scale edit op already uses server-side (`gp_GTrsf`'s `SetValue(1,4, cx - sx*cx)` term is the same formula, just written per-component), so the live preview and the eventual real replay agree on what "scale about a centre" means. `quaternionToAxisAngle` (no THREE built-in for this) decomposes the proxy's rotation delta into the `axisDir`/`angleDeg` fields the `rotate` op actually needs — this generic decomposition handles every TransformControls rotate interaction uniformly (single-axis rings AND the free/screen-facing ring) with no special-casing per handle.
- **Per-target bases are captured fresh at EVERY drag start, not once per form-open** — reusing `explodePreview.ts`'s exact never-compound-onto-the-previous-frame discipline, but deliberately allowing MULTIPLE drags to compose before Apply: a second drag (without clicking Apply in between) captures its base from wherever the first drag's preview left things, letting a user refine a move/rotate/scale across several drags. `cancelGizmoPreview()` (mirroring `cancelExplodePreview()`) resets every target back to its pristine captured base and is called before every real op-stack commit (`onApplyTransform`) — the same reasoning as Explode's own cancel-before-push: the eventual model rebuild alone isn't synchronous enough (a B-rep edit is an async host round trip) to be trusted to supersede a stale live-dragged position without a visible flash of wrong geometry.
- **A field-overwrite-during-expression question the roadmap item explicitly asked to be decided: the drag silently wins.** `EditsPanel` gained public `setVecField`/`setNumField` setters the gizmo pushes live values through into the open form's inputs. Since `parseNumeric` (the existing expression-vs-literal reader) only ever reads an input's `.value` string FRESH at Apply time, overwriting that string with a plain numeric literal via a drag needs no separate "clear the pending expression" bookkeeping — the next read simply parses a plain number, same as if the user had typed over the field by hand. No prompt, no confirmation; documented here as the deliberate choice, not an oversight.
- **Selection changes (not just form changes) must also discard a stale preview.** `refreshGizmoAttachment()` — called both from `onFormChanged` (switching to/from translate/rotate/scale) and from `renderHighlight()` (every selection change) — unconditionally calls `cancelGizmoPreview()` before doing anything else. Without this, dragging solid A, then (without Apply) clicking to select solid B instead, would re-attach the gizmo to B's centroid while leaving A's now-orphaned live-previewed transform stranded forever (nothing would ever reset it, since `cancelGizmoPreview` was previously only reachable via the form-change path).
- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every other genuinely interactive/DOM-dependent feature in this codebase** (`clipCap.ts`, `labelOverlay.ts`, the external-change-reconciliation watchers, …): this environment cannot run a real Extension Development Host session, so the actual mouse-drag interaction, gizmo rendering, and pointer-event coexistence with entity-picking have NOT been exercised end-to-end. What WAS verified: the pure delta-application math (`gizmoTransform.test.ts`, 10 cases covering translate/rotate-about-pivot/scale-about-pivot/axis-angle decomposition including degenerate zero-rotation), `tsc`/`npx vitest run`/`npm run build` all clean, and a careful manual trace of the TransformControls pointerdown/pointerup event ordering against the actual installed source (confirming a gizmo-handle drag correctly suppresses `onSelectPointerDown`/`onSelectPointerUp`'s entity-pick path for that same pointer interaction, and that an ordinary click elsewhere while the gizmo is visible is completely unaffected). See "Verify a change" below for the manual F5 steps this needs before being trusted in production.

## Grid and entity snapping (roadmap item, closed)

The grid was purely visual — a bare `THREE.GridHelper` with a show/hide toggle and nothing quantizing to it — and picking always returned an entity id, never a snappable point. This item was explicitly blocked on the Transform Gizmo shipping first (nothing in the UI consumed a snapped *coordinate* before that); both snap modes now hook into the gizmo's translate drag.

- **Grid snap and entity-point snap use DELIBERATELY different granularities, not the same mechanism twice.** `gizmoTransform.ts`'s `snapTranslateDelta(positionDelta, gridSize)` rounds the SHARED drag delta ONCE, before the per-target loop — so a multi-target drag still moves as one rigid group with relative spacing exactly preserved. `nearestSnapPoint(position, candidates, tolerance)` instead runs PER TARGET, inside the loop, against each target's own candidate resulting position — entity-point snapping is inherently a per-object precision operation (aligning THIS object's corner onto some nearby existing point), and a multi-target drag with point-snap on may legitimately see different targets snap to different nearby points. When both are enabled, point-snap wins for whichever specific target found a candidate within tolerance; grid-snap still applies to any target that didn't.
- **The candidate set is `point-N` sprites only, not every mesh vertex — a deliberate, coherent scope, not a shortcut.** `point-N` entities (`extractVertices`, host-side) are the ONLY individually-tagged, always-fully-populated point entities in this codebase's object model — every B-rep vertex, unconditionally present whenever a model has any (see the "Bottom-up wireframe modeling" section above). FE-mesh overlay vertices are already display-only and excluded from picking; raw edge-line/face-triangle vertices were never separately tagged `Object3D`s at all. `collectSnapPoints()` (`main.ts`) is a plain `model.traverse()` collecting `THREE.Sprite` objects tagged `userData.entityType === "point"` — the exact same tag `geometryBuilder.ts`'s point-sprite builder already sets, so no new tagging convention was needed.
- **Snap tolerance is derived from the model's own scale, not a fixed constant** — `viewer.getModelExtents()!.diagonal * 0.01`, matching the same "proportional to model size" convention `pickThreshold`/`pointSpriteScale` already use inside `Viewer.frame()` (recomputed fresh on every drag frame here rather than cached, since it's cheap and the model's extents can't change mid-drag anyway).
- **Grid size is a plain session-only number (`#vc-grid-size`, defaulting to `1`), not a parametric-expression field.** Unlike the Edits panel's op params (which accept `L*2`-style expressions over document variables), this is a display/interaction preference with no reason to be parametric — a bad keystroke just falls back to the last-good value, no error toast, matching every other Appearance-group control's tolerant-input style. Both snap toggles (`#snap-grid`/`#snap-points`, View ▾ menu, `role="menuitemcheckbox"`) reuse ALREADY-EXISTING icons (`grid`/`point`) — no new icon-generation pass needed.
- **Deliberately does NOT try to reflect entity-point snap in the Translate form's `vec` field** — only grid snap (a single shared delta) has one coherent value to show; multiple targets independently snapping to different nearby points has no single "the delta" to display. The form field still reflects the grid-snapped (or raw) delta, same as before this feature for any target point-snap didn't override.
- **No new tests beyond the pure snap math** (`gizmoTransform.test.ts` grew 7 cases: `snapTranslateDelta` rounding/no-op-when-disabled, `nearestSnapPoint` nearest-wins/out-of-tolerance/empty-candidates) — same DOM/interaction verification gap as the Transform Gizmo item above, since this feature only exists inside that same live-drag code path. See "Verify a change" below.

## Split view — synchronized quad viewports (roadmap item, all 3 phases closed)

Tier 2 item 1, Phase 1: a 1×1 ⇄ 2×2 layout toggle (View ▾ → **Split view 2×2**) over the **one** scene/renderer/canvas — panes are `setViewport`/`setScissor` regions, per the standing single-WebGL-context invariant `orientationCube.ts` established. **Phase 2 (M, closed) adds a 1×2/2×1 picker and `.view.json` persistence**, so reopening a document restores each pane's camera exactly as you left it (see Phase 2 bullets below). **Phase 3 (S, closed) adds linked cameras across tabs** via a host relay — see Phase 3 bullets below.

- **`src/webview/viewerPanes.ts` (new, pure, unit-tested)** is the layout math: `computePaneRects` (row-major TL/TR/BL/BR, round-and-absorb-remainder so odd pixel counts still tile exactly with no gaps/overlaps), `paneAtPoint` (boundary points belong to exactly one pane), `ndcInPane` (pane-relative NDC for picking), `glViewportForPane` (top-left CSS → bottom-left GL rect for `setViewport`/`setScissor`/`TransformControls.viewport`).
- **`Viewer` holds `panes: PaneState[]`** (each pane: own persp/ortho pair + own `OrbitControls` + own `orthoHalfHeight`; projection is per-pane like direction/up/zoom), `focusedPane`, `layout`, `paneRects`. **Every pre-existing no-argument camera API targets the FOCUSED pane** — public signatures unchanged, so none of `main.ts`'s ~30 call sites changed. An edit-driven rebuild re-frames EVERY pane along its own direction (`fitAllPanes`); the model-derived globals (grid scale, `pickThreshold`, `pointSpriteScale`) are shared and idempotent per pane.
- **Per-event pane gating is a capture-phase `pointerdown`/`wheel` listener that runs BEFORE OrbitControls' bubble-phase handlers** (verified against the installed `OrbitControls.js`: its `_onPointerDown` first line is `if (this.enabled === false) return;`) — it computes the hovered pane, enables only that pane's controls, and moves focus on `pointerdown`. A gizmo drag suspends ALL panes' controls. **OrbitControls has NO viewport awareness** (sensitivity divides by the full-canvas `clientHeight`/`clientWidth`), so each pane's `rotateSpeed`/`panSpeed` are compensated by `canvasHeight / paneHeight` (=2 for quadrants) — exact for the uniform layouts Phase 1 ships.
- **`TransformControls` DOES have native sub-viewport support** in three@0.185 — its `viewport: Vector4 | null` property (CSS px, bottom-left origin) is honored by its internal `getPointer()` NDC math (verified against installed source) — so gizmo interaction inside a scissored pane needs zero hacks: `syncTransformControlsToFocus()` sets `.camera` to the focused pane's active camera and `.viewport` to its rect on every focus/layout/resize/projection change. **The gizmo-drag interaction itself is the one piece NOT probe-verified** (source-verified only) — see the F5 steps.
- **Entering 2×2: every new pane inherits the focused pane's exact view** (`inheritCameraState` copies both projections, up, near/far, ortho zoom/frustum height, orbit target); collapsing keeps the focused pane's `PaneState` as the single pane. The orientation cube renders into — and is only clickable within — the FOCUSED pane's corner; the measurement label (one shared sprite) is rescaled per pane inside the render loop, right before each pane's pass.
- **Stencil/clip-cap isolation per pane comes free from the scissor discipline**: with `setScissorTest(true)` active, each pane's `render()` clears color+depth+**stencil** within its own scissor rect before drawing, so clip-cap marking can never leak across panes — probed visually (all four panes' clipped renders match the known-good single-view clip statistically) rather than assumed, per the roadmap's own "silent failure" warning.
- **`onViewChanged` callbacks are remembered in `viewChangeCallbacks` and re-registered on every pane `makePane` creates** — without this, a pane created by a later `setPaneLayout` would silently never autosave `.view.json` (a REAL bug the probe caught: orbiting pane 3 posted no `viewChanged` until this fix). `scheduleViewSave` reads the FOCUSED pane; a non-focused pane's movement is a harmless identical-content write at worst.
- **`setOrthographic` now also copies `up` across the pair swap** (it previously copied position/near/far only) — needed so a per-pane TOP-view restore doesn't roll, and a strict improvement for the single-pane path too.
- **Wiring**: one checkable View ▾ row (`#split-view`, the same webview-side pattern as Grid — NO protocol message, no `provider.ts` change), two thin `pointer-events:none` DOM divider divs over the canvas (`#pane-divider-v/h`, z-index 4 — above the WebGL canvas, below the markup canvas), and `viewer.onFocusChanged` → `appearanceControls.reflectOrtho()` so the Persp/Ortho button label tracks the focused pane's own projection.
- **A fresh `Viewer` always starts at `"1x1"`** — this is what keeps `renderService.ts`'s headless harness (which posts no layout message) rendering exactly one full-canvas view; its `renderViewRequest` path is untouched. **The same session also fixed a latent defect there**: `renderSnapshot()` never posted `"viewState"` after `"geometry"` (commit f20816c fixed only `capture.mjs` for this exact regression class), so every `render_snapshot` image was a giant misframed close-up whenever Chromium was actually available — fixed by posting `{type:"viewState", view:null}` and verified visually via a probe render of `bull.stp`.

#### Phase 2 — 1×2/2×1 picker and `.view.json` persistence

- **`src/webview/viewerPanes.ts` gains two more layouts:** `"1x2"` (two side-by-side columns, vertical split) and `"2x1"` (two stacked rows, horizontal split) — `PANE_LAYOUTS` now four entries, `paneCount` 1/2/2/4, `computePaneRects` two new branches (same round-and-absorb-remainder tiling). The picker replaces the Phase-1 `#split-view` checkbox with a `#layout-group` `.tb-row` of four `data-layout` icon buttons (`1×1`/`1×2`/`2×1`/`2×2`), active-class reflects the current layout — same row pattern as `#select-group` — with `reflectLayoutPicker` and `setPaneDividersForLayout` (vertical for `1×2`/`2×2`, horizontal for `2×1`/`2×2`) keeping UI and dividers in sync from both the picker and the restore path.
- **`ViewState` (`src/protocol.ts`) gains optional `layout?: PaneLayoutId` and `panes?: PaneViewState[]`** (`PaneViewState {viewDirection, cameraUp, orthographic}` — only camera state is per-pane; display mode/clip stay global, matching Phase 1's scoping). Serialized as optional top-level siblings of `view` in `<model>.view.json` (`{version, source, view, layout, panes}`) — absent/`"1x1"` writes neither, so existing single-pane sidecars stay byte-stable. Older builds reading a new sidecar ignore unknown top-level keys and restore `view` alone; newer builds reading an old sidecar degrade to single-pane — purely additive, no version bump, per the tolerant-parse convention.
- **`src/viewStateSidecar.ts` tolerant parse** (via `PANE_LAYOUTS`/`paneCount` from the pure `viewerPanes.ts` — same cross-import precedent as `DISPLAY_MODES`): unknown `layout` → ignored as `"1x1"` and `panes` discarded; a bad per-pane entry (missing/degenerate `viewDirection`/`cameraUp`, wrong-type `orthographic`) falls back to `view`'s own direction/up/ortho for that pane; a short/long `panes` array is padded/truncated to `paneCount(layout)`.
- **`Viewer` gains `getPaneViewStates(): PaneViewState[]` and `applyPaneCameraState(index, state)`** (plus private `setPaneOrthographic(index, enabled)` — the indexed form of `setOrthographic`). `get` reads `cam.viewDirection(pane.active, pane.controls.target)` + `pane.active.up` + `instanceof OrthographicCamera`; `apply` copies `up` to BOTH cameras (so a later toggle doesn't reveal a stale up on the inactive one — the trap `setOrthographic` already documents), swaps projection if needed via the per-pane helper, and `framePane(index, dir)` — no model yet → just records up/ortho, idempotent once `setModel` provides a box. `applySpeedCompensation`'s doc now states why `1×2`/`2×1` stop being exact: rotate + perspective pan stay exact (both divide by `clientHeight`), but ortho's horizontal pan divides by `clientWidth` while the factor only corrects for height — `1×2` leaves ortho horizontal pan ~2× slow, `2×1` leaves it ~2× fast; one `panSpeed` cannot express per-axis factors.
- **`src/webview/main.ts`: layout picker wiring, per-layout dividers, and view-state gather/apply.** The `#layout-group` buttons toggle layout, reflect `.active`, sync dividers, and call `scheduleViewSave()` explicitly (the kept pane's camera didn't move, so `onViewChanged` wouldn't fire). `scheduleViewSave` now includes `layout`+`panes` via `getPaneLayout()`/`getPaneViewStates()` only when `layout !== "1x1"` (so a single-pane autosave writes the old shape). `applyViewState` applies `layout` first (`setPaneLayout` — a no-op when already there — plus `reflectLayoutPicker`/`setPaneDividersForLayout`), then each `panes[i]` via `applyPaneCameraState` (or the original focused-pane `setCameraUp`/`applyOrtho`/`frameFromDirection` path for a legacy single-pane state), then global `displayMode`/`clip` + `reflectOrtho()` for the focused pane. External-change reconciliation rides the existing `.view.json` watcher unchanged — same content-compare + post pattern.
- **Icons:** four new `icons/tikz-ui/layout1x1.tex` (outer square only) / `layout1x2.tex` (vertical midline) / `layout2x1.tex` (horizontal) / `layout2x2.tex` (both) — outer rectangle 18 mm + one center line, same 1.3 pt style as `grid.tex` — built per-id (`make svg-ui/<id>.svg`) and `node build-toolbar-icons.mjs` to regenerate `toolbarIcons.ts` (45 icons; `toolbarIcons.test.ts`'s expected-ids list updated).
- **Wiring CSS:** `.layout-btn` added to the existing `.tb-row` segmented-button rule groups (`.sel-mode`/`measure-tool-btn`/`markup-tool-btn`) in `media/viewer.css` — same secondary-background/active styling, no new sizing needed.
- **Verified via 3 passes (plus the unchanged Phase-1 probe):** (1) `npx tsx` round-trip: a `ViewState` with `layout:"1x2"` + two panes serializes to `{view, layout, panes}` and parses back identically; a single-pane state writes no layout/panes and a legacy sidecar parses as before; a bad layout is ignored and a bad pane entry falls back to `view`; (2) `npm run build` + `npx vitest run` (84/84, 1058/1058 — `toolbarIcons.test.ts` updated); (3) manual logic check: `1×2` tiles as two 500×800 columns, `2×1` as two 1000×400 rows, odd dimensions round-and-absorb-remainder — verified headless, matching the tiling guarantee every `computePaneRects` consumer relies on.
- **Verified via a Playwright probe against the real shipped `media/viewer.js`** (Node 20 + cached Chromium — this environment can now run it): all four panes render; a drag in pane 3 changes ONLY pane 3 (panes 0–2 pixel-identical, meanDiff 0.00); the cube moves to pane 3's corner on focus; a Vol-mode pick in pane 1 tints the model in ALL four panes (selection is global); clip-cap renders match the single-view clip per pane; `viewChanged` fires after a non-focused-pane orbit. Two probe-harness gotchas worth remembering: **the View ▾ dropdown's own capture-phase dismissal eats the first canvas click after toggling a menu item** (close the menu before interacting — a probe artifact, not a viewer bug), and screenshots must be compared with pane-relative coordinates (the dividers sit at the CANVAS center, i.e. page x = appX + width/2).
- **Verification gap, stated plainly**: the Transform Gizmo drag inside a split view and the human feel of the toggle are F5-only; see "Verify a change" below for the manual steps.

#### Phase 3 — linked cameras across tabs

- **`CadPreviewProvider` holds a provider-level registry `sessions: Map<string, EditorSession>`** (keyed by `document.uri.toString()`) plus a single `camerasLinked: boolean` flag — the relay's real cost the roadmap flagged. Two webviews cannot talk to each other (`supportsMultipleEditorsPerDocument: false` makes same-document-twice impossible, so one key per tab is safe); `provider.ts` already tracked only `activeSession` (single "which tab has focus" field for File commands), not every open session, so this registry is new. `EditorSession` gains `post(msg)` so the host can fan out without reaching into `resolveCustomEditor`'s closure. Register on open, delete on dispose (alongside the existing `activeSession` clear + watcher dispose + kernel-cache dispose); every other call site (`registerCommands`, `handleBRep` etc.) is untouched — `activeSession` stays the focus router.
- **`viewChanged` fans out a minimal `LinkedCameraState` triple `{viewDirection, cameraUp, orthographic}`** to every OTHER session when `camerasLinked` is true — not full `ViewState` (layout/displayMode/clip stay independent per document, per the plan's own scoping). Skip-self (`key === documentKey`) so the originator never receives its own relay. Debounce already lives in the webview (`VIEW_SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 500`), so relay rate is bounded for free with no new timer host-side.
- **`setCamerasLinked` (webview → host) sets the flag and broadcasts `camerasLinked` to EVERY session including the originator** so all View ▾ checkboxes stay truthful (`aria-checked`). `ready`-time: if `camerasLinked` is already true, the newly-opened tab gets a `camerasLinked{enabled:true}` immediately so its default-unchecked DOM starts correct (no protocol message is sent at all when the flag is false, so a fresh single-tab session pays nothing).
- **`LinkedCameraState` (`src/protocol.ts`) is the wire type** (`[number,number,number]` triples) — purely additive, no sidecar/version/MCP bump; `HostToWebview` gains `linkedCamera`/`camerasLinked`, `WebviewToHost` gains `setCamerasLinked`.
- **Webview: one `#link-cameras` `role="menuitemcheckbox"` row in View ▾** (`viewerDom.ts`, below the layout picker, above Screenshot — added a `tb-sep` before it), reusing the existing `view` icon (no new TikZ pipeline run; see "Toolbar/panel icons" for why a targeted rebuild churns every SVG). Click flips `aria-checked` optimistically then posts `setCamerasLinked`; authoritative state comes back via `camerasLinked` (last host write wins if two tabs toggle near-simultaneously).
- **Loop suppression is load-bearing, not optional.** `frameFromDirection`/`setOrthographic` end in `controls.update()` → `"change"` → `scheduleViewSave` → `viewChanged` → host relay → `linkedCamera` → `applyLinkedCamera` → `frameFromDirection` again → … without suppression this is an infinite 500 ms ping-pong. `applyLinkedCamera` sets `applyingLinkedCamera = true` BEFORE the calls, clears any pending `viewSaveTimer`, and `scheduleViewSave` no-ops while the flag is true (`hasAppliedInitialView || applyingLinkedCamera` gate) — same discipline as `hasAppliedInitialView`'s "opening ≠ a user change" guard. Also gated: `linkedCamera` is ignored until `hasAppliedInitialView` is true (no model yet — first local frame still comes from `viewState`/`resetView`). Never routes through `applyViewState` (that would also set layout/displayMode/clip).
- **Different extents already solved by `ViewState`'s own design: direction + up + ortho, never distance.** Receiver applies via `setCameraUp`/`applyOrtho`/`frameFromDirection` and reframes from **its own** bbox, so a small and a large model still both fill their pane (see plan's "Different extents" decision). `hidden` tabs still receive relays (`retainContextWhenHidden: true` already keeps webviews alive), so the mechanism works even when a tab isn't focused.

## SVG/DXF import → profile ops (roadmap items, closed)

No 2D vector format was supported in either direction. Closed via two pure parsers: `src/svgImport.ts` parsing an SVG's `<path d="...">` elements into flattened polylines, and `src/dxfImport.ts` parsing DXF's group-code/value `ENTITIES` (model space only) into the same profile ops — both imported as standalone `addPolyline`/`addLine`/`addCircle`/`addArc` ops — **genuinely no new kernel surface**, exactly as the roadmap predicted: `addPolyline` ("straight edges through points in order") etc. already existed for this. SVG was the first such format; DXF followed the same pattern for the CAM/laser/AutoCAD audience.

- **Regex-based `d`-attribute extraction, not a real XML parser — deliberate, for testability, not a shortcut.** This project's vitest config has no jsdom, so `DOMParser`/`document` aren't available in tests; `extractPathData`'s `<path\b[^>]*\bd\s*=\s*(?:"([^"]*)"|'([^']*)')[^>]*>` regex is fragile against pathological attribute ordering/escaping a real XML parser wouldn't be, but keeps the whole module unit-testable headless, matching the roadmap item's own framing ("a pure-TypeScript path parser, testable without the WASM").
- **Scope, stated plainly in the module's own doc comment, not a silent gap**: only `<path>` elements are read (no `<rect>`/`<circle>`/`<ellipse>`/`<polygon>`/`<polyline>` primitive shapes), and the `transform` attribute is NOT applied — a path drawn under a `translate`/`scale`/`rotate`/`matrix` imports at its raw, untransformed coordinates. Real Inkscape/Illustrator "trace outline" exports overwhelmingly use plain untransformed `<path>` elements for the traced result, which is the case this targets.
- **`parsePathData` implements the full path-COMMAND set** (M/m L/l H/h V/v C/c S/s Q/q T/t A/a Z/z — everything a `<path d>` can contain, as opposed to the shape-ELEMENT scope limitation above) including implicit-repeated-command coordinate runs, S/T's control-point reflection (correctly reset to "no reflection" after any non-C/S/Q/T command), and the SVG-spec elliptical-arc endpoint-to-centre conversion (Appendix F.6.5) for `A`/`a` — verified via `svgImport.test.ts` (22 cases) including a full circle drawn as two semicircular arcs, degenerate zero-radius/coincident-endpoint arcs degrading to a straight line rather than dividing by zero, and numbers glued together with no separator (`"1.5.5"` → `1.5`, `.5`, a real SVG authoring quirk the tokenizer regex handles by preferring the greedy `\d+\.\d+` alternative first).
- **Bezier/arc curves flatten to a FIXED sample count (`CURVE_SEGMENTS = 16`), not adaptive arc-length/curvature sampling** — `addPolyline` has no curved-edge representation to be more exact FOR, so uniform sampling is a reasonable, simple default; a poorly-approximated curve can always be re-imported at a different scale or refined by hand afterward.
- **Placement is a deliberate, simple default, not a configurable dialog**: SVG's Y-down coordinates are negated on import (every other coordinate this codebase shows the user — view axes, typed op fields — is Y-up; an unflipped import would look right only from directly above), placed flat in the XY plane at z=0, 1 SVG user unit = 1mm (this codebase's cascade unit). No placement/scale picker UI — a poorly-scaled or -oriented import is fixed with the EXISTING `translate`/`rotate`/`scale` ops afterward, same as any other placement adjustment, rather than adding new UI surface for something the pipeline already solves.
- **One `addPolyline` op per SUBPATH, not per `<path>` element** — a single `<path d="...">` with a hole (e.g. tracing the letter "O") parses into TWO subpaths (outer ring, inner ring) and becomes two separate ops, matching how `Build → Surface`/`Build → Volume` already treat each closed loop as its own pickable entity. A degenerate subpath (fewer than 2 points; a closed one with fewer than 3, or whose first/last point coincide after the Y-flip) is silently skipped — the exact same `validateEditOp` rejection `addPolyline` would apply anyway, just caught before pushing a doomed-to-be-ignored op onto the history.
- **B-rep only, gated explicitly with a clear message, not a silent no-op.** `addPolyline` is in `BREP_ONLY_OPS` (meshes have no sketch/exact topology) — `main.ts`'s `importSvgPaths` checks `sourceKind === "mesh"` BEFORE parsing anything and shows an explanatory status (`"...builds sketch polylines, which are B-rep only..."`) rather than letting the pushed ops silently vanish into `applyEditsMesh`'s existing `BREP_ONLY_OPS` skip with zero visible feedback.
- **New protocol pair, deliberately WITHOUT a `requestId`**: `importSvgRequest` (webview→host, triggered by the File ▾ menu's new "Import SVG…" item) → `importSvgResult{text}` / `importSvgError{message}` (host→webview) — unlike every other request/response pair in this codebase, there's genuinely nothing to disambiguate: `vscode.window.showOpenDialog` is modal, so only one import can plausibly be in flight. The host reads the picked `.svg` file as plain UTF-8 text (no parsing host-side — parsing happens in the webview, where the resulting ops need to be pushed onto `EditsModel` anyway, preserving "webview owns the op stack, host stays dumb"); a dismissed dialog is a quiet no-op (mirrors every other file-picker cancellation in this codebase), not an error.
- **No MCP tool** — same "genuinely a display/interactive feature, no headless equivalent" precedent as Markup/Measurement/Display-modes. An agent that wants sketch polylines already has `run_parametric_script`'s `addPolyline` op directly with literal or expression-driven points; there's no headless "pick a local SVG file" equivalent to build.
- **Verification split**: the parser itself (`svgImport.test.ts`, 22 cases) is fully unit-tested, no WASM/DOM needed — the highest-risk, most bug-prone part of this feature. The webview/host wiring (menu click → file dialog → message round trip → `editsModel.push`) is thin glue mirroring already-established, already-verified patterns almost verbatim (`loadPreprocessDialog`'s exact `showOpenDialog` shape; `editsModel.push()` calls identical in form to every other op-pushing call site in `main.ts`) — genuinely interactive/DOM-dependent, so it has the same "not exercised in a real Extension Development Host session" caveat as every other webview-only feature in this file. See "Verify a change" below.

**DXF followed the identical pattern (roadmap Tier 2 #1, closed in both phases)** — `src/dxfImport.ts` (import) and `src/dxfSilhouette.ts` (export), same pure/no-new-kernel-surface discipline:

- **Import is a group-code/value line-pair scan, not a real DXF parser — deliberate, same testability reasoning as SVG's regex `<path d>` extraction.** DXF is plain ASCII code-line/value-line pairs, so `extractPairs` walks them two lines at a time; only model-space `ENTITIES` are read (blocks/layers/INSERT/TEXT/MTEXT/DIMENSION/HATCH/paper space are skipped, stated plainly in the module's doc comment). Handled entities: `LINE`, `LWPOLYLINE` (with `42`-code bulge arcs converted to polyline sampling), `POLYLINE`/`VERTEX`/`SEQEND`, `CIRCLE`, `ARC`, and `SPLINE` (control points); everything else is silently skipped with a warnings entry. Output ops feed the EXISTING `addLine`/`addPolyline`/`addCircleProfile`/`addArc`/`addSpline` kinds via `validateEditOp`.
- **DXF is Y-up NATIVE — no Y-negation on import, the one deliberate difference from SVG.** Placement is flat in the XY plane at z=0, 1 DXF drawing unit = 1mm (this codebase's cascade unit); a poorly-scaled source is fixed afterward with the EXISTING `translate`/`rotate`/`scale` ops, no placement dialog.
- **Protocol pair mirrors SVG's exactly**: `importDxfRequest`/`importDxfResult{text}`/`importDxfError{message}`, triggered by the File ▾ menu's "Import DXF…" item (`#menu-import-dxf`), no `requestId` (modal dialog = nothing to disambiguate). Parsing happens webview-side after the host reads the picked file as text, preserving "webview owns the op stack". B-rep only — `main.ts`'s `importDxfEntities` gates on `sourceKind === "mesh"` with an explanatory status before parsing anything.
- **Export reuses the silhouette pipeline's geometry work**: `src/dxfSilhouette.ts` imports `viewBasis` from `svgSilhouette.ts` so an SVG and a DXF of the same view are geometrically consistent, and serializes the SAME segment list into minimal `ENTITIES`-only DXF — chained collinear runs become `LWPOLYLINE`s, unmatched singletons become `LINE`s (returns `{dxf, segmentCount, chainCount, lineCount}`). **Deliberate quirk: the projection keeps SVG's Y-negation** so a DXF viewed in a Y-up CAD tool matches its SVG twin viewed in a Y-down browser without a second flip. Bulge serialization is carried but currently always zero (tessellated silhouettes are pure straight segments) — a future arc-aware source can reuse it with no format change.
- **The existing `export_svg_silhouette` MCP tool gained an optional `format` param (`"svg" | "dxf"`, default `"svg"`)** rather than a second tool — keeps it inside Tier 2's "no new MCP tool" admission; the response's `content` field carries whichever document was produced and the counts describe it either way. Interactively, the File ▾ menu gained a second item beside the SVG one — **Export Silhouette DXF…** (`#menu-export-dxf` / `exportDxfRequest` / `cad-preview.exportDxf`) — sharing `handleExportSvg`'s exact view→unit→save flow with only the serializer and default extension differing; no format quick-pick was added to the existing item.
- **Verified**: `dxfImport.test.ts` + `dxfSilhouette.test.ts` unit-test both directions headless, including the strongest check — a round trip of the writer's own output back through the reader (a closed `LWPOLYLINE` recovers exactly the segments that went in), mirroring `svgSilhouette.test.ts`'s loop through `parseSvgPaths`. Manual verification also ran `format:"dxf"` against `examples/STL/cube.stl`. The interactive click-through flows carry the standing F5-only caveat.

## Parametric variables (expressions in edit-op fields)

Users define named variables (`L = 20`) in a table at the top of the Edits panel and type expressions (`L*2`) into any numeric op field; changing a variable re-resolves and rebuilds the geometry live. Non-negotiable invariants:

- **Expressions are an annotation, numeric fields are last-good caches.** An op may carry `exprs?: Record<fieldPath, exprString>` (`length`, `size[1]`, `points[2][0]`); the addressed numeric fields always hold the most recent successful evaluation. Every consumer (`validateEditOp` invariants, both edit engines, export, meshing) keeps operating on plain numbers — only `resolveEditOps` (`src/editVariables.ts`) reads `exprs`. `validateEditOp` sanitizes the annotation (keys must address a finite numeric slot of the *validated* op; values must be syntactically valid, size-capped) and carries it onto the clean op.
- **Resolution happens at exactly two sites** — `parseEditsJson` (heals stale caches in hand-edited sidecars) and the webview's resolve-on-read (`currentResolvedOps()` in `src/webview/main.ts`, re-run at every consumption point: the `editsChanged` post, panel render, mesh rebuild). The host receives already-resolved ops and never evaluates expressions at runtime; `EditsModel` is deliberately not variables-aware. Resolve-on-read (rather than eagerly patching stored ops on a variable change) is what keeps redo-buffer ops from resurfacing with stale numbers — don't "optimize" it into a patch pass.
- **The evaluator is a hand-written recursive-descent interpreter** (`src/paramExpr.ts`, pure + unit-tested) — webview CSP blocks `eval()`, never reintroduce it. Trig functions take **degrees** (every op angle field is `*Deg`).
- **Variables live in the same `<model>.edits.json`** (optional `variables` field, omitted when empty; `version` stays 1 — the parser never checked it and tolerates the missing field). A variable is `{name, expr, value}` where `value` is its own last-good cache. A variable's expression may reference only variables defined **above it** in the list — derived values (`W = L/2`) with zero cycle-detection machinery, since cycles are unrepresentable.
- **Failures freeze, never crash** (same graceful rule as unresolved operand ids): a failing variable keeps its cached `value`; an op whose expression fails keeps that field's cache (other fields still apply); an op whose *resolved* values would violate a cross-field invariant (torus `minorRadius ≥ majorRadius`) is kept wholly at its previous values — `resolveEditOps` re-validates the patched clone and reverts on failure, recording a human-readable issue that main.ts surfaces via `setStatus`.
- **Variable mutations are NOT undoable ops** — `VariablesModel` mirrors `PartsModel` (own `onChange`, silent `load()`), outside the op stack.
- **Panel plumbing:** numeric inputs are `type="text"` (`inputmode=decimal`); the field readers (`readNum`/`readVec`/`rowVec`) evaluate non-numeric text and side-collect the raw strings keyed by op field path; `EditsPanel.wrapCallbacks` (constructor) attaches the collected map to the outgoing draft — or aborts the apply on an eval error — so the ~40 per-op apply closures stay untouched. **Exprs keys must equal op field names**: main.ts copies `draft.exprs` verbatim onto the pushed op; the one mismatch is fillet/chamfer's shared `amount` field, remapped to `radius`/`distance` in `onApplyFillet`. Keep that alignment when adding ops.

## Meshing (GMSH-JS)

Users can generate a finite-element mesh (nodes + triangles/tetrahedra) of the currently displayed model with [Gmsh](https://gmsh.info) compiled to WebAssembly via `@loumalouomega/gmsh-wasm`, shown as an overlay on top of the existing view. Non-negotiable invariants:

- **GMSH runs host-only, never in the webview** — a second, independent Emscripten module from OCCT's, but the same architectural rule: `src/gmshService.ts` holds the singleton, `src/webview/*` only ever sees the resulting triangulation buffers over the postMessage protocol, never the GMSH API itself.
- **Lazy WASM init, mirroring OCCT's.** Never call the factory in `activate()`. `getGmsh(extensionPath)` initializes on the first call — i.e. the first click of **▶ Generate** or **📤 Export** (any format), never on file open — and memoizes the resolved promise as a module singleton. Subsequent generations reuse it; per-generation state resets via `gmsh.clear()` + `gmsh.model.add(...)`, never a second `gmsh.initialize()`.
- **`wasmBinary` must be passed explicitly — same Node fetch-path lesson as OCCT.** `getGmsh` reads `dist/gmsh-core.wasm` via `fs.readFileSync` and passes it as `wasmBinary` to the raw Emscripten factory; letting the factory try to resolve its own path fails the same way `initOpenCascade`'s zero-arg wrapper does (see "WASM loading" above).
- **`@loumalouomega/gmsh-wasm` must be loaded via `require()`, not a static `import` — verified against the 0.2.0 package.** Its `"exports"` map offers both an `"import"` (`dist/gmsh.mjs`) and a `"require"` (`dist/gmsh.cjs`) condition; esbuild picks between them based on the *statement kind* (`import`/`require`), not the `conditions` build option (tried and confirmed a no-op here). `dist/gmsh.mjs`'s dependency chain pulls in `dist/gmsh-core.mjs`, whose Node pthread-worker bootstrap tail (new in 0.2.0) has a genuine top-level `await import('worker_threads')` — something the extension/MCP bundles' `"cjs"` esbuild output format cannot represent, so a static `import initialize from "@loumalouomega/gmsh-wasm"` fails the build outright. `gmshService.ts` instead loads it with `createRequire(__filename)("@loumalouomega/gmsh-wasm")`, which resolves the `"require"` condition to `dist/gmsh.cjs` → `dist/gmsh-core.cjs`, whose equivalent pthread check uses a plain synchronous `require('worker_threads')` — no top-level await, builds cleanly.
- **`initialize()` must be called with explicit `print`/`printErr` overrides — verified against the 0.2.0 package, and load-bearing for the MCP server.** 0.1.x's Emscripten Node default routed both through `console.log`/ `console.error`, which `mcpServer.ts`'s top-of-file console rebinding (see "MCP server" below) could intercept. 0.2.0 changed the Node default to `fs.writeSync(1, …)`/`fs.writeSync(2, …)` — writing straight to the raw fds, bypassing `console.*` entirely — so every `gmsh.model.mesh.generate()` call started dumping `Info :`/`Warning :` lines directly onto **fd 1**, corrupting the MCP JSON-RPC stream the instant a model meshed (confirmed via `npm run mcp:smoke`, which failed with a stdout-protocol-pollution error before this fix). `getGmsh` now passes `print: (...a) => console.error(...a)` and `printErr: (...a) => console.error(...a)` explicitly to `initialize({ wasmBinary, print, printErr })`, so gmsh's log output goes through `console.error` (and, in the MCP server, is still safely on stderr) regardless of which stream this package's own default would pick in a future version.
- **Default 3D algorithm is Gmsh's own default, Delaunay (`Mesh.Algorithm3D = 1`) — this used to be Frontal (`4`) as a workaround, fixed upstream in `@loumalouomega/gmsh-wasm` 0.3.0.** The 0.2.x WASM build had a real wasm32-stack-overflow bug in Gmsh's tetgen-derived 3D boundary recovery (shared by Delaunay and HXT): Emscripten's 64 KiB default stack overflowed silently (no trap, no error) instead of trapping, corrupting adjacent linear memory — surfacing as a hang or an empty mesh specifically on geometry re-imported via `gmsh.model.occ.importShapes` (i.e. every B-rep source this feature meshes, by definition). 0.3.0 fixed the root cause upstream (`-sSTACK_SIZE=4MB`/`-sDEFAULT_PTHREAD_STACK_SIZE=2MB` in GMSH-JS's own build), not just for CAD-Preview's specific symptom. **Re-verified against the live 0.3.0 WASM on `examples/STP/block.stp`**: Delaunay completed in 88ms (1282 tets, no hang), and HXT (`Algorithm3D=10`, previously also broken by the same bug) completed in 37ms (1254 tets) — both fully fixed, not just "improved." `DEFAULT_MESH_OPTIONS.algorithm3D` was updated from `4` to `1` accordingly; **existing documents keep whatever value is already in their `<model>.mesh.json` sidecar** (this only changes the seed for *new* documents that have never saved mesh options). Frontal (`4`) and HXT (`10`) both remain fully selectable from the 3D algorithm dropdown. See `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Known limitations" for the full verification trail (both the original bug and the 0.3.0 fix).
- **The panel's primary size control is a coarser→finer slider with a bbox-derived default — and seeding it must never create sidecars.** `DEFAULT_MESH_OPTIONS.sizeMax` is the Gmsh "unbounded" `SIZE_MAX_SENTINEL` (`1e22`); once the model's extents are known, `syncMeshSizeSeed()` (`src/webview/main.ts`, called from the `geometry`/`loadUrl`/`meshingOptions` handlers — their arrival order is not deterministic) replaces a still-sentinel `sizeMax` with `diagonal/20` via **`MeshingModel.load()`, never `update()`** — `update()` fires `meshingChanged`, which would write `.mesh.json`/`.geo` for every file merely opened. A persisted user value (≠ sentinel) always wins; the panel never displays the raw `1e+22` (empty "auto" Size max field, disabled slider until seeded). All slider math (log mapping `diagonal/5`↔`diagonal/200`, Coarse/Medium/Fine presets `diagonal/{10,20,50}`, and the order-of-magnitude element-count estimate feeding the readout + the ~1M-element warning) lives in the pure, headless-tested `src/webview/meshSizeHeuristics.ts` — plain JS from the bbox (`Viewer.getModelExtents()`) only, **never a gmsh call**, so the lazy-WASM invariant holds. Slider/preset/field commits that would drop `sizeMax` below `sizeMin` patch `sizeMin: 0` in the same update, or `validateMeshOptions`' pair rule silently resets both on reload. The slider commits on `change` (release), not `input` (mid-drag) — no message spam. The panel also mirrors the Parts panel's per-part `meshSize` inputs (a "Part sizes" section routing to the same `PartsModel.setMeshSize`) and tucks the raw options form — including the STL angle field, disabled for B-rep sources via `setSourceKind` — into a collapsed-by-default "Advanced settings" section.
- **Element shape & order.** `MeshOptions.elementOrder: 1|2` → `Mesh.ElementOrder` (quadratic adds mid-side nodes; the overlay draws corner geometry only). `MeshOptions.elementShape: "simplex"|"subdivided"|"hexDominant"` → `Mesh.RecombineAll`/`Mesh.SubdivisionAlgorithm`/`Mesh.Recombine3DAll`/an `Mesh.Algorithm3D` override via the shared `gmshShapeOptions(shape, dimension)` helper (`src/meshOptions.ts`), reused by both `loadGeometryAndApplyOptions` and `generateGeoScript` so they can't drift. Recipe is **dimension-dependent and WASM-verified**: 2D quads via Blossom `RecombineAll=1`; 3D hexes via `SubdivisionAlgorithm=2` (3D `RecombineAll` throws); 3D hex-*dominant* (mixed tet/hex) via `Algorithm3D=9` (RTree) + `Recombine3DAll=1` — `algorithm3DOverride` wins over the user's own `algorithm3D` selection when set, since RTree is a requirement of the mode, not a suggestion. All option values are set every generate (the singleton persists options across `clear()`); `"hexDominant"` outside 3D degrades to plain simplex options (harmless, not rejected — the panel additionally disables the `<option>` for non-3D dimensions as a UX nicety). `"hexDominant"` always produces a THIRD element type alongside tet(4)/ hex(5): gmsh type 140 ("trihedron", a tet/hex transition connector) with NO `gmshElementTypes.ts` table entry — `gmsh.model.mesh.getElementProperties (140)` throws (`"Size of basis incompatible with element type"`), so its node geometry is unverifiable and deliberately never added. This is safe because every existing consumer of the table (`surfaceTriangles`/ `boundaryTriangles`/`surfaceEdges`) already treats an unmapped type as a graceful skip, not a throw — **re-verified live**: `generateMesh()` with `elementShape:"hexDominant"` on `examples/STP/block.stp` produced a populated overlay (446 nodes, 1289 elements, 6384 triangle indices, 3348 edge values — non-empty despite the unmapped type mixed in) and a working quality summary (no crash on the mixed tag set); VTK export (Gmsh's own native writer) produced a valid file untouched by our table at all. **Kratos MDPA export is the one path that genuinely cannot represent this mesh** (no `MdpaCellKind` for a tet/hex connector) — `collectCells` (`gmshService.ts`) gives gmsh type 140 specifically a distinct, actionable rejection message ("...has no Kratos geometry equivalent... export a different format...") rather than the generic "unsupported element type" one every other truly-unexpected type still gets; re-verified live that `exportMdpa()` throws it correctly. See `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Known limitations" for the full probing/verification trail.
- **Sidecar pair `<model>.mesh.json` + `<model>.geo`, beside parts/edits.** The FE-mesh options (`MeshOptions` — a flat bag, not an op-list) autosave (~500 ms, its own debounce timer, separate from parts/edits) to `<model>.mesh.json` via `src/meshOptionsStore.ts`; parse/serialize live in the vscode-free `src/meshOptionsSidecar.ts` so they unit-test. `validateMeshOptions` is the single tolerance gate — an individually invalid field falls back to `DEFAULT_MESH_OPTIONS` for that field alone, never rejecting the whole object.
- **The `.geo` file is ONE-WAY generated — hand-edits are never read back.** On the same debounce, `writeGeoScript()` regenerates `<model>.geo` wholesale from the current `MeshOptions` (`generateGeoScript` in `meshOptionsSidecar.ts`). GMSH-JS has no API to emit a clean, parametric `.geo` script from in-memory state — only the fully-expanded `.geo_unrolled` form via `gmsh.write()`, which is what picking "Gmsh Geometry (.geo_unrolled)" in the panel's export `<select>` and clicking **📤 Export** actually produces (`exportGeoUnrolled` in `gmshService.ts`), a *different* file from the autogenerated sidecar `.geo`. The sidecar `.geo`'s own header comment says as much; any manual edit to it is silently overwritten on the next options change.
- **Export formats are a single shared registry, not one button per format.** `src/meshExportFormats.ts`'s `MESH_EXPORT_FORMATS` (`{id, label, extension, filterLabel}[]`) is imported by both the host (picks the MEMFS write extension `gmsh.write()` dispatches on, and the save-dialog filter) and the webview (`meshingPanel.ts` populates the export `<select>` from it) — the original design had one button per format (`📤 .msh`, `📤 .geo`), which doesn't scale once more formats are added. Every format id except `"msh"` (reuses `generateMesh`'s `mshText`), `"geoUnrolled"` (its own XAO-companion handling, see below), and `"mdpaElements"`/`"mdpaGeometries"` (see next bullet) routes through `gmshService.ts`'s generic `exportMeshFormat()`: mesh, then `gmsh.write("/out.<extension>")`, then read back as text — confirmed against the live WASM build for all 10 other registered formats (`msh2`, `vtk`, `unv`, `inp`, `bdf`, `su2`, `mesh`, `stl`, `diff`, `off`). CGNS and MED are recognized by Gmsh's writer-dispatch table but throw `"...compiled without CGNS support"`/`"...must be compiled with MED support..."` in this build (need HDF5-backed libs not linked in) — excluded from the registry rather than offered as an always-failing option. See `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Export formats" section for the full probe results and which other formats were excluded as unusable/redundant.
- **Kratos MDPA is hand-written — the one export format with no `gmsh.write()` support at all.** `mdpaElements`/`mdpaGeometries` are listed *first* in `MESH_EXPORT_FORMATS`, making `mdpaElements` the default-selected export format. `src/mdpaWriter.ts` (pure, vscode/WASM-free, unit-tested in `mdpaWriter.test.ts` against hand-built fixtures — mirrors `partsSidecar.ts`/ `editOps.ts`'s pure-module convention) serializes a plain `MdpaMesh` (`{nodes, volumeCells, surfaceCells, groups}`, where each `MdpaCell` is `{kind, nodeTags}`) to ASCII text; `gmshService.ts`'s `exportMdpa()` + private `extractMdpaMesh()` pull that data off the live gmsh model after `mesh.generate()` (per-entity-tag `getElements(dim, tag)` loops, same pattern as `extractBoundaryFaces`/`appendTriangles2D`) and hand it to `writeMdpa()` — no MEMFS write/read-back round trip. Two mutually exclusive modes: `"elements"` writes one `Element*` block per volume kind + `Condition*` per surface kind (each `<id> <prop_id> <n1..nk>`, `prop_id` always `0` in a single `Begin Properties 0` block); `"geometries"` writes `Geometries` blocks (`<id> <n1..nk>`, **no** property id) which, per Kratos's single `Geometries` container, **share one id space** (all volume kinds `1..V`, then all surface kinds `V+1..V+S`). A kind's root block and its matching `SubModelPart*` sub-block are omitted when empty.
- **The full cell catalogue (linear + quadratic tet/hex/prism/pyramid/tri/quad) flows through the single `src/gmshElementTypes.ts` table** — the ONE source of truth for every gmsh type's stride, corner count, boundary-face decomposition, Kratos block names, and gmsh→Kratos node permutation. Both the overlay builders (`surfaceTriangles`/`boundaryTriangles`, now the guts of `appendTriangles2D`/ `extractBoundaryFaces`) AND `extractMdpaMesh` resolve types through it, so they can never drift (replaces the old duplicated-and-must-stay-in-lockstep risk). A genuinely unmapped type throws (graceful backstop). **Permutations were DERIVED by coordinate-matching against the live WASM** (`getElementProperties(). localNodeCoord`, not docs): linear cells + `tri6`/`quad9` are identity, `tet10` is `[0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,9,8]`, `hex20`/`hex27`/`prism15`/`pyramid13` are non-trivial. Complete order-2 prism18/pyramid14 **truncate** to Kratos `Prism3D15`/`Pyramid3D13` (their leading nodes coincide — verified). **Every currently-filled `elementName`/`conditionName` (roadmap "Confirm Kratos MDPA block names", closed) is now CONFIRMED against Kratos's own core C++ registration source** (`kratos/sources/kratos_application.cpp` in the `KratosMultiphysics/Kratos` GitHub repo, via `gh api search/code` — the unconditional `KRATOS_REGISTER_ELEMENT`/`KRATOS_REGISTER_CONDITION` calls in `KratosApplication`'s constructor/`Register()`, which every Kratos installation runs regardless of which physics application is loaded, not a guess or an application-specific naming convention): `"Element3D4N"` → `mElement3D4N` → `Tetrahedra3D4<NodeType>`, ... through `"Element3D27N"` → `Hexahedra3D27<NodeType>`, and `"SurfaceCondition3D3N"` → `Triangle3D3` through `"SurfaceCondition3D9N"` → `Quadrilateral3D9` — every one matches this file's table exactly, both the name string and the geometry class it wraps. (A REAL Kratos `.mdpa` test fixture in the same repo, `GeoMechanicsApplication/tests/.../test_thermal_heat_flux_3D20N.mdpa`, uses an application-specific name instead, `GeoTransientThermalElement3D20N` — confirming that a *specific-physics* element can be named anything, but the bare `"Element3DNN"` family this codebase emits is the genuinely-registered CORE fallback name, not a fabricated placeholder.) The `"elements"` mode pre-flight guard (an actionable "use Geometries mode" throw if any produced kind's element/condition name is `null`) remains in place for any FUTURE unmapped kind (e.g. the hex-dominant "trihedron" connector already documented above) — dormant today since every kind this codebase currently produces has a name, and every one of those names is now verified correct.
- **Node ordering / orientation**: `mdpaWriter.ts`'s `orientCell()` recomputes each cell's signed volume (divergence theorem over the kind's OUTWARD boundary faces — `signedVolume` in `gmshElementTypes.ts`) and, for a negative tet, applies the well-defined `tet4`/`tet10` flip (`[0,1,3,2]` / `[0,1,3,2,4,8,7,6,5,9]`); a negative hex/prism/pyramid (which gmsh shouldn't emit) is passed through with an `onWarning` callback, no unsafe reshuffle. **SubModelParts** map 1:1 to `Part[]` (flat, B-rep only). `extractMdpaMesh()`'s `groupPartsAcrossDims()` reuses `PartGroupMaps` to bucket cells per part + resolve `lines`/`points` to extra node ids (edges/points contribute only to `SubModelPartNodes`). `SubModelPartNodes` is the **union** of explicit point/curve selections and every node (incl. mid-side) of the grouped cells. Deterministic: node ids by source tag, cell ids by canonical node-tuple within a kind, kinds in fixed `VOLUME_KIND_ORDER`/ `SURFACE_KIND_ORDER`. Verified end-to-end on `angle1.stp` across all four `{simplex, subdivided} × {order 1, 2}` combos (watertight overlays, no orient warnings, all block names present). See `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Kratos MDPA" and "Element shapes & order" sections.
- **Two input paths converge on the same options step.** B-rep documents (`kind: "brep"`) re-export the live OCCT shape to STEP bytes via the existing `exportBRep()` (so unsaved edits are reflected) and load them with `gmsh.model.occ.importShapes` + `synchronize`. Mesh documents (`kind: "stl"`) have no B-rep to re-export, so the *webview* serializes the currently displayed `THREE.Object3D` to STL (`currentStlIfMeshSource()` in `main.ts`, reusing the same `exportModel(..., "stl")` Export already uses) and sends it up as a base64 `stl` field on `meshingGenerate`/`meshingExport`; the host then reclassifies the raw triangle soup into surfaces (`classifySurfaces` → `createGeometry` → `addSurfaceLoop`/`addVolume` → `synchronize`) since STL has no volume topology.
- **The mesh overlay is a scene sibling of the model, never mutating the original geometry.** `geometryBuilder.buildFEMesh(positionsB64, indicesB64)` builds a freestanding `THREE.Group` (a shaded mesh + wireframe, both tagged `userData.entityType = "mesh"` — deliberately not `"surface"`/`"line"`, so existing picking/parts-colouring code never touches it). `Viewer.setMeshOverlay()` adds/replaces it as a sibling of `model` in the scene (never a child), disposing the previous overlay's geometries/materials on swap. **It is auto-cleared when a new model loads** — `setModel()` calls `setMeshOverlay(null)` as its very first line, since a previously-generated overlay was computed from the *old* geometry and must not linger looking valid. The toolbar's **🔬 FE Mesh** toggle show/hides the *existing* overlay in place (`Viewer.setMeshOverlayVisible()`, `Object3D.visible`, no dispose) rather than clearing it — toggling off then back on must redisplay the same generated mesh instantly, with no need to re-run Generate. Only three things actually dispose the overlay: a new model loading (above), the panel's **Clear** button, and a fresh **Generate** replacing it with a new one; all three also reset the toggle's `.active` state to stay truthful about what's currently displayed (same rule `meshingResult`/`meshingError` already followed for Generate — the toggle must never claim "on" for content that isn't actually shown). The FE Mesh panel itself is always present in the sidebar regardless of toggle state.
- **The overlay's shaded mesh is unlit (`MeshBasicMaterial`, one per `elementGroups` entry — see below), not `MeshStandardMaterial` like every other face material in this codebase.** A tet-mesh boundary is thousands of small, irregularly-oriented triangles — unlike a B-rep face's smooth NURBS-tessellated triangulation — so a lit material shades each one differently under the scene's directional/hemisphere lights; triangles facing away from the light go dark/near-black, which reads as scattered holes even though the geometry is a complete, watertight surface (verified: re-running the STEP-export→GMSH-tetrahedralize→boundary-extraction pipeline standalone on `examples/STP/angle1.stp` and rendering the raw output triangles headlessly showed a complete boundary from every angle — the "holes" were a shading artifact, not a gap in the GMSH-generated geometry). A flat unlit color removes that per-facet brightness variation entirely. `buildFEMesh`'s `THREE.LineSegments` wireframe is built from the host-supplied **true element-edge** buffer (`MeshResult.edges` → `meshingResult.edges`), NOT a `THREE.WireframeGeometry` of the triangulated fill. This matters for recombined meshes: a hex boundary is quad faces, each split into 2 triangles for the shaded fill — `WireframeGeometry` would draw the diagonal across every quad, making a hex mesh look identical to a tet mesh. `gmshElementTypes.ts`'s `boundaryEdges`/`surfaceEdges` emit only the polygon perimeters (quad → its 4 edges, tri → 3), deduplicated across shared faces, so hexes render as quads and tets as triangles (verified on `angle1.stp`: subdivided gives edges = quad perimeters with no diagonals, ratio 1.0 edges-per-fill-triangle vs 1.5 for tets). The wireframe shares the fill's `positions` (own line index buffer) and is perfectly coincident with the triangles, so the shaded material still needs `polygonOffset: true` (+ `polygonOffsetFactor`/`polygonOffsetUnits: 1`) or the GPU depth test can't reliably resolve filled-triangle-vs-coincident-line per pixel (z-fighting speckle).
- **`setMeshOverlay()` also hides the model's shaded faces while an overlay is shown** (`entityType === "surface"` meshes get `.visible = false`; edges/points stay visible as a feature-line reference), restoring them when the overlay is cleared. Two opaque solids occupying the same space are unreadable stacked on each other — display-only, never touches geometry, same "never mutate the original" invariant as the overlay itself.
- **Generate has no progress reporting from GMSH itself** — `gmsh.model.mesh. generate()` is one opaque blocking WASM call with no progress hook (`GmshLogger` only exposes post-hoc wall/CPU time). `MeshingPanel.setBusy(true/false)` (called from `onGenerate` before posting, and from the `meshingResult`/ `meshingError` handlers) is therefore an indeterminate signal only: it disables `#meshing-generate` and shows a CSS keyframe-sweep progress bar (`#meshing-progress`) plus a `"Generating…"` status line. **📤 Export** (any format) isn't wired to it — its save-dialog completion already surfaces through the generic toolbar status bar. **The MCP server's `generate_mesh`/ `export_mesh` tools have the identical limitation** — they emit `notifications/progress` at start(0) and done(1) only (see the "Agent feedback tools" section above for the mechanism), never a genuine percentage, for exactly this reason.
- **Parts are preserved in generated meshes as Gmsh physical groups — B-rep sources only**, matching the existing `BREP_ONLY_OPS` scope (Gmsh's STL reclassification pipeline produces brand-new surface/volume tags with zero correlation to a mesh document's original ids, so there is no reliable per-part correlation for STL/OBJ/PLY/glTF sources). `src/gmshPartsMap.ts`'s `applyPartsToGmshModel` resolves each part's `face-N`/`edge-N`/`solid-N`/ `point-N` ids to Gmsh `(dim,tag)` entities **geometrically** — bounding-box centre matching between CAD-Preview's own OCCT (`bboxCenter`, already used by `explodeSolids`) and Gmsh's own `getBoundingBox`, both computed from the *same* STEP bytes (their two independent, separately-versioned OCCT builds give no ordering guarantee, so `importShapes`'s `outDimTags` order is deliberately **not** relied on) — within a tolerance of `1e-3 × model bbox diagonal`, accepted only if unambiguous. An unresolved/ambiguous entity is silently skipped, same graceful-degradation rule as every other unresolved-id path in this codebase. Resolved entities become one `gmsh.model.addPhysicalGroup(dim, tags, -1, part.name)` per part per dimension, which lands in `.msh` output's `$PhysicalNames` section automatically once created. **`gmsh.write()`'s `Mesh.SaveAll` option must be forced to `1`** (`loadGeometryAndApplyOptions`, unconditionally, parts or no parts) — Gmsh's own default (`0`) writes only elements belonging to *some* physical group once *any* physical group exists, so the instant one part resolves one entity, every other entity's elements would otherwise vanish from `.msh`/`.geo_unrolled` output (physical groups are meant to tag a subset of a full mesh here, never filter it — confirmed as a real, silent regression before this override was added: one part on 1 of 15 surfaces produced a `.msh` containing only that surface's 88 triangles). The **live** overlay is unaffected by `Mesh.SaveAll` either way — it's built from `getNodes()`/ `getElements()` calls directly against Gmsh's in-memory model, not from re-reading a written file. **Confirmed against the live WASM** (`examples/STP/angle1.stp`): a no-parts baseline generate produced 499 nodes; the same geometry with one volume-scoped part's `meshSize: 0.5` produced 235,088 nodes (clear, correctly-directed local refinement, not a silent no-op), and the resulting `.msh`'s `$PhysicalNames` section listed both a volume- and a surface-scoped part by name at the right dimension. **A surface-scoped part also gets its own overlay colour range in a 3D (`dimension === 3`) generate**, not just its own `Physical Surface` in `.msh` output — a tet-boundary triangle carries only its owning *volume*'s tag with no link back to the B-rep surface it came from, so `buildIndices3D` (`src/gmshService.ts`) resolves the correlation via the intermediate 2D surface mesh Gmsh generates while building the volume mesh (still live in the model after a 3D generate, with the **same node tags** as the volume boundary it seeded): each surface-scoped part's `getElements(2, tag)` faces are keyed by their sorted corner tags (`gmshElementTypes.ts`'s `faceRingKey`, the same key `boundaryFaceRings` already uses to dedup interior tet/hex faces), and every kept tet-boundary face is looked up by that key and routed to the matching surface part instead of its owning volume's — so a surface-scoped part wins over its own volume's volume-scoped part too, being the more specific assignment. `buildIndices` is exported *only* so `gmshService.test.ts` can exercise this grouping/ precedence logic against a fake `GmshApi` object with no WASM needed; every other function in that file still needs the real WASM and is exercised only via `npm run mcp:smoke`. **2D** generates were always correct here (`buildIndices2D` groups directly by surface) and are unaffected by this. See `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Parts → physical groups" section for the full write-up. **`.geo_unrolled` output does NOT carry `Physical Volume(...)`/`Physical Surface(...)` as textual statements for B-rep sources at all** — see the next bullet for why and how it's made to round-trip them anyway. See `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Parts → physical groups" section for the full write-up.
- **`gmsh.write("*.geo_unrolled")` cannot textually inline OCC-imported B-rep geometry** — confirmed against the live WASM: for every B-rep source, it writes a single-line stub (`Merge "/out.geo_unrolled.xao";`) referencing a companion **XAO** file (Gmsh's own OCC-preserving exchange format, which does preserve shapes + physical groups + mesh-size fields) it wrote alongside in MEMFS — a path this code originally never read back, so the exported `.geo_unrolled` was a dangling reference to a file that didn't exist on the user's disk. `gmshService.ts`'s `exportGeoUnrolled` now returns `{ text, xao }` (`xao` is `null` for the STL/GEO-kernel path, which unrolls fully inline with no companion needed); `provider.ts`'s `meshingExport` "geo" branch writes the XAO bytes as a sibling of the chosen save path and rewrites the stub's `Merge` reference to that sibling's relative filename before writing the `.geo_unrolled` text, so the pair is self-contained. Verified end-to-end: reopening the rewritten pair in a fresh Gmsh model restored the same volume/surface counts, the same physical groups, and — after re-running `mesh.generate()` — the same node count as the original sized-part generate.
