- **A part's optional `meshSize` (a single target size, not min/max) becomes a Gmsh `Constant` field** scoped to that part's resolved entities (`VIn` + `PointsList`/`CurvesList`/`SurfacesList`/`VolumesList`), and all parts' Constant fields combine via a `Min` field set as the background mesh — so the smallest requested size wins on overlap, and unsized regions keep the global `sizeMin`/`sizeMax` clamps. **STL/mesh sources can't get per-entity sizing** (no correlation, same as physical groups above) — instead `meshOptions.ts`'s `applyStlPartSizeOverride` applies a one-off `sizeMin`/`sizeMax` override for that one generate/export call **only** when *exactly one* part in the document has `meshSize` set (never persisted to `<model>.mesh.json`; 0 or 2+ sized parts is ambiguous and silently no-ops).
- **The mesh overlay is multi-material, one `MeshBasicMaterial` per `meshingResult.elementGroups` entry** (`{name,color,indexStart,indexCount}`, via `THREE.BufferGeometry.addGroup`) — `gmshService.ts`'s `buildIndices` scopes `getElements(dim, tag)` to one entity at a time (instead of one global call) to bucket triangles into contiguous per-part ranges, with an always-present trailing ungrouped range (`name`/`color` both `null`, rendered in the original default blue) for anything not claimed by a part. For `dimension === 3` this buckets **per volume** — `getElements(3, tag)` limits tets to one volume before the shared-tet-face dedup runs — which stays correct even for two touching part-volumes, since Gmsh tags each tet by its single owning volume regardless of geometric adjacency.
- **Unit conversion for the FE Mesh panel's Gmsh-format export (roadmap item, closed).** `meshingExport` (the `.msh`/Kratos MDPA/VTK/etc. flow — a *different* export path from the model Export command's own unit conversion, see the "Export" section above) gained an optional `unit`, mirroring `exportBRep`'s `scaleFactor` mechanism rather than duplicating it: `provider.ts`'s `resolveMeshInput()` re-exports B-rep sources via the ALREADY-verified `exportBRep(..., unitScaleFactor(unit))` (the exact same geometric scale the model Export command uses), so Gmsh imports already-scaled STEP bytes and never needs to know unit conversion happened. STL/mesh-format sources have no OCCT re-export to hook, so this is the host-side STL vertex scaler CLAUDE.md previously flagged as needed: `stlParser.ts`'s new `scaleStlBytes(bytes, factor)` reuses the existing `parseStl()` triangle-soup parser, scales every vertex, and re-serializes as ASCII STL — facet normals are always recomputed from the (post-scale) winding via cross product, never trusted from the input file, matching `parseStl`'s own established convention. **`MeshOptions.sizeMin`/ `sizeMax` (and any per-part `meshSize`) are rescaled by the SAME factor** (`meshOptions.ts`'s new `scaleMeshOptionsForUnit`/ `scalePartsMeshSizeForUnit`, applied in `provider.ts`'s `resolveMeshPartsAndOptions` AFTER the pre-existing `applyStlPartSizeOverride` step, so a single sized STL part's raw-mm override is itself correctly carried into the target unit's space too) — this is exactly the proportional-rescaling requirement the roadmap item identified: an absolute mm-valued size target applied to now-inch-scaled geometry would otherwise produce a wildly different (usually far coarser) mesh density than intended. The `SIZE_MAX_SENTINEL` (`1e22`, "no explicit size yet") is left untouched by the scale — it's a flag, not a real mm value. **Scoped to Export only — Generate (interactive or `generate_mesh`) always stays native mm**, since its overlay is display-only with no exported file whose numbers need to mean anything to an external tool; `resolveMeshInput`'s `unit` param defaults to `"mm"` and only the FE Mesh panel's **Export** button (`onExport`, via a new `#meshing-export-unit` `<select>` beside the format picker, `DISPLAY_UNITS`-populated, defaulting to `"mm"`) and the MCP `export_mesh` tool's optional `unit` param (mirroring `export_brep`'s, but simpler — every Gmsh output format is scale-agnostic, unlike STEP/IGES' declared-header-unit gotcha, so there's no format-dependent fallback) ever pass a non-`"mm"` value. **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM, not just unit-tested**: exported the same `bull.stp` FE mesh at native mm and at `unit: "in"`, parsed both `.msh` files' `$Nodes` blocks, and confirmed the inch export's max node-coordinate magnitude is the mm export's × exactly `1/25.4` (mm max 186.309 → in max 7.335, ratio 0.03937) — and repeated the same check for an STL-sourced (`cube.stl`) export to confirm the `scaleStlBytes` path independently produces the identical ratio.
- Bundling gmsh-wasm is *why this project is GPL-2.0-or-later, not MIT* — see the "License" section above and the README's "Licensing" section for the full rationale. Full technical write-up (input paths, GMSH API call sequences, protocol messages, licensing, and the upstream GMSH-JS gaps found while building this): `doc/gmsh-integration.md`.

## fTetWild robust volume meshing (roadmap "Robust volumetric meshing from a skin mesh", closed — via a route the item's own text never considered)

Gmsh's own STL→volume path (`classifySurfaces`/`createGeometry`/`addSurfaceLoop`/`addVolume`, see the Meshing section above) needs a watertight, manifold, well-oriented boundary. **On the meshes people actually have — scans, sculpts, print-ready STLs with holes or self-intersections — it does not throw; it silently produces a mesh with zero elements.** Verified live: `examples/STL/holed-cube.stl` (a real, committed fixture — `cube.stl` with a single facet removed) meshed under `engine: "gmsh"` reports `elementCount: 0` with no exception at any layer. The roadmap item's own diagnosis was right (*"the gap is robustness, not capability"*) but its own proposed routes — an unmerged meshio++ SDF branch, or a WASM build of MMG that doesn't exist and would force a GPLv3 license upgrade (LGPL-3.0-family) — were both never taken. [fTetWild](https://github.com/wildmeshing/fTetWild) (`float-tetwild-wasm`, MPL-2.0, built and published by this project's own maintainer) closes it instead: a fourth host-side WASM kernel, alongside OCCT/Gmsh/meshio++, that tetrahedralizes dirty triangle soups directly with no `classifySurfaces` step at all.

- **License, checked before anything was bundled, per this file's own rule.** `float-tetwild-wasm` is **MPL-2.0** with the "Incompatible With Secondary Licenses" notice **not** invoked (confirmed: it appears only as the Exhibit B boilerplate at the end of `LICENSE.MPL2`; no source file attaches it) — MPL-2.0 §3.3 permits GPL relicensing, so this does **not** force the GPLv3 one-way door the OpenSCAD/MMG roadmap items warn about for themselves. The shipped `.wasm`'s actual dependency set, read directly from the build's own `_deps/` (not assumed from the README): **geogram (BSD-3), libigl (MPL-2.0), Eigen (MPL-2.0), spdlog (MIT), fmt (MIT), Shewchuk predicates (public domain)** — GMP is force-disabled for the Emscripten target (`CMakeLists.txt`'s own `FLOAT_TETWILD_USE_GMP OFF ... FORCE`), `fast-envelope` sits behind a flag that's `OFF` by default and absent from the actual build, and **CGAL appears nowhere** in the dependency tree. First MPL-2.0 dependency in this codebase; see README's "Licensing" section for the full attribution.
- **`src/ftetwildService.ts` mirrors `meshioService.ts`'s ESM-only-WASM template exactly** (lazy singleton, non-memoized rejection, `resetFtetwild`/`resetFtetwildIfAbort`/`wrapFtetwildFault` — the last's message ends in the identical `"...the kernel has been reset; try the operation again."` phrasing every other kernel's fault wrapper uses, so `scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs`'s `callWithCleanRetry` recognizes it too). Two constraints verified against the live package, not assumed from its source:
  - **Must load with `{ threads: false }`, never the default — still true as of v0.2.0.** The package's own `threadsSupported()` (`node_modules/float-tetwild-wasm/index.js`) returns `true` unconditionally under Node (unlike its browser branch, it never checks `crossOriginIsolated`) — byte-identical between v0.1.0 and v0.2.0 — so the default always picks the threaded build, which needs a `libtbb.so.12.16` side-module and spawns a 4-worker pthread pool that keeps Node's event loop alive. The serial build has none of this (verified: zero `worker_threads`/`SharedArrayBuffer` references in its glue).
  - **`moduleArgs.print`/`printErr` overrides were required under v0.1.0, and are no longer needed as of v0.2.0 — a real upstream fix, re-verified rather than assumed.** v0.1.0's `Parameters::init()` (C++) wrote 9 unconditional `std::cout` lines per call (`bbox_diag_length = ...`, `eps = ...`, ...), **not gated by `is_quiet`**, plus more from its simplification pass, and its threaded build's Node glue routed `std::cout`/`std::cerr` through a raw `fs.writeSync(1/2, ...)` instead of `console.log`/`console.error` — bypassing `mcpServer.ts`'s stdout rebinding and corrupting the MCP JSON-RPC stream the instant a tetrahedralization ran (confirmed empirically at the time: 1181 bytes of unwanted text per call with no override). v0.2.0 gates every one of those prints behind `if (!is_quiet)` at the source (`Bindings.cpp` forces `is_quiet = true`) and defaults BOTH builds' Node stdio through `console.log`/`console.error`. `getFtetwild()` was updated to drop the `moduleArgs` override entirely — confirmed clean via `npm run mcp:smoke`'s existing "stdout stays pure JSON-RPC" discipline, which would fail loudly on any stray byte.
  - **`Module.wasmBinary` is silently ignored** (no `if(Module["wasmBinary"])` assignment exists in either build's glue) — the `.wasm` is located via `import.meta.url` and must exist as a real file on disk, same packaging requirement as `@meshioplusplus/wasm`. `esbuild.mjs`'s three Node configs list `"float-tetwild-wasm"` as `external`; `.vscodeignore` carves out the whole package (both builds, `libtbb.so*` included — a deliberate choice not to prune the never-loaded threaded files, since the packaging fragility wasn't judged worth the ~3.4MB saving).
- **`tetrahedralize(mesh, params)` calls the package's `tetrahedralizeTyped()` entry point (v0.2.0+), not the plain-array `tetrahedralize()`.** v0.1.0 only exposed `tetrahedralize(vertices, faces, params)` returning plain JS `number[]` — the package's own glue marshalled element-by-element via `push_back`/`.get(i)` loops, an O(n) boxed-number crossing in both directions with no zero-copy path. v0.2.0 adds `tetrahedralizeTyped`, a zero-copy heap-view path (`Module.HEAPF64.set(...)` etc.) accepting `Float32Array`/`Uint32Array` directly and returning `Float64Array`/`Uint32Array` — which this codebase's actual input types (`mesh.positions: Float32Array`, `mesh.indices: Uint32Array`) need anyway, since the shipped `index.d.ts` types the plain `tetrahedralize()` params as `number[] | Float64Array`/`number[] | Int32Array`, neither of which our arguments satisfy. Per the package's own README, ~49× faster input marshaling and ~9× faster output unmarshaling on an ~82k-triangle mesh. `tetrahedralize()`'s own wrapper still throws on `status !== 0` or an empty result — a normal rejection (malformed/degenerate input), deliberately **not** routed through `wrapFtetwildFault`'s abort-recovery path, since it isn't a WASM abort.
- **A real, live-caught winding-convention bug in v0.1.0, found during this codebase's own verification — later fixed upstream in v0.2.0, and this codebase's compensation removed accordingly.** v0.1.0's `tets` node order was the OPPOSITE of the right-handed `(b-a)×(c-a)·(d-a) > 0` convention MSH `tet4`/Gmsh's `getElementQualities("minSICN")` expect. Verified directly at the time: tetrahedralizing `examples/STL/cube.stl` and computing every returned tet's signed volume against fTetWild's raw `[a,b,c,d]` order gave **negative volume for all 3393 of 3393** tets in one run; swapping to `[a,b,d,c]` gave **positive volume for all 3626 of 3626** in a second run — left unswapped, `getElementQualities` returned severe, uniformly negative `minSICN` (measured mean ≈ −0.87) even though the geometry itself was completely correct. `ftetwildService.ts`'s `tetsToMsh41(vertices, tets)` (pure, unit-tested, no WASM) applied this swap when writing each element's node reference. **Fixed upstream in v0.2.0**: `src/MeshIO.cpp`'s `extract_volume_mesh` now ends with `T.col(2).swap(T.col(3))` — exactly this codebase's own hand-rolled reorder, applied at the source instead — and the published package ships a `assertPositiveWinding()` smoke assertion in its own CI, gating publication on it. This codebase's `tetsToMsh41` swap was removed on upgrading to v0.2.0 (leaving it in place would have silently re-inverted every element back to the v0.1.0 bug); re-verified via `npm run mcp:smoke`'s `quality.min > -1e-6` assertion, which returned a healthy `min: 0.435` post-upgrade (see the v0.2.0 upgrade bullet below).
- **The hand-off design: write MSH 4.1 text, `gmsh.merge()` it into the SAME gmsh model `generateMesh` would otherwise have used directly — verified live before writing any integration code.** A throwaway probe wrote a hand-built single-tet MSH 4.1 file, `gmsh.merge()`d it, and confirmed: `getEntities(3)` returns a genuine discrete-volume 3D entity (`{"dimTags":[3,1]}`), `getElements(3, undefined)`/`getNodes()` resolve the tet correctly, `getElementQualities(..., "minSICN")` computes a real, correct value on a **merged** (not **generated**) mesh, and `gmsh.write()` round-trips the whole thing back to equivalent MSH 4.1 text. **This is what makes the fTetWild integration cheap**: once the tets are merged in, every existing read-back path — `buildIndices`'s `extractBoundaryFaces`→`boundaryTriangles` (the boundary IS the display triangulation for a 3D generate, unchanged), `buildEdges`, `computeQualityAndWorstElements`, `gmsh.write()` for `mshText`, `extractMdpaMesh`, and the whole `via: "meshio"` export bridge — works completely unchanged. Nothing downstream was reimplemented.
- **`gmshService.ts`'s `populateMeshedModel(extensionPath, gmsh, input, options, parts)`** is the one new dispatch point every meshing/export entry point (`generateMesh`, `exportMeshFormat`, `exportMdpa`) now calls instead of the old `loadGeometryAndApplyOptions` + `runMeshGenerate` pair directly:
  - `"gmsh"` (the resolved default) is byte-for-byte the original sequence.
  - `"ftetwild"` parses the STL bytes via `meshHeal.ts`'s `parseToWeldedMesh` (promoted from module-private — exported specifically because it's the exact `{positions, indices}` shape `tetrahedralize` needs, and it's already the shared front-end `check_mesh_health`/`promote_mesh_to_brep` use for all four dirty-mesh formats), tetrahedralizes, writes MSH via `tetsToMsh41`, and `gmsh.merge()`s it at a short MEMFS path (`/i.msh`, defensively — this build's OCCT path-length cliff is documented elsewhere in this file; unconfirmed but untested for gmsh-wasm).
  - **`effectiveEngine(input, options)` downgrades `"ftetwild"` to `"gmsh"` with an explanatory `warnings` entry — never silently, never by erroring — for a B-rep source** (already exact geometry; fTetWild's whole value is dirty triangles) **or a non-3D `dimension`** (fTetWild is a volume mesher only). `MeshResult` gained `engineUsed`/`warnings` fields so both the interactive panel and `generate_mesh`'s MCP response can see which engine actually ran and why, if it differed from what was requested.
  - `exportGeoUnrolled` deliberately does **not** go through `populateMeshedModel` — it throws a clear error under a would-be `"ftetwild"` request instead, since `.geo_unrolled` represents Gmsh's own geometry-import step, which fTetWild never runs; there is nothing to unroll.
- **Option mapping reuses the existing size UI with zero new sizing controls.** fTetWild's `idealEdgeLengthRel` (target edge length, as a fraction of the input's own bbox diagonal) is derived from `MeshOptions.sizeMax` (an absolute size) at call time — `idealEdgeLengthRel = min(1, sizeMax / bboxDiagonal(welded))`, with fTetWild's own default (`0.05`) used when `sizeMax` is still `SIZE_MAX_SENTINEL`. This is not a coincidence worth re-deriving: `syncMeshSizeSeed()`'s own bbox-derived seed (`diagonal/20`) already equals `0.05` exactly, so the existing slider and Coarse/Medium/Fine presets keep working unchanged under either engine. `MeshOptions` gained two fields: `engine: "gmsh" | "ftetwild"` (default `"gmsh"`) and `ftetwildEpsRel: number` (fTetWild's envelope size, same bbox-diagonal-fraction convention, default `1e-3`) — both added to `validateMeshOptions`'s **terminal return object literal** (that function builds its result field-by-field rather than spreading, so a field silently vanishes if only the interface is updated — the same trap this file's own review process has caught before). `sizeMin`/`algorithm2D`/`algorithm3D`/`elementOrder`/`elementShape`/`stlAngle` are all meaningless under `"ftetwild"` and greyed (not hidden) in the FE Mesh panel, matching `hexDominant`'s existing `option.disabled` idiom — a UX nicety only, since `effectiveEngine`/`validateMeshOptions` already make every combination safe regardless of what the panel currently disables. `meshOptionsSidecar.ts`'s `generateGeoScript` emits an explanatory comment instead of the trailing `Mesh <dimension>;` directive under `"ftetwild"` — a `.geo` script is inherently Gmsh-only, and running it for real would silently mesh via Gmsh's own path instead of reproducing what actually happened.
- **`repair_mesh` (new MCP tool, plus the Mesh Health panel's Repair (robust)… button) is Phase 3 — the roadmap item's own "repair, then reuse the existing mesher" shape, closed without either of its originally-scoped blockers.** `gmshService.ts`'s `repairMesh(extensionPath, bytes, format, external?)` is a thin, deliberate reuse: parse to welded mesh → `weldedMeshToStlBytes` → call `generateMesh` itself with `engine: "ftetwild", dimension: 3` → the result's own `positions`/`indices` (the tet mesh's boundary, already computed by the exact same `buildIndices` path every other 3D generate uses) → re-serialize as STL via `weldedMeshToStlBytes` again. **A tet mesh's boundary is watertight and manifold by construction**, regardless of how broken the input was — no new boundary-extraction or winding logic was needed; the function is almost entirely composition of already-verified pieces. No triangle-count ceiling is imposed (unlike `meshHeal.ts`'s `MAX_HEALABLE_TRIANGLES`, a property of the *different*, per-triangle OCCT sewing pipeline `check_mesh_health`/`promote_mesh_to_brep` use) — the kernel-worker's existing 5-minute watchdog is the backstop for now. Wired through the standard 4-touch-point kernel-worker `Pipeline` pattern (`mcpTools.ts`'s `Pipeline` interface + `repairMeshTool`, `kernelWorker.ts`'s dispatch table, `kernelClient.ts`'s `callKernel` entry, `mcpTools.test.ts`'s `fakePipeline`). Interactively, `MeshHealthPanel`'s new **Repair (robust)…** button is the mirror-image gate of the existing **Promote to B-rep…** button — enabled only when at least one component did **NOT** close (`requiredTolerance === null`), since a mesh that already closes has nothing to repair — and `provider.ts`'s `handleRepairMesh` mirrors `handlePromoteToBrep`'s exact structure (a new parameter-free `repairMeshButtonClicked` message, the shared `promptSaveAndWrite` helper) but simpler: always STL, no format/unit quick-picks. Deliberately does **not** auto-chain into Check Healability/Promote on the result — the user reviews the repair themselves first.
- **`resolveMeshInputHeadless` (`mcpTools.ts`) also gained OBJ/PLY/glTF support** (via the same `parseToWeldedMesh` + a new `weldedMeshToStlBytes` — `meshComponents.ts`'s natural inverse of `weldTriangleSoup`, facet normals always recomputed from post-weld winding, never trusted from the source) — closing a real, previously-documented headless gap: those three formats used to be meshable only interactively (the extension serializes the webview's `THREE.Object3D` to STL). `describeCapabilities()`'s `headlessLimitations` entry for `.obj/.ply/.gltf/.glb` was rewritten accordingly (it used to say "not meshable ... headless", which is now false).
- **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM, not just unit-tested** (`npm run mcp:smoke`, all 32 tools, 317 checks): `generate_mesh(engine:"gmsh")` on `holed-cube.stl` confirms the motivating defect (`elementCount: 0`); the same file under `engine:"ftetwild"` succeeds (`engineUsed:"ftetwild"`, positive `quality.min`, confirming the winding fix holds in the real pipeline, not just the isolated probe); a clean `cube.stl` succeeds under both engines; a genuinely non-manifold fixture doesn't crash fTetWild; `engine:"ftetwild"` on a B-rep source correctly falls back to `"gmsh"` with the expected warning; `export_mesh` under `"ftetwild"` succeeds for both a `via:"gmsh"` format (`.msh`) and a `via:"meshio"` one (`.med`), proving the merge-into-gmsh reuse holds for the export bridge too, not just Generate; `.geo_unrolled` under `"ftetwild"` throws the clear, specific error. **Phase 3's full loop, also live-verified**: `check_mesh_health(holed-cube.stl)` reports 3 free edges/`requiredTolerance: null` → `repair_mesh` writes a real repaired STL (2184 nodes / 9610 elements in one run) → `check_mesh_health` on THAT reports **0 free edges**, closes at `1e-6`, healed volume ≈1000.37 (the analytic cube is 1000 — fTetWild's own reconstruction introduces a small, expected discretization error) → `promote_mesh_to_brep` on the repaired file **succeeds** (`skippedComponents: []`), where it previously had nothing closeable to promote at all. `npm run perf` shows no regression on the unchanged Gmsh default path (still gated at the harness's 3× threshold).
- **Upgraded `float-tetwild-wasm` 0.1.0 → 0.2.0** — a real evidence-gathering pass, not a routine bump, because the winding fix documented above meant the version bump and the removal of this codebase's own compensating swap had to land in the SAME change or every element would silently re-invert (the v0.1.0 bug returning under a different guise). Confirmed via two independent explorations before touching any code: the published npm tarball (not just the git checkout, which can be ahead or behind) was diffed field-by-field against v0.1.0, and `publish-npm`'s CI job (`needs: build-wasm`, which runs the new `assertPositiveWinding()` smoke check) completing successfully seconds before the npm publish timestamp is what closes the "is the fix actually in the binary I'm about to depend on" question, not just "is it in the source". Six concrete changes landed together: removed `tetsToMsh41`'s winding-compensation swap (+ flipped its two pinned-output unit tests); switched `tetrahedralize()` to the new zero-copy `tetrahedralizeTyped` entry point; dropped the now-unnecessary `moduleArgs.print`/`printErr` overrides (kept `{threads:false}` — the Node threads-default bug is confirmed UNCHANGED in v0.2.0, byte-identical `threadsSupported()`); deleted `src/types/float-tetwild-wasm.d.ts` (a local `declare module` SHADOWS a package-shipped `.d.ts` rather than merging with it, so editing it in place would have silently kept compiling against the stale v0.1.0 shape — it had to be deleted, not updated, now that v0.2.0 ships real `index.d.ts` types); extended the local `FtetwildParams` mirror with the four newly-exposed fields (`disableFiltering`/`coarsen`/`manifoldSurface`/`numThreads`) with no caller wiring them yet — recorded as available, not spent speculatively. `.vscodeignore`'s nine-file carve-out needed no change (confirmed by diffing `ls node_modules/float-tetwild-wasm/dist/` against it — all nine filenames unchanged, only sizes grew ~65KB/wasm) and neither did the `esbuild.mjs` `external` comment (its ESM-only/`Module.wasmBinary` rationale was re-verified unchanged; its `{threads:false}` aside never made a stdio-specific claim to begin with). Verified end-to-end post-upgrade: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (confirming `tetrahedralizeTyped`'s stricter shipped types accept this codebase's actual `Float32Array`/`Uint32Array` arguments with zero casts), `npx vitest run` 1233/1233, `npm run build` clean, `npm run mcp:smoke` 317/317 checks including the decisive `quality.min: 0.435` winding canary (not the ≈−0.87 an un-fixed or double-swapped mesh would show) and the full repair→re-check→promote chain, `npm run perf` shows no regression on the untouched Gmsh default path, and a real `vsce package` pass confirmed all nine carve-out files ship at their new sizes.

- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every other webview-touching feature in this codebase**: the FE Mesh panel's Engine `<select>` and the Mesh Health panel's Repair (robust)… button both compile clean and are wired following established patterns exactly, but neither has been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session. See "Verify a change" below for the manual F5 steps this needs before being trusted in production.

## Toolbar/panel icons — generated, theme-adaptive SVG

The toolbar and a few panels (tree-close, parts delete/remove, the meshing large-mesh warning) used plain-color emoji (📤🔍🕸️🌳🔬🖱️📍🧊◼️📏▶, plus ⚠/✕) as button icons. These are now monochrome, `currentColor`-based inline SVG that track VS Code's light/dark theme automatically, replacing the emoji. The set grew from 17 to **41** with the toolbar-dropdown regroup (see the next section): every remaining emoji (`▦ 📐 📷 ✎`) and unicode placeholder (`⊙ ＋ ↶ ↷`) is now a generated icon too, along with the Measure/Markup tool pickers and the five Display-mode buttons.

- **`src/toolbarIcons.ts` is GENERATED — never hand-edit it.** It's produced wholesale by `icons/build-toolbar-icons.mjs` from `icons/svg-ui/*.svg` (themselves built from `icons/tikz-ui/*.tex` via `pdflatex` + `pdftocairo -svg`). To change an icon: edit its `.tex` source, then `cd icons && make ts`. See `icons/README.md` for the full pipeline.
- **The 46 `icons/tikz/*.tex` Edits-panel op icons go through the identical pipeline into a SEPARATE generated file, `src/webview/opIcons.ts`** — a distinct generator (`icons/build-op-icons.mjs`, `cd icons && make ops-ts`) reading `icons/svg-ops/*.svg`, not `svg-ui/*.svg`, and sharing the currentColor/fill-opacity post-processing logic with `build-toolbar-icons.mjs` via `icons/svgIconPostProcess.mjs` (kept in one place so the two can't drift). Separate generator because `opIcons.ts`'s type is `Record<PanelOpId, string>` — `PanelOpId` imported from `opCatalog.ts`, not a self-contained union like `ToolbarIconId` — so an id mismatch is a `tsc` error, not just a runtime check. This set used to be flat-color PNG previews with no wiring at all; `editsPanel.ts`'s `buildTabContent()` now sets each icon via `icon.innerHTML = OP_ICONS [entry.id]` (a plain `Record` index — no `?? fallback` needed, the `Record` type already makes a missing key unreachable).
- **A handful of the 46 op-icon sources needed real redesigns, not just a pipeline swap, because a small `.op-btn` has a real (non-white, theme-dependent) `--vscode-button-secondaryBackground` behind it, unlike a fixed white PNG canvas.** Two categories, both caught by rendering the set against the actual panel background before wiring it in: (1) 14 files used a bare `\draw[gray]` stroke (not a `gray!N` *fill*) for de-emphasis/ghost lines — fixed the same way `isolate.tex` already did, dropping the stroke color and keeping/adding `dashed`. (2) 5 files (the three hole ops, the torus, and boolean subtract) used `\fill[white]` to visually "erase" part of a filled shape — against a non-white background this painted a solid white blob instead of reading as a hole. Fixed with a genuine even-odd compound path (`\fill[gray!N, even odd rule] (outer)(hole);`, one non-self- overlapping polygon when two sub-holes would otherwise double-cancel) so the hole area is actually transparent; boolean subtract (two circles that only *partially* overlap, not a hole-in-a-container shape) needed the same trick applied to a clip instead — `\pgfseteorule` + `\clip (A)(B);` clips to "A xor B", and filling A inside that clip gives exactly the "A minus B" crescent. Note `\clip[even odd rule] (...)` and `\path[clip, even odd rule] (...)` both throw `"Extra options not allowed for clipping path command"` in this TikZ version — the low-level `\pgfseteorule` call before a plain `\clip` is what actually works. Full write-up: `icons/README.md`.
- **Every icon is `currentColor`-based, not a fixed color** — the generator strips `pdftocairo`'s literal black (`rgb(0%, 0%, 0%)`) stroke/fill down to `currentColor`, and any gray shading fill (from a TikZ `gray!N` fill) down to `currentColor` + a proportional `fill-opacity` (`(100-N)/100`), so an icon's relative internal shading survives (e.g. `volume`'s front/top/side faces) instead of flattening to one constant tint. The fixed `width`/ `height` `pdftocairo` emits are stripped too — only `viewBox` survives, so CSS (`.toolbar-icon svg { width: 1em; height: 1em; }` in `media/viewer.css`) controls the rendered size, and the icon inherits whatever `color` its containing button/text already has (VS Code already themes those) instead of setting its own.
- **Gotcha, hit once and now regression-tested:** `pdftocairo -svg` always emits a fill as a `fill="rgb(...)" fill-opacity="1"` *pair*. A first-pass regex that only rewrote the `fill="rgb(...)"` part left the original `fill-opacity="1"` trailing right after the new one — two `fill-opacity` attributes on one `<path>`, and the second (unwanted) one silently won, erasing the intended partial-opacity shading. The generator's regex now consumes both in one match. `src/toolbarIcons.test.ts` asserts no generated path ever has more than one `fill-opacity` attribute.
- Since this module has **no `vscode`/DOM dependency**, both the host (`provider.ts`, building static HTML) and the webview (`partsPanel.ts`/`meshingPanel.ts`) import it directly — no bundling or `asWebviewUri` needed, the SVG markup is just inlined into whatever HTML string or `innerHTML` assignment needs it.
- Chevrons (`▾▸⌄⌃`), plain arrows (`↑↓←→`), and the zoom `−`/`+` are deliberately NOT covered by this — they already render as clean monochrome glyphs in every renderer, unlike real emoji, so replacing them would add icons for no visual benefit. Same for `#vc-ortho` and the clip axis letters.
- **Only FILLS get the gray→`currentColor` mapping — never strokes.** The generator's second regex matches `fill="rgb(X%, X%, X%)" fill-opacity="1"` specifically; a TikZ `gray!N` *stroke* comes out of `pdftocairo` as `stroke="rgb(X%, X%, X%)"` and is left untouched, i.e. frozen at a literal gray in both themes. So shade with `fill=gray!N`, and where an icon needs a de-emphasised outline use `dashed` instead (what `isolate` does).
- **Design at 1em, not at 34px** — two things that look fine large and fail at real toolbar size, both found by rendering the set headlessly through `media/viewer.css`'s actual `.toolbar-icon svg { width: 1em }` rule: a `>=Stealth` arrow tip on an `arc` disappears entirely (`undo`/`redo` use an explicit `\filldraw` triangle instead of `->`), and glyphs sharing a silhouette need a **fill** difference, not just an edge-style one (`edges`/`xray`/`hiddenLines`/`flat`/`wireframe` are five variants of the same isometric cube, separated by `gray!10`…`gray!35` fill levels).
- **`pdftocairo` (poppler-utils) is required and has no safe substitute.** The generator keys on the exact literal `rgb(0%, 0%, 0%)` that `pdftocairo -svg` emits. `dvisvgm` writes `fill='#000'` instead, so the black→`currentColor` rewrite would silently no-op — and because the result then contains no `rgb(0%, 0%, 0%)` literal, `toolbarIcons.test.ts`'s "no literal black" assertion still **passes** while every icon is permanently black in dark themes. Silent, test-invisible corruption; install poppler-utils instead.
- **Rebuild only the ids you changed** (`make svg-ui/<id>.svg` + `node build-toolbar-icons.mjs`, or `make svg-ops/<id>.svg` + `node build-op-icons.mjs` for an op icon), not bare `make ts`/`make ops-ts` — each depends on `ui`/`ops`, which re-renders every `.tex` file in that set, and a different local TeX Live/poppler version produces byte-different path rounding that churns every committed `svg-ui/*.svg`/`svg-ops/*.svg` and the whole of the corresponding generated `.ts` file.

## Top File menu (Open / Save / Save As / Export)

A full-width `#menubar` sits at the very top of the webview (`getHtml` in `provider.ts`) with a single **File ▾** dropdown. Two trigger surfaces converge on the same host code — the in-webview dropdown buttons (which `postMessage`) and real VS Code commands/keybindings — so they must stay in lockstep. Non-negotiable invariants:

- **The CAD file is still never written.** "Save" (`saveSidecars` message / `cad-preview.save` / Ctrl+S) only force-flushes the parts/edits/mesh sidecars immediately (clearing the three debounce timers, then `writeParts`/ `writeEdits`/`writeMeshOptions`+`writeGeoScript` on the retained `currentParts`/`currentEdits`/`currentVariables`/`currentMeshOptions`). "Save As…" and "Export…" both reuse the existing `exportRequest` → `handleExport` flow — do not invent a second export path. "Open…" (`openFile` message / `cad-preview.open` / Ctrl+O) is host-only: `showOpenDialog` + `vscode.openWith` into `cad-preview.mesh` (`openFileDialog()`), the only File action that works with no editor focused.
- **Commands reach the focused editor via `activeSession`.** Each `resolveCustomEditor` builds an `EditorSession` (`{uri, export(), save()}`) and registers it as `this.activeSession` on `onDidChangeViewState` when `webviewPanel.active`, clearing it on dispose. `save`/`saveAs`/`export` commands no-op when nothing is focused; keybindings are scoped `when: activeCustomEditorId == 'cad-preview.mesh'` so Ctrl+O/Ctrl+S don't clobber VS Code's globals. Commands are registered in `register()` alongside the custom-editor provider and disposed with it.
- **`#toolbar` is offset below the menubar** (`top: 42px` in `viewer.css`) since it's `position:absolute` on the body and the in-flow menubar would otherwise overlap it. The dropdown wiring (`setupFileMenu()` in `main.ts`) lives inside the same `try/catch` as the other view-control setup so a failure can't block the `ready` handshake. The File icons (`home`/`open`/`save`/`saveAs`) are part of the generated `tikz-ui` toolbar-icon set — see the section above.
- The File menu's own open/close plumbing now lives in the shared `src/webview/dropdownMenu.ts` (see the next section); `setupFileMenu()` is just the `item(id, msg)` wiring on top of a `setupDropdown()` handle.

## Toolbar dropdown menus

The toolbar collapsed from ~21 inline controls into 3 always-visible buttons (`#fit`, `#tree-toggle`, `#meshing-toggle`) plus 4 dropdown triggers (**View ▾** / **Select ▾** / **Measure ▾** / **Markup ▾**), all sharing the File ▾ menu's markup pattern. Non-negotiable invariants:

- **`src/webview/dropdownMenu.ts` is the ONE implementation** — `setupDropdown (triggerId, panelId)` returns a `DropdownHandle | null` (null, never a throw, on a missing element, since callers sit in `main.ts`'s shared setup `try`). Module scope holds only a `Set<DropdownHandle>` and a `boolean`; all DOM access is inside the functions, per this repo's no-DOM-at-import rule (no jsdom in vitest — the trap `dotTexture()`/`labelOverlay.ts` already hit). Opening one menu closes the others; exactly 2 global listeners total exist regardless of menu count.
- **Two bugs this fixed, don't reintroduce them.** (1) The old containment test was `e.target !== btn`; every trigger wraps its icon in a `<span class="toolbar-icon"><svg>`, so clicking an *open* menu's own icon made `e.target` the `<svg>` → `pointerdown` closed it → the trigger's `click` reopened it, i.e. the File menu could not be dismissed by clicking its icon. Use `trigger.contains(t) || panel.contains(t)`. (2) The dismissing `pointerdown` runs in the **capture** phase and calls `preventDefault()` + `stopPropagation()`, so the click that closes a menu doesn't also reach what's underneath — `#markup-canvas` is `pointer-events:auto` while markup mode is on, so without this, clicking away from the Markup menu drew a stroke.
- **Clicks inside a panel deliberately do NOT close it** (flip a mode, pick a tool, choose a colour in one visit). One-shot items (`#menu-*`, `#screenshot`) call `handle.close()` themselves.
- **Every pre-existing element id survived the move** — `#grid`, `#edges`, `#screenshot`, `#sel-toggle`, `.sel-mode[data-mode]`, `#select-group`, `#measure-toggle`, `#measure-clear`, `#measure-readout`, `#markup-toggle`, `#markup-color`, `#markup-undo/redo/clear` are all still queried by `getElementById`/`querySelectorAll` exactly as before, just nested deeper. `#measure-tool`/`#markup-tool` kept their ids but changed from `<select>` to a `.tb-row` container of `data-tool` buttons (an `<option>` can't hold an SVG), so `.value`/`change` readers became a click loop + a local variable. `#measure-group`/`#markup-group` are gone.
- **`#meshing-toggle` stays top-level on purpose** — its `.active` is host-driven (`main.ts`'s `meshingResult` adds it, the panel's Clear removes it) and must stay truthful about whether an overlay is displayed. Folding it into a menu would force those two host paths to also update a trigger.
- **A trigger mirrors its mode's `.active`** so the collapsed toolbar still shows that Select/Measure/Markup is live, plus a `::after` dot (because `inputValidation-infoBackground` is nearly invisible in some light themes) and a dynamic `title`. Never a dynamic *label* — the toolbar is right-anchored, so a width change jitters the whole row.
- **Two CSS specificity traps.** `#toolbar button` is `(0,1,0,1)` and paints every descendant button solid blue, so (a) menu-item styling must be written `#toolbar .tb-dropdown button, #file-dropdown button` `(0,1,1,1)`, not a bare `.tb-dropdown button` `(0,0,1,1)` which loses; and (b) the 1-of-N segments then lose to *that*, so they're `#toolbar .tb-dropdown .sel-mode` `(0,1,2,0)`. The mode toggles (`#toolbar #measure-toggle.active` etc.) are `(0,2,1,0)` and need no change.
- **`#toolbar`'s z-index is 15**, above `#view-controls` (10) and `#markup-canvas` (5), so an open panel paints over both; `.tb-dropdown`'s 30 is local to that stacking context. `#measure-readout` is a **sibling of** `#toolbar`, not a child, on its own absolutely-positioned line at `top: 74px` — inline, its longest error string would stretch the right-anchored strip off-canvas, the exact problem this regroup fixed.

## New Blank Model — starting with no source file (issue-shaped UX gap, closed)

Every entry point could only ever OPEN an existing file — `openFileDialog`, drag-and-drop, the Models tree, `loadPreprocessDialog` — all four ending in `vscode.openWith(<existing uri>)`. Meanwhile the Edits panel already had a complete from-scratch vocabulary whose creation ops are documented as needing no operands (`occtOperations.addPrimitive` appends into `compound(existing + new)`, and "existing" being empty is fine). The capability existed; there was no document to reach it from.

- **A blank document is an ordinary B-rep document whose source file is an EMPTY COMPOUND.** Everything authored afterwards lives in the replayable `<file>.brep.edits.json` op-list, exactly as for an edited `bull.stp` — this is the general "base shape ∘ ops" model with an empty base, not a new concept. **The read-only-CAD invariant is untouched**: creation happens BEFORE any editor session exists, precisely like `loadPreprocessDialog` and `handlePromoteToBrep` already write brand-new files host-side and then `openWith` them.
- **`buildPrimitivesFile` was ALREADY a `Pipeline` key** (`mcpTools.ts`'s interface, `kernelWorker.ts`'s dispatch table, `kernelClient.ts`'s client entry) — so this feature needed **zero** new kernel plumbing, only the removal of one line. **That line — `if (ops.length === 0) throw` — was dead code**: its only caller (`mcpTools.ts`'s `decompose_to_primitives`) already guards `emission.ops.length === 0` itself and warns "No primitives recognized — nothing written." rather than calling. Removing it is byte-for-byte behavior-preserving for that tool, which is why `doc/mcp-server.md` needed no edit at all — a documented non-change, not silence.
- **`.brep`, not `.step` — a preference, and the probe says so rather than a guess.** All three writers accept an empty compound (BREP 192 bytes, STEP 1661 — its `Transfer` genuinely returns `RetDone` and emits 20 entities — IGES 648). BREP was still chosen: it is OCCT's native serialization, carries no unit header to declare for geometry that isn't there yet, and skips `handleBRep`'s STEP/IGES `latin1` unit-detection path entirely. **Stated consequence**: `exportTargetsFor` excludes a document's own format, so a blank document's Export… offers STEP/IGES + every mesh target but **not** BREP.
- **Refuses to overwrite an existing file**, rather than relying on VS Code's own overwrite prompt (which reads as routine and is easy to click through). Blanking an existing model would leave its own `.edits.json` replaying against an empty base — plausible-looking, silently wrong geometry, the misleading-false-result mode this codebase rejects features over. The refusal names **File ▸ Open…** as the fix, per the `dirtyGuard`/`assertNotSourcePath` "ship a fix with the refusal" convention. Also reuses `loadPreprocessDialog`'s `routeFile` cross-check verbatim, since the save dialog's filter is advisory on some platforms.
- **Three trigger surfaces, one code path**: `#menu-new` → the new `newBlank` protocol message → `newBlankModelDialog()`; the `cad-preview.new` command (registered beside `cad-preview.open`, deliberately NOT `withSession` — it must work with no tab focused); and a second `viewsWelcome` link in the Models view. **No keybinding** — `Ctrl+N` is VS Code's global New File and must not be shadowed (Import SVG/DXF set the same precedent).

### The defect this uncovered: an empty shape aborts the OCCT WASM instance

**`BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh_2` on an empty `TopoDS_Compound` aborts the entire OCCT module** — `wasmTable.get(...) is not a function`, a hard fault that poisons the instance until `resetOcct()` runs, not an OCCT exception `wrapOcctFault` can wrap meaningfully. Found by the probe, then narrowed by bisecting `loadBRep`'s tail one call per fresh process:

- `readShape` on the 192-byte file → **fine** (`IsNull() === false`, `ShapeType === 0` = `TopAbs_COMPOUND`)
- the solid `TopExp_Explorer` → **fine** (walks 0 solids)
- `extractEdges` → **fine** (0), `extractVertices` → **fine** (0)
- `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh_2` → **aborts**, from a file-read compound AND an in-memory one alike

**The threshold is "no sub-shapes at all", NOT "no faces" — probed, not assumed.** A compound holding one edge + one vertex and zero faces (exactly what a blank document looks like once the user draws a line before any surface) meshes fine with `IsDone() === true`. So `tessellateByGroup` explores for any `TopAbs_VERTEX` and returns `[]` before constructing the mesher when there is none; a faces-free wireframe shape deliberately still goes through the mesher.

This is a genuine latent robustness fix, not just blank-document scaffolding: it was reachable from any op list that reduced an ordinary document to nothing — a boolean that cuts a solid away entirely, or undoing back past the last op that produced geometry.

### Verification

Probe first, per this codebase's standing discipline (throwaway esbuild-bundled script driving the real exported entry points, not committed, one stage per process so an abort in one can never be mistaken for accumulated state from another): empty BREP writes (192 B) → reads back as 0 groups/edges/points with tree `{"id":"root","label":"BREP","children":[]}` → `[addBox]` on that base gives exactly 1 group / 6 faces / 12 edges / 8 points → `loadBRepCached`'s append-reuse branch works off an empty base → `exportBRep` bakes it to an 18644-byte STEP → that STEP reads back as 1 group / 6 faces. All six green, so the `emptyBase`-flag fallback (which would have widened three hot kernel functions) was never needed.

`npm run test:integration` covers the real flow end-to-end in a real VS Code: the command writes a 192-byte file whose bytes actually start with `DBRep_DrawableShape` (not merely non-empty — that catches a zero-filled or half-written file that would still pass a length check), the **command's own** `openWith` lands the tab, the overwrite refusal leaves a pre-existing file byte-identical, and — via `extension.ts`'s `ExtensionMode.Test` seam — the document posts `geometry` and never `error`, which is what proves the empty-shape guard through the live kernel. Bug-injected by removing the guard: the trace flips to `[…,"macros","error"]` and both assertions fail.

**A test-harness bug worth recording, because it broke an unrelated case and the cause was not obvious.** The first version of that integration case called `openDocument(dest)` itself after `waitForFile`, issuing a SECOND `openWith` on the same URI while the command's own was still in flight. That left a tab behind which `closeAll()` had already run past, and the *next* case's `updateWorkspaceFolders` then never propagated — surfacing as 9 failures in the Models-view test, ~200 lines away, with nothing pointing at the real cause. Bisected by disabling each new case in turn. Fixed by waiting for the command's own open instead, which is also the stronger assertion.

## Collapsible sidebar sections

Nine sections (`#tree-panel`, `#parts-panel`, `#edits-panel`, `#meshing-panel`, `#mass-panel`, `#mesh-health-panel`, `#region-fit-panel`, `#macros-panel`, `#standard-parts-panel`) stacked in a fixed 220px column, all always expanded. Each now collapses to just its header, independently, persisted per document.

- **A dedicated chevron button per header, not a click-anywhere header — forced by the markup, not preference.** Every header already contains action buttons (Isolate/New, Undo/Redo/Clear, Generate/Export/Clear plus two `<select>`s, Compute, Check/Promote/Repair, …), and `#tree-header` additionally holds `<input id="tree-filter">`, which a header-wide handler would toggle on every keystroke's click. A `<button aria-expanded>` is also focusable and screen-reader-correct. Copies the FE Mesh "Advanced settings" chevron precedent (`▸`/`▾`, `classList.toggle("collapsed")`).
- **The chevron must be a SIBLING of `#x-title`, never a child** — `TreePanel` overwrites `#tree-title.textContent` on every render (`treePanel.ts:30`) and would wipe a nested one. Pinned by a `test:webview` assertion.
- **THREE independent visibility mechanisms act on these panels and must never fight**: `#tree-panel.visible` (is the Components tree shown at all), the `hidden` PROPERTY on `#mesh-health-panel`/`#region-fit-panel` (source-format eligibility), and `.collapsed`. The first two set `display` on the PANEL, so the collapse CSS must not — it only hides the panel's own non-header children (`#side > .collapsed > :not(.panel-header)`) and drops it to `flex: 0 0 auto`. That is what makes all three compose in any combination rather than overriding one another.
- **`:not(.panel-header)` rather than `#x-body`, because the panels are not uniform** — `#meshing-panel` has FOUR body siblings (progress/body/status/quality) and `#standard-parts-panel` three (search-row/body/status). A `#x-body`-based rule would silently leave three of `#meshing-panel`'s four children visible.
- **`flex: 0 0 auto` is load-bearing for exactly two panels.** `#parts-panel` and `#edits-panel` are `flex: 1`; without it a collapsed header still claims its share of the column while its body is hidden — it *looks* collapsed and frees nothing. Bug-injected: the panel stays at 191.6px with a 29.6px header, caught by asserting the panel shrinks to its header height rather than by a class-name check.
- **The shared header rule became `.panel-header` instead of a 7-id list, and that fixed a real drift.** `#region-fit-header` and `#standard-parts-header` matched **no** CSS rule in the whole file and rendered unstyled. The class is set on all nine headers in `viewerDom.ts` and is also what the collapse rule keys off, so the two can never disagree about which element is a header. The `border-top` sub-list stays an explicit id list on purpose (`#region-fit-panel`/`#standard-parts-panel` already carry `border-top` on the panel itself).
- **`#variables-section` deliberately gets no chevron** — nested inside the already-scrolling `#edits-scroll`; the FE Mesh precedent covers nested collapse if it is ever wanted.
- **Persistence rides `ViewState.collapsedPanels`**, the `layout`/`panes` precedent exactly: a top-level sibling of `view` in `.view.json`, **no `VIEW_STATE_SIDECAR_VERSION` bump**, tolerant parse via the pure `sanitizeCollapsedPanels` (unknown ids dropped, non-array → `[]`), omitted entirely when nothing is collapsed so an untouched sidecar stays byte-stable. Host needs **zero** changes — `viewStateStore.ts` and the `viewChanged` handler pass `ViewState` through opaquely, so external-change reconciliation works for free.
  - **Two traps, both bug-injection-verified.** `serializeViewStateJson` must destructure `collapsedPanels` out alongside `layout`/`panes`, or it lands INSIDE `view` where the parse never looks (8 tests fail). And `parseViewStateJson` must fold it into `base` **BEFORE** the `if (!layout || layout === "1x1") return base` early return, or it is silently dropped for the overwhelmingly common single-pane sidecar (3 tests fail).
  - Collapsing moves no camera, so the toggle calls `scheduleViewSave()` itself — exactly like the split-view layout picker. `setCollapsed` (the restore path) deliberately never fires `onChange`, the silent-`load()` contract `PartsModel`/`PlanesModel` follow.
  - **`viewStateSidecar.ts` (host-side) imports `sanitizeCollapsedPanels` by VALUE**, which is safe for the same reason as its existing `DISPLAY_MODES`/`viewerPanes` imports: `collapsiblePanels.ts` is pure data + pure functions with no three.js import and no DOM access at module scope. Contrast its `import type { ClipAxis }`, which must stay type-only because `clipping.ts` has a top-level `import * as THREE`.

### A pre-existing defect this uncovered: the gated panels were never actually hidden

`#mesh-health-panel { display: flex }` and `#region-fit-panel { display: flex }` are unconditional author rules, and no `[hidden]` override existed for either (a grep for `[hidden]` found only `#measure-tol-group` and `#plane-entry`). **An author `display` rule beats the UA's `[hidden] { display: none }`, so `meshHealthPanel.ts`/`regionFitPanel.ts`'s `panel.hidden = !eligible` was completely INERT** — both panels rendered for every source format, including B-rep, where they have nothing to show. This directly contradicted CLAUDE.md's own claim that Mesh Health is "visible ONLY for a genuinely NATIVE .stl/.obj/.ply file"; both features carry the documented F5-verification gap, which is presumably why it was never seen.

**A visible side effect of the fix, and a false alarm it caused — recorded so the next reader doesn't chase it.** With the two panels correctly gone, the remaining sections shift down into the region `#view-controls` overlays (it is `position: absolute; left: 50%; translateX(-50%); z-index: 10` — a wide centred floating panel that covers the bottom of a 220px sidebar at ordinary window widths). In the regenerated `viewer-main.png` that makes Mass Properties' "Compute" *look* clipped, which was initially read as the new chevron squeezing header buttons. It is not: measuring every header control in the real bundle shows `#mass-refresh` at `right: 212` against a sidebar edge of `221`, with `scrollWidth === clientWidth`, **with and without** the chevron — nothing overflows at 220px. A speculative CSS "fix" (header `padding-left` + ellipsizing titles) was written, then reverted once the measurement disproved the premise. A guard assertion was also written and then **deleted**: two separate injections (reverting the fix, then a 90px chevron) both failed to trip it, and by this repo's own standard an assertion that cannot be made to fail is worse than none. The `#view-controls`-over-sidebar overlap is pre-existing and orthogonal to this change.

This is the **third** instance of a hazard `media/viewer.css`'s own comments already document twice. Fixed with the two-line `#id[attr]` override (specificity (1,1,0) beats (1,0,0)), and it now has the regression test it never had: bug-injecting the fix out makes the panels measure **79px** and **107px** on a B-rep source. The collapse CSS structurally cannot compound it, since `.collapsed` only ever sets `display` on children.

## What's New panel (post-update changelog)

`activate()` (`src/extension.ts`) fires `maybeShowWhatsNew(context)` (`src/whatsNew.ts`), fire-and-forget (never `await`ed — it must not delay or block activation), which pops a "What's New" webview panel after an update.

- **First use of `context.globalState`/`workspaceState` in this codebase.** Everything else persists via sidecar JSON files next to the source document; this is the one exception, since there's no per-document source to attach a "last version seen" fact to. Key: `"cadPreview.lastVersion"`.
- **Fresh installs are silent.** No stored value means nothing to diff against, so `maybeShowWhatsNew` just records the current version and shows nothing — it does NOT dump the entire changelog history on a brand-new user.
- **`CHANGELOG.md` is read-only from this feature, and only ever read, never written** — same "one-way" spirit as the generated `.geo` sidecar, but in the opposite direction: this feature has zero write path to it. It already ships inside the packaged `.vsix` (nothing in `.vscodeignore` excludes root-level `.md` files), so no build step was needed to make it available at runtime via `vscode.workspace.fs.readFile`.
- **Version comparison is hand-rolled, not a semver dependency** (`compareVersions` in `src/changelogParser.ts`, plain numeric `x.y.z` triples) — this project's versions are always simple triples, so adding a bundled semver package would just be another dependency to license-check for no real benefit.
- **`src/changelogParser.ts` is vscode-free and unit-tested** (`parseChangelog`/`entriesSince`/`renderEntryHtml`), mirroring this project's parse-vs-store split (`partsSidecar.ts`/`partsStore.ts`, etc.); `src/whatsNew.ts` is the `vscode`-dependent half (globalState check + the actual `createWebviewPanel` call).
- **The panel opens in `vscode.ViewColumn.Beside`, never `Active`** — the check runs from `activate()`, which VS Code triggers exactly when a CAD file's custom editor is also about to open (there's no `onStartupFinished` activation event in `package.json`, only the implicit `contributes.customEditors`/`contributes.commands` ones) — `Beside` means the two never fight over the same tab slot.
- **This is the first standalone `vscode.window.createWebviewPanel` in the extension** — every other webview goes through `CustomReadonlyEditorProvider`. It gets its own small nonce-gated CSP HTML (styled with `var(--vscode-*)` theme variables, no external stylesheet), sharing only `getNonce()` — pulled out to `src/nonce.ts` — with `provider.ts`'s `getHtml`.
- A manual **`CAD Preview: Show What's New`** command (`cad-preview.whatsNew`, standalone like `cad-preview.open`, registered in `provider.ts`'s `registerCommands()`) always shows the **full** changelog via `showLatestWhatsNew`, regardless of `globalState` — distinct semantics from the automatic "since you last looked" popup.

## MCP server (headless agent access)

A standalone stdio MCP server (`src/mcpServer.ts` → third esbuild bundle `dist/mcp-server.js`) exposes the load/edit/mesh/export pipeline to AI agents with no VS Code — registration + tool reference in `doc/mcp-server.md`. Non-negotiable invariants:

- **stdout IS the JSON-RPC channel.** OCCT prints through `console.log`, so `mcpServer.ts`'s **very first statements** rebind `console.log/info/warn/debug` to stderr — before anything can initialize a WASM factory. gmsh-wasm (0.2.0+) no longer goes through `console.*` on Node at all — see `getGmsh`'s explicit `print`/`printErr` overrides in the "Meshing (GMSH-JS)" section above, which route it back through `console.error` so this same rebinding still applies to it. Never add a plain `console.log`/`process.stdout.write` anywhere the MCP bundle can reach; `npm run mcp:smoke` fails on any stdout pollution (it breaks the JSON-RPC framing immediately).
- **Module split for testability:** `mcpServer.ts` (SDK wiring + zod schemas) → `mcpTools.ts` (18 tool handlers over an injected `Pipeline` object; imports **only `import type`** from `occtService`/ `gmshService`/`entityFacts`/`renderService` — their `.wasm` imports (or, for `renderService.ts`, its dynamic `import("playwright")`) resolve under esbuild's wasm-path plugin/external list, never under vitest) → `mcpSidecars.ts` (node-fs sidecar store over the same pure `*Sidecar.ts` parsers the extension uses; must stay byte-compatible with `provider.ts`). **Since the "OCCT/Gmsh/meshio++ in a forked child process" work (roadmap item, closed for this server — see CLAUDE.md's dedicated section below), `mcpServer.ts` no longer imports the real service-module functions at all** — its `Pipeline` is `createKernelClient(extensionPath)` (`src/kernelClient.ts`); the actual `occtService`/`gmshService`/`entityFacts`/`renderService`/etc. functions are now only ever imported by `src/kernelWorker.ts`, the forked child's own entry point (its own, separate esbuild bundle, `dist/kernel-worker.js`) — `mcpTools.ts`'s own `import type`-only discipline was what made this swap possible with zero changes to that file.
- **`wrap()` (`mcpServer.ts`) does two things beyond the original JSON-result round trip, both additive — every pre-existing `wrap((args) => tool(ctx, args))` call site is untouched.** (1) Its handler now takes a second `onProgress: ProgressCallback` parameter (the type is defined in `mcpTools.ts`, not `mcpServer.ts`, so `mcpTools.ts` stays MCP-SDK-free — `wrap()` builds it from the SDK's real `extra: RequestHandlerExtra` argument, calling `extra.sendNotification({method:"notifications/progress", params:{progressToken,...}})` only when the caller opted in via `_meta.progressToken`; a no-op otherwise). Only `generate_mesh`/ `export_mesh` use it, and only for start(0)/done(1) signaling — Gmsh's `generate()` has no mid-call progress hook (see "Meshing (GMSH-JS)" below), so this is never a genuine percentage. **`describe_capabilities` is the one tool NOT registered through `wrap()`** — it has no `inputSchema`, so the SDK calls its callback with a single `extra` argument (no `args`), an arity `wrap()` doesn't model; it's inlined directly instead (harmless, since it's instant/pure and progress reporting would be meaningless for it). (2) A handler result carrying an `images: RenderImage[]` field (currently only `render_snapshot`'s) gets one text block (JSON, with `images` summarized as `[{label, mimeType}]` — base64 omitted so the JSON payload doesn't double the response size) followed by one `{type:"image", data, mimeType}` content block per image — the SDK's `CallToolResultSchema` already supports an image content type (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.29.0's `ImageContentSchema`, confirmed present in the installed version).
- **Stateless per call, sidecars are the state.** Every tool re-reads the model + sidecars fresh and writes sidecars back — the same `<model>.edits.json` / `.parts.json` / `.mesh.json` / `.geo` files the extension reads on reopen, so agent and extension edits interoperate. The WASM singletons are already memoized module-level, so statelessness costs one file read.
- **The CAD source file is still never written** — every caller-chosen output path goes through `mcpSidecars.assertNotSourcePath`.
- **Ops are raw JSON through `validateEditOp`** (not per-op tool schemas, which would drift against the 44-kind union). The one hand-maintained piece is `OP_PARAM_DOCS` in `mcpTools.ts` (per-kind parameter docs for `describe_capabilities`), locked to the live op catalog by `mcpTools.test.ts`.
- **Headless scope:** B-rep sources get the full pipeline (edits baked via `exportBRep(...,"step")` before meshing, mirroring `provider.ts`'s `resolveMeshInput`); `.stl` is meshable from raw file bytes (edits NOT baked — warned); `.obj`/`.ply`/`.gltf` can't be meshed/exported headless (their serialization is the webview's Three.js). Mesh-legal ops are still persisted to the sidecar for mesh sources (the webview replays them); `BREP_ONLY_OPS` are rejected.
- `extensionPath` resolves to the bundle dir's parent (`dist/` holds the WASM binaries beside the bundle), overridable via `CAD_PREVIEW_ROOT`.
- `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` + `zod` are MIT (GPL-compatible, per the License section's bundled-dependency rule) and are bundled only into `dist/mcp-server.js`, never `dist/extension.js`.

## Agent feedback tools: inspect, measure, render_snapshot (P1 roadmap features)

Three tools closing the "agent edits headlessly, then can't verify or see the result" gap `doc/roadmap.md`'s P1 tier identified, mined from `earthtojake/text-to-cad`'s agent-feedback conventions (fact-only tools, three-valued pass/fail/need-more-info verdicts, "visual review is diagnostic not authoritative"). Non-negotiable invariants:

- **`inspect`/`measure` (`src/entityFacts.ts`) are B-rep-only, same gate as `get_mass_properties`/`compare_models`**, and follow the identical dispatch convention: `mcpTools.ts` never parses `solid-N`/`face-N`/ `edge-N`/`point-N` ids itself, it forwards raw strings into the pipeline function, which regex-dispatches (mirroring `massProperties.ts`'s `computeMassProperties` exactly) to `collectSolids`/`collectFaces`/ `collectEdges`/`collectVertices` (all four already exported from `occtOperations.ts`). One parse/replay per call (`readShape` + `applyEditsBRep`, no shape cache), same as every other B-rep read path.
- **`inspect`'s `center` is deliberately the bounding-box centre (`bboxCenter`), NOT `get_mass_properties`' area/volume-weighted `centerOfMass`** — for an asymmetric shape these are different points. Stated in both the code doc comment and the tool description so an agent doesn't conflate the two. `measure`'s distance/axis-component math is built on the same bbox-centre convention.
- **`facePlane` (`occtOperations.ts`) was promoted from module-private to exported** for `entityFacts.ts`'s `normal` field — its only other caller is `mateShape`, unchanged.
- **`inspect` reports `planeOrigin` beside `normal` for a planar face** (roadmap "two small plane-handling gaps", closed): the plane's own origin as OCCT computed it (`gp_Pln.Location()` via the already-shared `facePlane`, which always returned `{pt, nl}` — only `.nl` was kept before). A point genuinely ON the face's plane, unlike `center` (the bbox centre, which is coplanar with a planar face but not guaranteed to lie on an annular or concave one) — usable directly as `planePoint` for `section`/`splitByPlane`/`mirror`. Purely additive (`null` for non-planar faces/other kinds, same tolerant-consumer convention as every other optional field); zero extra OCCT calls; flows through `inspectEntity`'s verbatim spread and `kernelIpc`'s plain-object marshalling untouched. Verified live via a new `mcp:smoke` assertion (the added box's planar face: origin present + coplanar with the bbox centre along `normal`).
- **`SurfaceType` mapping, verified against the live WASM** (brute-force probing, same convention as every other OCCT call in this codebase): `GeomAbs_SurfaceType.GeomAbs_Plane.value = 0`, `GeomAbs_Cylinder = 1`, `GeomAbs_Cone = 2`, `GeomAbs_Sphere = 3`, `GeomAbs_Torus = 4` — confirmed by building a box/cylinder/cone/sphere/torus via `BRepPrimAPI_Make*` (the already-verified primitive ctors from the "Geometry editing" section above) and reading `BRepAdaptor_Surface_2(face,true).GetType().value` off each. The remaining members (`BezierSurface=5`, `BSplineSurface=6`, `SurfaceOfRevolution=7`, `SurfaceOfExtrusion=8`, `OffsetSurface=9`, `OtherSurface=10`) were read off the enum directly, not individually probed — `entityFacts.ts` collapses all of them to `"other"`.
- **`render_snapshot` (`src/renderService.ts`) is the runtime sibling of `scripts/screenshots/`'s Playwright docs-build harness** — same real `media/viewer.js` bundle + `viewerDom.ts`'s `viewerBodyHtml()` DOM, same tiny static-file server + `/__harness` route + SwiftShader launch flags, but driven programmatically (one `renderViewRequest`/result round trip per view) instead of a fixed shot list, and fed geometry directly from an in-process `loadBRep()` result via `encodeBuffer()` (the exact transform `scripts/screenshots/fixtures-entry.ts` already uses) — **no temp file write or re-parse**. `scripts/screenshots/capture.mjs` itself is untouched; this is a deliberate sibling implementation for a different consumer (`dist/mcp-server.js` at agent-call time vs. `npm run docs:screenshots` at build time).
- **Playwright stays a devDependency — `render_snapshot` degrades gracefully, it never crashes the MCP server.** `.vscodeignore` excludes `node_modules/**` wholesale with no carve-out for `playwright`, and its Chromium binary is a separate ~150–300MB download (`npx playwright install chromium`) that never runs for an installed `.vsix`. `renderService.ts` does a dynamic `await import("playwright")` in a try/catch (and a separate `isRenderAvailable()` launch-then-close probe `render_snapshot` calls before doing any real work) — either path returns `{supported: false, reason}` instead of throwing when Playwright/Chromium aren't present, the same shape `get_mass_properties`/`compare_models` already use for mesh-format sources. `esbuild.mjs`'s `mcpConfig.external` lists `"playwright"` (alongside `gmsh-wasm`/`meshioplusplus`) so the dynamic import resolves via real `node_modules` at runtime rather than getting inlined into the bundle.
  - **A `try/catch` around the dynamic `import()` is NOT sufficient by itself — verified the hard way, via a real regression a routine `playwright` dependency bump (1.61.1 → 1.62.1) introduced.** `playwright-core`'s own `lib/bootstrap.js`, loaded as a side effect of `import("playwright")`, calls `process.exit(1)` **synchronously at module-load time** whenever `process.versions.node`'s major version is below its own minimum (raised to 20 in that bump). `process.exit()` is not a thrown exception — no `try/catch` can intercept it — so under Node < 20 the entire MCP server process died the instant `isRenderAvailable`/`renderSnapshot` first imported the package, confirmed via `npm run mcp:smoke` under Node 18 (this codebase's `package.json` declares no Node engine requirement of its own, since the extension/MCP server run under whatever Node the host — VS Code's bundled Electron, or an MCP client — provides, which may be older than whatever a devDependency itself has bumped to). Fixed with a version check (`nodeSupportsPlaywright()`, comparing `process.versions.node`'s major against `MIN_NODE_MAJOR_FOR_PLAYWRIGHT`) BEFORE either call site ever attempts `import("playwright")` at all — never triggering `playwright-core`'s own gate in the first place is the only way to keep this gracefully degrading rather than crashing. A lesson for any future optional-dependency dynamic import in this codebase: a `try/catch` around the import only catches a thrown rejection, not a dependency that decides to terminate the process itself.
- **New protocol messages, deliberately separate from `screenshotRequest`/ `screenshotResult`/`screenshotError`** (the interactive single-view Screenshot feature): `renderViewRequest` (host→webview: `direction`, `up?`, `label`, `focus?`/`hide?` as `{entityType,entityId}[]`, `wireframe?`) → `renderViewResult`/`renderViewError` (webview→host). A separate pair avoids `requestId` namespace confusion and carries fields the interactive feature has no reason to. `focus`/`hide` are typed `{entityType,entityId}[]` (matching `SelectedEntity`) rather than bare id strings because **no id-prefix-to-`EntityType` parser exists anywhere in the webview** (every existing caller already carries `entityType` alongside `entityId`) — `renderService.ts`'s `toSelectedEntity()` (host-side, already B-rep-aware from its own `solid-N`/`face-N`/`edge-N`/`point-N` regex dispatch) is the natural place to attach it.
- **`main.ts`'s `renderViewRequest` handler needs no state restoration** after mutating camera-up/view-direction/visibility/wireframe — every request this feature ever sends targets a disposable, harness-only headless page (`renderService.ts` creates and closes one Chromium page per `render_snapshot` call), never a live interactive session.
- **`Viewer.setCameraUp()`** (new, one-liner beside the existing `getCameraUp()`) is required for a near-vertical `direction` (the TOP view, `up:[0,0,-1]`) to avoid a gimbal-lock-like flip — the default `[0,1,0]` up is parallel to a straight-down/up view direction. **The default 4-view packet** (`renderService.ts`'s `DEFAULT_VIEWS`): two opposed isometrics (`[1,0.8,1]`/`[-1,-0.8,-1]` — negating all 3 components guarantees every outward face normal has a positive dot product with at least one of the two, by construction) + TOP + FRONT.
- **`src/webview/labelOverlay.ts`'s `drawLabel()` must never run at module load / import time** (no jsdom/canvas polyfill in this repo's vitest config — the exact trap `geometryBuilder.ts`'s `dotTexture()` hit once already, see the Points section above) — it's plain Canvas2D, only ever invoked from inside `Viewer.captureLabeledScreenshotBase64`, itself only reachable via a real `renderViewRequest` message. Not realistically unit-testable under this repo's setup; verified via manual F5 + `mcp:smoke` only.
- **`describe_capabilities` does NOT call `isRenderAvailable()`** — that would add a full browser launch/close to a tool meant to be instant and side-effect-free. Availability is self-discovered by calling `render_snapshot` and checking `supported`.
- **`describeCapabilities()`'s new `verdictConventions` field** states the fact-only/three-valued-verdict framing (tools report facts; agents render pass/fail/need-more-info; a `supported:false`/tool-or-network failure is need-more-info, never a silent pass or fail; `render_snapshot`'s images are diagnostic, convert concerns into `inspect`/`measure` checks) — referenced from `inspect`/`measure`/`render_snapshot`'s tool descriptions rather than repeating the policy prose in each one.

## Analytic surface parameters on `inspect` (roadmap item 8 Phase 1, closed)

`surfaceType: "cylinder"` used to be the entire answer — no radius, no axis — even though the `BRepAdaptor_Surface` handle was alive for exactly one statement before being deleted. `EntityFacts.surfaceParams` now carries the parameters, as a discriminated union rather than flat fields.

- **Every accessor was verified against the live WASM, because none had a call site here and the manifest is class-level only.** `Supported APIs.md` states outright that it lists class definitions with no method information, and this repo's history is a list of green-but-broken bindings (`ShapeFix_Shape`, `Message_ProgressRange_1`, `Interface_Static.CVal`, `HLRBRep_*`). Confirmed working: `surf.Cylinder()` → `{Radius, Location, Axis}`; `surf.Cone()` → `{RefRadius, SemiAngle, Apex, Location, Axis}`; `surf.Sphere()` → `{Radius, Location}` — **no `Axis()`**; `surf.Torus()` → `{MajorRadius, MinorRadius, Location, Axis}`. Each round-tripped against a primitive built with known values (`MakeCylinder_3(Ax2([10,20,30],[0,0,1]),3,7)` → r=3, loc=[10,20,30]; `MakeCone_3(Ax2(o,+z),5,2,4)` → RefRadius=5, SemiAngle=−36.8699° = −atan(3/4), Apex=[0,0,6.6667], analytically exact).
- **Values are in WORLD coordinates of the post-replay shape** — `BRepAdaptor_Surface` applies the face's own `TopLoc_Location` inside the accessor. Verified under translation in BOTH transform modes (`BRepBuilderAPI_Transform_2` with `copy=true`, and location-only with `copy=false` — the harsher case) and under rotation. Same frame as `bbox`/`center`/`planeOrigin`; nothing needs converting. Lengths stay in the source file's units, unconverted, like every other `EntityFacts` length.
- **Calling the wrong accessor throws a raw JS `number`** (an OCCT exception pointer, e.g. `16412792`), not an `Error` — `oc.getExceptionMessage` and `oc.OCJS` are both `undefined`, so there is no decoder. The module survives it. Two consequences, both handled: gate on `GetType()` **symbolically** before calling, and keep the `try/catch`, because an escaped throw would surface to the user as the literal string `"16412792"`. Note it is NOT matched by `isOcctWasmAbort`'s vocabulary, so it would not cause a spurious kernel reset — contained, but only because nothing lets it escape.
- **It works on algorithm-generated geometry, which is the case that actually matters.** Filleting a box edge at r=2 produces exactly one cylindrical face reporting `Radius=2`. An imported STEP's faces are analytic surfaces from someone else's kernel, not `BRepPrimAPI` output, so a reader that only worked on primitives we built ourselves would be worthless — `mcp:smoke` asserts the fillet case for this reason.
- **`SemiAngle` is signed and the sign is load-bearing**: positive means the radius GROWS along `axisDirection`. Verified both ways (`r1=5→r2=2` narrowing gives −36.87° with the apex ahead; `r1=2→r2=5` gives +36.87° with the apex behind). Reported in **degrees** (`semiAngleDeg`), per this codebase's universal `*Deg` convention.
- **`refRadius` is the radius AT `axisLocation`, and is meaningless without it** — which is why `apex` ships alongside as an absolute anchor. The identity `refRadius === |apex − axisLocation| · tan|semiAngle|` holds wherever OCCT puts the location, and is what `mcp:smoke` asserts, since a location-specific assertion would be brittle.
- **`axisLocation` is a point ON the axis and nothing more** — not the face's centre, not necessarily within its extent, not stable across kernels (a `BRepPrimAPI` cylinder puts it at the base circle's centre; a filleted box edge elsewhere). Documented explicitly, because an agent reading it as "where the hole starts" would be wrong.
- **A nested union, deliberately overruling the local all-flat convention.** Flat works elsewhere because each field has one meaning; here `radius` would mean cylinder-radius *or* sphere-radius *or* "the cone's radius at an unstated location". Disambiguating flat costs ten fields on a ten-field interface, all null for solids, edges, points, planar faces and `other` faces. The union is also the shape Phase 2's per-solid face inventory needs.
- **Never reuse `normal`/`planeOrigin` for a curved face's axis.** `main.ts`'s `applyClipFromFaceFacts` gates purely on `if (!facts.normal || !facts.planeOrigin)`; populating them for a cylinder would make Clip ▸ Face silently accept it and cut on a meaningless plane, replacing today's explanatory refusal. A `test:webview` case asserts that refusal, and a `mcp:smoke` case asserts a cylindrical face still reports both as null.
- **`faceSurfaceInfo` (`occtOperations.ts`) collapsed a double adaptor.** `getEntityFacts` used to build two `BRepAdaptor_Surface_2` on the same face — its own, plus `facePlane`'s — which also meant a plane's normal had two independent sources that could drift. `facePlane` is now a three-line projection of `faceSurfaceInfo`, so they cannot; a `mcp:smoke` assertion compares `surfaceParams.normal`/`.origin` to `normal`/`planeOrigin` with strict equality to pin it. This also made the hardcoded `SURFACE_TYPE_BY_VALUE` ordinal table dead — the dispatch is symbolic now. **`occtOperations.ts` must import `SurfaceType`/`SurfaceParams` with `import type`**: `entityFacts.ts` imports this module at runtime, so a value import closes a genuine `require()` cycle in the CJS bundle.
- **`BRepAlgoAPI_Defeaturing` was probed in the same pass and WORKS — recorded, not wired.** Box+fillet (7 faces, volume 991.4159) → `SetShape` / `AddFaceToRemove` / `Build` / `Shape` → 6 faces, volume 999.99999999999, i.e. the exact analytic box restored. Bound members include `SetShape, InputShape, AddFaceToRemove, AddFacesToRemove, FacesToRemove, Build, HasHistory, Modified, Generated, IsDeleted, History` plus `Shape()` from the base class; there is no `HasErrors`. **`Build()` takes no `Message_ProgressRange`**, which matters because that class is not constructible in this build. This is the "remove a recognized feature" primitive item 8's Phase 3 wants — the probing is done, but nothing ships using it yet.

## Primitive recognition report — `recognize_primitives` (roadmap item 8 Phase 2, closed)

The MCP tool is `recognize_primitives`; the kernel function behind it is `recognizePrimitives` (`src/primitiveReport.ts`). Phase 1 answers "what shape is this face". Phase 2 answers "what IS this solid" — per solid, the face inventory by surface type, a candidate primitive, and a **fit residual** so the caller renders the judgment. Emits no ops and reclassifies nothing; Phase 3 stays queued.

- **Two corrections to the roadmap's own plan, both from reading the code rather than trusting the text.** (1) It named `collectAllEntitySignatures` the "natural home" — but that is a flat, kind-partitioned list built by two independent top-level calls, so it carries **no solid→faces association at all**, and it sits on a latency-sensitive path (`rebindPartsAcrossOps` runs it on every interactive op-list change, twice per topology-changing op boundary). Widening it would tax every edit with a `BRepAdaptor_Surface` per face for data rebinding never reads. `primitiveReport.ts` is a **sibling walk** instead. (2) The residual cannot come from OCCT: this build has **no maximum-distance query** (`BRepExtrema_DistanceSS` constructs but never computes; `Extrema_ExtFlag_MAX` is accepted then ignored — already recorded in `entityFacts.ts`).
- **A per-FACE residual would have been worthless, which is the deeper reason the whole-solid framing is right.** A face that genuinely IS a `Geom_CylindricalSurface` has its tessellation nodes on that cylinder **by construction**, so a per-face deviation reads ~0 always and answers nothing. Measuring the whole boundary against the idealized primitive is what catches "a cylinder, but with a chamfered rim" — the case that actually matters for imported geometry.
- **`src/primitiveSdf.ts` (pure, no OCCT/THREE, unit-tested) is the residual.** Closed-form signed distance for all five primitives — a capped cylinder and a truncated cone both reduce to a revolved 2D rectangle/trapezoid in (radial, axial) coordinates. `maxDeviation` takes `Math.abs`, since a point *inside* the ideal primitive is just as much a deviation as one outside, and returns **`null` for an empty point set rather than `0`** — a residual that could not be computed must never read as a perfect fit, the same rule `checkMeshHealth` follows for a component that never closed. The cone's SDF is a max-of-half-spaces, which is exact outside the lateral extent and a conservative UNDER-estimate only in the corner regions — the safe direction, since it can never overstate a deviation.
- **`src/primitiveRecognition.ts` (pure) requires an EXACT face count per signature**, so a filleted box does not silently pass as a box: its extra cylindrical face makes the count wrong, and reporting `candidate: null` with a `{plane: 6, cylinder: 1}` inventory is the honest answer.
