Escape on the VIEW pick **cancels** (it's the primary choice), unlike `pickExportUnit`'s Escape, which deliberately falls through to mm.
- **The MCP tool `export_svg_silhouette` leads its description with the limitation** — outline only, no hidden-line removal, NOT a dimensioned technical drawing — because keeping that distinction on the record is literally what the roadmap item asked for. `view` and `direction` are mutually exclusive with `direction` winning plus a warning; an unknown view falls back to FRONT with a warning rather than throwing (the same never-fail-on-ambiguous-input convention `unit` uses). Since the closed DXF item it also takes an optional `format: "svg" | "dxf"` param (default `"svg"`) — see the "SVG/DXF import → profile ops" section above; same tool, same view/unit handling, different serializer.
- **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM** (`npm run mcp:smoke`): `cube.stl`'s FRONT view gives **exactly 4 segments and a 10.4 viewBox** (the analytically-known 10×10×10 cube plus a 2% margin — the best kind of smoke assertion); a B-rep source produces a real drawing; ISO genuinely differs from FRONT; `cube.glb` also gives exactly 4; `unit:"in"` scales the drawing by exactly 1/25.4; an unknown view name warns and falls back; and a meshio-only source is rejected with a clear error.
- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every other webview-touching feature in this codebase**: the File ▾ → Export Silhouette SVG… click → view quick-pick → unit quick-pick → save dialog flow has NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session. See "Verify a change" below.

## meshio++ integration (P3 roadmap feature)

`doc/roadmap.md`'s "meshio++ WASM integration" item (P3 #11) bundles a **third** host-side WASM module, [`@meshioplusplus/wasm`](https://github.com/loumalouomega/meshioplusplus) (MIT-licensed, maintained by this repo's own maintainer), alongside OCCT and Gmsh — to import mesh-only formats (VTK/VTU/MED/CGNS/Exodus/XDMF/Kratos MDPA) as viewable documents, and to export generated FE meshes to formats Gmsh's own writers can't produce (MED, CGNS, and XDMF, which Gmsh doesn't even recognize as an output extension).

- **Import funnels every meshio-only format through meshio++'s own STL writer — a deliberate scope decision, not a shortcut.** Rather than building a new tessellation/rendering path for ~40 formats, `src/ meshioService.ts`'s `convertToStlBoundary()` calls `convertSurface()` (stays inside meshio++'s C++ core — a volume mesh becomes its boundary, confirmed correct via a hand-built tetrahedron: converting to MED/CGNS/ Exodus/XDMF and then `convertSurface`-ing each back to STL all produced the same correct 4-facet boundary), producing plain ASCII STL bytes the webview loads through the **exact same STL loader** a native `.stl` open uses (a new `loadMeshBytes` protocol message, base64-over-postMessage — not a `data:` URL, sidestepping CSP/size-limit uncertainty). This means a meshio-imported document inherits the **entire existing mesh (Three.js) pipeline for free** — facet splitting, Parts, every mesh-legal edit op, Export, Mass Properties, Measurement — with **zero new `Viewer`/webview geometry code**. The trade-off: scalar point/cell/field data arrays in the source file are still **not preserved** at all (their NAMES are surfaced read-only, see below) — `kind: "cell"` REGION names now can become real Parts (see the next bullet), but multi-material grouping beyond that, and any non-region data, is not. Import format list is curated to the roadmap's explicitly-named formats (`src/fileRouter.ts`'s `MESHIO_FORMATS`) rather than all ~40 meshio++ supports — the extension-map table is cheap to extend later.
- **Richer meshio++ import visibility (roadmap item, closed) — region names are surfaced read-only on import, AND `kind: "cell"` regions now auto-become real, selectable/colourable Parts for the tetrahedral/triangular-boundary case.** `@meshioplusplus/wasm` bumped `^9.8.0` → `^9.9.0` (the `point_data`/`cell_data`/`field_data`/`regions` fields on its `Mesh` object, and `readMesh()`/`readMetadata()` themselves, were ALREADY present in 9.8.0 — confirmed by diffing 9.8.0's vs 9.9.0's `index.d.ts` directly, the diff is only new `*_data_components` width-metadata fields — so this gap was always closeable at the currently-pinned version; 9.9.0 was still worth taking for those refinements plus general upstream fixes).
  - **`meshioService.ts`'s new `readMeshioMetadata(bytes, format)`** calls the CHEAP `readMetadata()` (explicitly documented as loading a file's *shape* without its heavy arrays) and returns `{regions: {name,kind,numEntries}[], pointDataNames, cellDataNames, fieldDataNames}` — never throws (a malformed/unreadable file degrades to every field empty, since this is purely supplementary information that must never block or fail an import `convertToStlBoundary` would otherwise handle fine). Still used for the read-only status-line/warning path described below, alongside (not instead of) the region→Parts correlation.
  - **`meshioService.ts`'s `convertToStlBoundaryWithRegions(sourceBytes, meshioFormat)` is the mechanism this item's prior write-up identified as feasible-but-unshipped, now actually shipped and verified against the live WASM (not a synthetic mesh — `examples/MED/two-material-tets.med`, this repo's own permanent fixture, is the verification target).** `readMesh()` a full `Mesh` (not the cheap `readMetadata()`), then `extractSurface(mesh, recordParentIds=true)` (called directly on the JS `Mesh`, not through `convertSurface`'s file-to-file path) returns a boundary mesh whose `cell_data["surface:parent_cell"]` — **the exact field name, re-confirmed via a live probe against the real fixture** — gives each boundary triangle's ORIGINAL parent cell index, letting a caller correlate every boundary triangle back to whichever `kind: "cell"` region (if any) that parent cell belonged to, entirely host-side, with no geometric bbox-matching heuristic needed (unlike `gmshPartsMap.ts`'s part-preservation, which has no equivalent direct index correlation available to it). Confirmed empirically: `extractSurface` itself warns and drops the boundary mesh's OWN `regions` array (`"N named region(s) dropped — the output cells and points are newly created and have no correspondence with the input's"`) — expected and irrelevant, since the correlation uses the ORIGINAL (pre-extraction) mesh's regions, never the boundary's own.
  - **The STL bytes themselves are built DIRECTLY from `extractSurface`'s boundary mesh (its own `points`/`cells`), not from a separate `convertSurface` file-to-file call — deliberately, even though a probe on the fixture showed the two produce byte-identical triangle geometry/order.** Relying on that incidental match across two independently-callable APIs was exactly the risk this item's original write-up flagged; building the STL from the SAME mesh object the correlation ran against makes the geometry/region-index correspondence correct by construction. Facet normals are recomputed from vertex winding (never trusted from meshio), the same convention `stlParser.ts`'s `scaleStlBytes` already established.
  - **Gated to the tetrahedral/triangular case, by design — the scope this item's prior write-up said was safe to validate first.** If the extracted boundary contains anything other than plain 3-node `"triangle"` blocks (e.g. quads from a hexahedral volume, or a higher-order block), or there are no `kind: "cell"` regions at all, or the correlation resolves nothing, `convertToStlBoundaryWithRegions` falls back to the plain, unaffected `convertToStlBoundary()` with no region data — never a subtly-wrong triangulation for a cell type no fixture has validated. Quad/hex-boundary and `kind: "point"`/`"side"` region support remain unshipped, same "needs real diverse fixtures" reasoning as before, now narrowed to just that remaining slice.
  - **Bridging a region into this codebase's `Part` concept — the piece the prior write-up said needed new design work — is `src/meshioRegionParts.ts`'s `buildPartsFromMeshioRegions(stlBytes, regions)`.** Pure and WASM-free (only `stlParser.ts`'s `parseStl` + `meshFacets.ts`'s `segmentCoplanarFacets`, both already DOM/WASM-free — the latter gained an optional `triangleRegion` parameter, purely restrictive: two triangles never merge across a differing region even if coplanar, so every existing call site that omits it is byte-for-byte unaffected), so it runs IDENTICALLY host-side (`provider.ts`'s `handleMeshio`) and headlessly (`mcpTools.ts`'s `load_model`) — one implementation shared by both, not two that could drift. Ids are `node-0` (a meshio import always yields exactly one root mesh) / `node-0/face-K` (K from the SAME region-gated `segmentCoplanarFacets` the webview's own `splitMeshesIntoFacets` reproduces — see the next bullet for why that reproduction is required for the ids to resolve to anything). Degrades to `[]` (no Parts) when the region-aware segmentation would collapse to a single or `MAX_FACETS`-exceeding facet count, mirroring `buildMeshFacetGroup`'s own "keep the mesh whole" rule — same graceful-skip convention as everywhere else in this codebase.
  - **Never overwrites existing Parts — `handleMeshio`/`load_model` both auto-create only when the parts sidecar is still empty**, and persist immediately (`writeParts`) so a reopen doesn't need to recompute the correlation. `provider.ts`'s `handleMeshio` now owns the ENTIRE parts round trip for the meshio route (including sending the `"parts"` message itself) instead of the generic `sendParts()` every other route uses — because it may need to read the sidecar, decide whether to auto-create, and persist BEFORE sending, all before the webview can be told anything; both `sendParts` and `handleMeshio` now return the `Part[]` actually in effect so `provider.ts`'s `currentParts` closure variable stays in sync (previously only updated by a user-initiated `partsChanged` or `rebindPartsOnAppend` — hitting Save immediately after an auto-create, before any other edit, would otherwise flush a stale empty array over the sidecar just written).
  - **The per-triangle correlation is re-sent on EVERY open where it succeeds, not just the one that auto-created Parts** (`loadMeshBytes`'s new `regionAssignment` field: `regionNames: string[]` + base64 `Int32Array` `triangleRegionIndex`, one entry per STL triangle) — the webview's `splitMeshesIntoFacets` needs the identical region-gated segmentation on every load to reproduce the SAME `face-K` numbering the ids were computed against, or a reopen's Parts would silently stop resolving to real facets. **Only ever applied while the edit-op list is EMPTY** (`main.ts`'s `rebuildMeshModel`, gated via a module-level `importedRegionInfo`) — it indexes `pristineMesh`'s ORIGINAL triangle order, which a topology-changing mesh edit (boolean/hole/primitive-add) invalidates; the instant the user pushes a first op, region-aware splitting turns off for that and all subsequent rebuilds (re-enabled on Undo back to empty, since the cached pristine geometry is untouched). A part whose ids stop resolving after that point degrades exactly like any other stale-id case already documented in the Parts section above — no new failure mode, just the existing one applying here too.
  - **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM** (`npm run mcp:smoke`, `examples/MED/two-material-tets.med`): `load_model` auto-creates exactly 2 Parts (`"MaterialA"`→`node-0/face-{0,1,2}`, `"MaterialB"`→`node-0/face-{3,4,5}`, matching the fixture's known two-tet boundary), `get_state` reflects them with no facet id double-assigned across Parts, a second `load_model` call on the same file does NOT re-create/duplicate anything (existing-parts gate holds), and `generate_mesh` on the same source still succeeds unchanged (purely additive, no regression to the geometry-only path). MCP's `load_model` warnings distinguish a correlated region (`"...(see get_state's parts)"`) from an uncorrelated one (`"...— not preserved as Parts/geometry"`) rather than always claiming the latter.
- **Must load via a DYNAMIC `import()`, not gmsh-wasm's static-import pattern — verified against the live package, a genuine gotcha.** `@meshioplusplus/wasm`'s `package.json` is `"type": "module"`, `"main": "./src/index.mjs"`, with **no `"exports"` map and no `require` condition at all** — unlike `@loumalouomega/gmsh-wasm`, which is dual CJS/ESM (a real `dist/gmsh-core.cjs` Node's "require" condition resolves to, letting a static `import` compile to a working `require()` in this CJS-bundled extension host). `require("@meshioplusplus/wasm")` here throws `ERR_REQUIRE_ESM`. `meshioService.ts`'s `getMeshio()` instead does `const { loadMeshioPlusPlus } = await import("@meshioplusplus/wasm")` inside its lazy-init function — esbuild's `"cjs"` output format leaves a dynamic `import()` of an **external** module as a literal runtime `import()` call (not converted to `require()`), and Node's CJS modules are permitted to `await import(...)` an ESM package at runtime. Must stay `external` in `esbuild.mjs` for this reason (nothing statically imports its `.wasm`, so no `wasmPathPlugin` change was needed either).
- **Always loads with `{ variant: "seq" }` — never `"auto"`, the package's own default — for the same class of risk this repo already hit once with gmsh's worker pool.** The package's `resolveVariant()` picks the threaded (pthread) build whenever `typeof crossOriginIsolated === "undefined"`, which is **unconditionally true under Node** — so `"auto"` would always pick the threaded build here, eagerly spawning ~8 `worker_threads.Worker`s at load. Forcing `"seq"` avoids that risk entirely; the sequential build is plenty fast for this pipeline's file sizes. `.vscodeignore` accordingly carves out only the sequential variant's four files (`package.json`, `src/index.mjs`, `dist/meshioplusplus_wasm.mjs`, `dist/meshioplusplus_wasm.wasm`) — the threaded variant's files would be dead weight given `"seq"` is always forced, and skipping them roughly halves this package's footprint in the packaged `.vsix`.
- **Console output already goes through `console.log`/`console.error` by default (confirmed against the live package's built glue) — like OCCT, unlike gmsh 0.2.0's raw-fd writes — so `mcpServer.ts`'s existing top-of-file stdout rebinding already covers it.** No new suppression code was needed, unlike gmsh's `print`/`printErr` override.
- **Not copied to `dist/` via `copyWasm()`, unlike OCCT/gmsh — a real, verified deviation from the established WASM-packaging convention.** OCCT/gmsh pass `wasmBinary` explicitly to bypass the module's own file lookup, so their `.wasm` files can live anywhere and get copied to `dist/` for a predictable path. `@meshioplusplus/wasm`'s loader instead auto-locates its own `.wasm` sibling via `import.meta.url` — confirmed: its raw glue derives `scriptDirectory` from `import.meta.url` under Node and reads its own `.wasm` relative to that — which only resolves correctly if the package's files stay in place under `node_modules/`, never copied elsewhere. So this package is handled entirely by the `.vscodeignore` carve-out above; `esbuild.mjs`'s `copyWasm()` needed no new entry.
- **The export bridge (`exportViaMeshio()`) takes `generateMesh()`'s own MSH 4.1 `mshText` directly — requires `@meshioplusplus/wasm` ≥ 9.8.0, and its history is a live-WASM discovery trail worth keeping.** Against 9.4.1, feeding MSH 4.1 into `convert(..., {inFormat: "gmsh"})` threw `"Gmsh $Entities not supported by the C++ reader"`, forcing a legacy MSH 2.2 detour (`exportMeshFormat(..., "msh2")`); 9.7.0 parses `$Entities` natively **and resolves physical-group membership from it**, so a 4.1 read yields named `regions` (one per part) that 2.2 never carried — re-verified: a 2.2 read of the same grouped mesh yields NO regions, so 4.1 input is load-bearing for group preservation, not just a convenience. **Every format, MED included, is now a single, uniform `convert()` call — the MED-specific two-step is gone as of 9.8.0.** Under 9.7.0, direct `convert(→ med)` threw `"MED: gmsh physical groups handled by Python fallback"` on gmsh's always-attached `"gmsh:physical"` cell_data, and separately rejected 4.1's one-block-per-entity layout (`"MED files cannot have two sections of the same cell type"` — a meshed unit box arrives as 27 blocks), forcing a `readMesh()` → `merge([mesh])` → rebuild-without-`cell_data` → `writeMesh(→ med)` workaround. 9.8.0's `write_med` now bridges `gmsh:physical` to MED families and consolidates same-type blocks natively in C++, so that entire workaround was deleted — re-verified end-to-end against the live 9.8.0 WASM with a real Gmsh-generated 4.1 file (not just a unit test): a box with `MyVolume`/`MySurface` physical groups round-tripped both region names through the plain `convert()` path with no special-casing. CGNS/XDMF/VTK never needed this — plain `convert()` already worked directly on 4.1 text. Full write-up (including the XDMF `.h5`-companion handling) lives in `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "The meshio++ bridge" section.
- **The CGNS pure-surface (2D-dimension) read-back gap is also closed in 9.8.0 — re-verified against the live WASM, not assumed from the changelog.** Before 9.8.0, CGNS was a private tetrahedra-only encoding whose writer emitted only the first `tetra` block it found, so every 2D-dimension FE-mesh generate (which has no tetra block at all) wrote a file this same WASM build's own reader rejected (`"HDF5: missing dataset ' data'"`) — volume (3D) meshes were unaffected, since they always have a `tetra`/`hexahedron` block. 9.8.0 rewrote CGNS to a genuine CGNS/SIDS-compliant subset (one section per cell block, not "first tetra block only"), and re-verifying against a real Gmsh 2D-dimension generate (fed through the same pipeline `exportViaMeshio()` uses) confirms the round trip is now clean. `doc/gmsh-integration.md`'s "Known limitations" no longer lists this.
- **`resolveMeshInputHeadless` in `mcpTools.ts` gives meshio-only formats a genuinely NEW headless capability, beyond what `.obj`/`.ply`/`.gltf` get** — since `convertToStlBoundary()` runs entirely host-side (no webview needed, unlike those three, which only the webview's Three.js loaders can parse), `load_model`/`generate_mesh`/`export_mesh` all work headlessly for VTK/MED/CGNS/Exodus/XDMF/MDPA sources, verified end-to-end via `npm run mcp:smoke` against the real WASM (not mocked): a hand-built tetrahedron `.vtk` file is loaded, meshed, and exported to MED, CGNS (3D — the pure-2D CGNS gap doesn't apply), and XDMF (confirming the `.h5` companion is written and its embedded reference correctly rewritten).
- **MDPA import (via meshio++'s native reader) and MDPA export (the hand-written `mdpaWriter.ts`, unchanged by this feature) are two entirely independent code paths that happen to share a file extension** — a considered-and-rejected idea, not a gap: meshio++'s WASM MDPA reader/writer can't represent Kratos `Properties`/`Table`/`Geometries`/ `Constraints` blocks (the `MdpaInfo` side-channel isn't forwarded through the WASM binding), so it could never replace the hand-written exporter's richer SubModelPart/property support without regressing it.
- **Colour-by-scalar-field for meshio++ imports (roadmap item, closed)** turns the point/cell data array NAMES already surfaced (`readMeshioMetadata`) into an actual on-model colour ramp — the piece that item's own text originally scoped out. Webview-only, no MCP tool (same "genuinely a display feature, no headless equivalent" precedent as Display Modes/Markup/Measurement).
  - **`meshioService.ts`'s `readMeshioFieldValues(sourceBytes, meshioFormat, fieldName, kind)`** is the on-demand (not eager — a field's values only cross postMessage once the user picks it) sibling of `convertToStlBoundaryWithRegions`, reusing the identical `readMesh()` → `extractSurface(mesh, recordParentIds=true)` call sequence so its output is correlated onto the SAME boundary triangle soup (verified deterministic: identical bytes always produce byte-identical boundary geometry/order). **`kind: "point"` needed zero correlation math — a genuinely surprising, verified-not-assumed simplification**: probed with a deliberately interior point (excluded from every boundary face) carrying a distinctive value, and `extractSurface`'s own `boundary.point_data[field]` came back with that value correctly DROPPED and the remaining values correctly re-indexed to the real boundary points, not naively truncated — so `readMeshioFieldValues` just reads `boundary.point_data[field]` directly and expands per-POINT to per-CORNER via each triangle's own point indices. `kind: "cell"` reuses the exact `cell_data["surface:parent_cell"]` provenance array region-correlation already established: the original mesh's `cell_data[field]` (one array per original cell block) is flattened block-major (same indexing `Region.entries` uses) and each boundary triangle's parent-cell value is broadcast to its 3 corners. Both verified end-to-end against `examples/MED/two-material-tets.med` (a throwaway Node script bundling the real function against the live WASM, the same discipline used to verify `convertToStlBoundaryWithRegions` before mcp:smoke coverage existed) — `Temperature` (point) and `cell_tags` (cell) both came back correctly correlated. Returns `null` (never throws) for a missing field, a non-scalar (multi-component) field, or a non-pure-triangle boundary.
  - **The overlay is a scene sibling of `model`, built from `pristineMesh`'s own UNSPLIT geometry — not the model's own per-facet split sub-meshes.** This is deliberate, not the obvious approach: `splitMeshesIntoFacets` regroups triangles by coplanarity into new per-facet geometries whose vertex order bears no relation to the original file's triangle order the host's per-corner `values` are correlated against, so recolouring those sub-meshes in place would need re-deriving the same triangle→facet mapping this design sidesteps entirely. `src/webview/geometryBuilder.ts`'s `buildColorFieldOverlay(basePositions, valuesB64, min, max)` takes `pristineMesh`'s **live** `Float32Array` position attribute (already in the browser from the original STL load — only the much smaller per-corner scalar values travel over postMessage) and builds an unlit (`MeshBasicMaterial({vertexColors: true})`) mesh, same triangle-soup-shading-artifact reasoning as `buildFEMesh`.
  - **`src/webview/colorMap.ts`** is a small, pure viridis-like ramp (the standard 5-stop control-point approximation, linearly interpolated) — no colour-science dependency. `valueToColor(value, min, max)` degenerates to the ramp's midpoint for a constant field (`min === max`) rather than dividing by zero. Pure/DOM-free, unit-tested.
  - **`Viewer.setColorFieldOverlay()`/`setColorFieldOverlayVisible()`** mirror `setMeshOverlay`'s dispose/replace pattern exactly, but composability with `meshOverlay` needed a real fix: both overlays independently want to hide the model's shaded faces while shown, and the old code had `setMeshOverlay` unconditionally call `setModelFacesVisible(this.meshOverlay === null)` — a second overlay toggling on/off would stomp on whichever state the first one had set. `refreshModelFacesVisibility()` now centralizes the decision (hidden whenever ANY overlay exists AND is visible) and both `setMeshOverlay`/`setMeshOverlayVisible`/`setColorFieldOverlay` call it instead of setting visibility directly — a real, narrowly-scoped refactor required by adding the second overlay, not scope creep. `setModel()` clears the colour-field overlay unconditionally (same "stale overlay from now-replaced geometry" rule `meshOverlay`/`worstElementsOverlay` already follow), which is also what makes ANY edit correctly invalidate an active colour-field view — `rebuildMeshModel()`'s call to `setModel()` does this for free, with `main.ts`'s `resetColorFieldSelection()` additionally resetting the `<select>`/legend UI to match.
  - **Gated to the pristine, unedited geometry only, same reasoning as `importedRegionInfo`** — `readMeshioFieldValues`' correlation indexes the ORIGINAL triangle order, which a topology-changing mesh edit invalidates. `main.ts` resets the selection (and, via `Viewer.setModel()`, the overlay) on every `rebuildMeshModel()` call, i.e. on every edit — a colour-field view must be deliberately re-picked after an edit, never silently reapplied to now-different geometry.
  - **UI**: a new "Colour by field" group in `#view-controls`' Appearance area (`src/viewerDom.ts`), hidden (`hidden` attribute) unless the source declares ≥1 point/cell data array name — populated from `loadMeshBytes.meshioMetadata` (already-shipped, read-only visibility), values fetched via a new `colorFieldRequest`/`colorFieldResult`/`colorFieldError` protocol round trip (same requestId + stale-response-guard idiom as `massPropertiesRequest`). The legend (`#vc-colorfield-legend`) shows a CSS gradient bar (`colorMap.ts`'s `viridisCssGradientStops()`) plus plain-formatted (no length-unit suffix — a scalar field isn't length-dimensioned) min/max text.

## meshio++ dependency currency + OpenFOAM import (roadmap Tier 1, closed)

The roadmap's "Dependency currency" tier closed in one pass: `@meshioplusplus/wasm` bumped **9.9.0 → 10.9.0** (13+ published versions of drift; dependabot had already moved manifest/lockfile to 10.0.0 in PR #45, but `npm install` had never been re-run — `node_modules` still held 9.9.0), and one of the bump's "unlocks" (OpenFOAM import) was taken in the same change rather than left as a separate item.

- **Every documented meshio++ invariant was re-verified against the installed 10.9.0 artifact — all held, zero code changes needed for the loader itself.** Still `"type": "module"` / `"main": "./src/index.mjs"` with no `"exports"` map and no `require` condition (the dynamic `import()` stays mandatory); `resolveVariant()` still picks the threaded build whenever `crossOriginIsolated` is undefined (= always under Node), so `{ variant: "seq" }` stays load-bearing (`parallelBackend()` confirmed live to report `"seq"`); the glue still self-locates its `.wasm` via exactly one `import.meta.url`; console routing is still exactly one `console.log` + one `console.error` with ZERO raw fd writes (the gmsh-wasm 0.2.0 stdout-corruption class did not recur); the `.vscodeignore` four-file carve-out still matches the v10.9.0 `files` array byte-for-byte. The JS-facing API surface CAD-Preview calls (`loadMeshioPlusPlus(moduleOverrides?, {variant})`, `readMesh`, `readMetadata`, `extractSurface(mesh, recordParentIds?)`, `convertSurface`, `convert`, `FS.*`, and the `cell_data["surface:parent_cell"]` provenance name) is unchanged; ~40 new bindings are purely additive.
- **Packaged size: seq `.wasm` went 6,074,949 B → 6,981,814 B (+0.9 MB, ~15%)** — cgnslib 4.5.2 joined HDF5/netcdf as pinned cross-compiled deps at v9.22.0, plus whatever 10.x added. Recorded here because it ships inside the `.vsix`.
- **THE defect-shaped reason this ranked first, verified live:** v9.13.0/v9.14.0 fixed the C++ MDPA reader, which until then THREW (`"MDPA: non-sequential node ids are not supported by the C++ reader"`) on any deck whose node ids weren't exactly `1..n` in file order — gapped ids being routine in real Kratos decks. Now permanent regression coverage: `examples/MDPA/gapped-ids.mdpa` (ids 7/23/41/100, element id 5) + an mcp-smoke block asserting `load_model` routes it, its metadata warning surfaces the preserved-id data names, and `generate_mesh` works. Two fixture-authoring findings worth keeping: the MDPA reader accepts NO top-level comment syntax (hence the fixture README), and Kratos entity names must be the core-registered ids (`Element3D4N` — what our own `mdpaWriter.ts` emits), NOT geometry-class names (`"Tetrahedra3D4N"` → `"MDPA: unknown Kratos entity name"`). A deck WITH a `Table` block is still rejected by name (`"MDPA: a top-level Table block is not supported by the C++ reader (set ReadOptions::mLenien[t]…)"`) — we do not opt into `readMeshSelective`'s lenient mode, so `doc/file-formats.md`'s statement stays true.
- **v9.14.0's id preservation surfaces as NEW metadata names**: an MDPA import now reports `pointDataNames: ["mdpa:id"]`, `cellDataNames: ["gmsh:physical", "mdpa:id"]` — additive, factual, and they flow into the existing colour-by-field selector like any other scalar field. No code special-cases them.
- **OpenFOAM polyMesh import (`.foam`)** — the one unlock taken now; everything else from the bump's unlocks list (decimate for `MAX_HEALABLE_TRIANGLES`, convertCells region-provenance chaining, gradient/transient feeds) remains queued as its own future items. meshio++ gained an OpenFOAM reader/writer (v9.20.0+); upstream's format doc records `.foam` as the extension and the reader resolves `<marker's parent>/constant/polyMesh/{points,faces,owner,neighbour,boundary}` via `std::filesystem`.
  - **A `.foam` marker is NOT a mesh** — so foam cannot ride the bytes-in funnel every other meshio format uses. `meshioService.ts`'s new **`convertFoamCaseToStlBoundary(markerPath)`** takes the marker's REAL filesystem PATH (a plain string, crossing kernel-worker IPC untouched), locates `<parent>/constant/polyMesh` (then bare `<parent>/polyMesh` as fallback, mirroring upstream's `_resolve_polymesh`), stages marker + siblings into MEMFS under `/foamcase/**` with per-level `FS.mkdir`, converts, and cleans up in `finally`. A missing directory throws a clear application error BEFORE any WASM work (cannot corrupt the singleton); conversion faults go through `wrapMeshioFault` as usual.
  - **Why it hand-builds the STL instead of calling `m.convertSurface` — a real live-WASM finding that also exposed a pre-existing shared-pipeline limitation:** `convertSurface` linearizes higher-ORDER cells (tri6→tri3) but does NOT split multi-node boundary FACES, and meshio++'s STL writer then silently emits ZERO facets for a quad-only boundary (verified against a single-hex case: `extractSurface` returns one `quad` block, `convertSurface`'s STL is literally `solid endsolid`). Hex-dominant meshes are THE typical OpenFOAM content, so the converter instead does `readMesh` → `extractSurface(mesh, false)` → fan-triangulate every boundary face about its first vertex (private `boundaryTrianglesToAsciiStl` helper, handling rectangular + CSR-polygon blocks, recomputing normals from winding, skipping degenerate triangles). **Known pre-existing gap recorded, deliberately not fixed here**: the same quad-drops-silently behavior presumably affects any all-hex VTK/MED volume imported through the SHARED `convertToStlBoundary()` path today — no fixture exercises it, and changing the shared path was out of this item's scope. A defect-shaped follow-up if hex-mesh imports ever matter.
  - **Geometry-only by construction**: meshio++'s OpenFOAM reader reads no point/cell/field data (time-directory field files like `U`/`p`/`T` aren't read) and carries patch names through a C++ side-channel struct (`OpenFoamInfo`) NOT surfaced to JS — so there are no regions for the Parts auto-create machinery to consume and no metadata to read. Call sites skip both rather than stage the case twice for a guaranteed-empty answer: `provider.ts`'s `handleMeshio` passes `EMPTY_MESHIO_METADATA` and takes the foam branch; `mcpTools.ts`'s `load_model` returns early with a geometry-only warning; `resolveMeshInputHeadless` routes to the path-based converter (so `generate_mesh`/`export_mesh` work headlessly).
  - **Wiring**: the standard 4-touch-point pattern — `Pipeline` interface key in `mcpTools.ts`, typed dispatch entry in `kernelWorker.ts`, `callKernel` object-literal entry in `kernelClient.ts`. Routing: `foam` extension → `{strategy:"meshio", format:"openfoam"}` in `fileRouter.ts` (+ `MESHIO_FORMATS`, + `CadFormat` union, + `package.json` customEditors selector, + the open-dialog filter list); `exportTargets.ts`'s `Record<CadFormat,…>` tables gained exhaustive `openfoam` entries (import-only — never offered by `exportTargetsFor`). `compare_models`' rejection message now builds its meshio-format list FROM `MESHIO_FORMATS` after the hardcoded copy drifted stale on this exact change.
  - **Fixture**: `examples/OpenFOAM/hex-case/` (empty `case.foam` marker + `constant/polyMesh/*`), generated BY meshio++'s own writer so the committed files are exactly what the library produces; boundary = 6 quads → 12 fan-triangulated triangles. mcp-smoke copies the whole case dir, asserts `load_model` routes/format/warning and `generate_mesh` produces a real mesh ("12 facets in solid 0" visible in the worker log).

### Format coverage widened to match the used-vs-pinned gap (roadmap item, Phase 1 of "Adopt the unused meshio++ capability surface", closed)

A dependabot bump took `@meshioplusplus/wasm` 10.0.0 → 10.14.0 (npm's `latest`) with **zero new capability adopted** — probing the installed WASM directly found ~63 exposed functions, 42 readers, 45 writers against this codebase's actual usage of 5 functions, 7 import formats, 3 export-via-meshio formats. This closes `doc/roadmap.md`'s Tier 2 item 6 (previously stale — it still said "pins `^9.9.0`... npm's latest is 9.22.0") and is Phase 1 of a 4-phase plan for the rest of the surface (health/quality, field/data tooling, mesh operations — see that roadmap item for the remaining phases).

- **A confirmed, fixed import defect: this extension could not re-open the `.xdmf` it itself exports.** Every meshio entry point (`convertToStlBoundary`/`readMeshioMetadata`/`convertToStlBoundaryWithRegions`/`readMeshioFieldValues`) used to write ONLY a source's own bytes into MEMFS, under a synthetic renamed path (`/in.<format>`) — so a multi-file source could never be read back. Verified live: writing an XDMF (which always has an `.h5` sibling under the default `"HDF"` data format) and reading the `.xdmf` back with the `.h5` deleted throws `HDF5: could not open file /model.h5`. **`stageMeshioSource`/`unstageMeshioSource`** (new, private, `meshioService.ts`) fix this — every function gained optional trailing `sourceName?`/`companions?: readonly MeshioCompanion[]` params (additive; every pre-existing call site omitting them keeps today's exact byte-for-byte behavior), and stages the primary under its real basename (or the old synthetic path, if `sourceName` is omitted) plus every companion under ITS own real/referenced basename, flat in MEMFS root — verified this needs no subdirectory juggling for either convention a multi-file format uses (XDMF's content-driven `<DataItem>` reference, or a stem-convention format's basename-swap sibling lookup like tetgen `.node`↔`.ele`), since HDF5/stem resolution is relative to the READING file's own directory and a flat root IS that directory for everything staged this way.
- **`src/meshioCompanions.ts`** (new, pure, unit-tested) is the shared "which sibling basenames to look for" half — `extractXdmfHdfReferences(xdmfText)` (a plain regex scan for `Format="HDF"` `<DataItem>` elements, no XML parser — this vitest config has none, the same constraint `svgImport.ts` documents) and `stemCompanionCandidates(basename, format)` (extension-swap for tetgen/triangle/ensight, wired in the registry for a future companion-format addition, not yet used by any registered format). `meshioCompanionCandidates()` dispatches between the two. The disk-I/O half is per-consumer, mirroring `resolveGltfBuffers`' existing precedent for external glTF `.bin` buffers exactly: `provider.ts`'s `resolveMeshioCompanionsFor` (`vscode.workspace.fs`) and `mcpTools.ts`'s `resolveMeshioCompanions` (`node:fs`) both read whatever candidate exists beside the source, silently skipping a missing one — self-contained XDMF (`"XML"`/`"Binary"` data formats) needs no companion and is unaffected either way.
- **A SECOND, independent, pre-existing meshio++ 10.14.0 defect was found while verifying the fix above, and is genuinely NOT fixable in this codebase.** `generate_mesh` on a re-imported `.xdmf` this extension itself exported fails with `"XDMF: unknown mixed topology index"` — reproduced with a bare hand-built vertex+line+triangle+tetra mesh, zero CAD-Preview code involved, so this is meshio++'s own writer/reader pairing failing to round-trip its own "Mixed" topology output. Since `generate_mesh` always forces Gmsh's `Mesh.SaveAll=1` (see the Meshing section above), which includes 0-D point elements alongside the volume mesh, **almost every realistic XDMF export from this extension hits this bug on reimport.** Proven strictly later-stage than, not a symptom of, the companion defect: the SAME Mixed-topology fixture with its `.h5` deleted fails with the ORIGINAL `HDF5: could not open file` error instead — the companion lookup runs first and succeeds; only then does topology parsing fail. `load_model` on such a file still succeeds (metadata/region reading degrades gracefully, per every meshio entry point's pre-existing "never throws" contract); `generate_mesh` throws the Mixed-topology error, which `scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs` asserts EXACTLY (via `callTolerant`, not `call`) rather than silently tolerating — a future meshio++ release fixing this flips the assertion from "expected error" to "unexpected success", which is the intended signal to revisit.
- **5 new import formats close a genuine export/import asymmetry**: `gmsh` (`.msh`/`.msh2`), `abaqus` (`.inp`), `unv`, `su2`, `medit` (`.mesh`) — this codebase's own FE Mesh panel already WROTE these via Gmsh's writer but had no way to re-OPEN any of them. Each was verified round-trippable against the live WASM (export a real tetrahedralized `bull.stp`, then meshio++'s `readMesh()` the result back) before being added to `CadFormat`/`MESHIO_FORMATS`/`EXTENSION_MAP`. **Two formats Gmsh also writes were tried and REJECTED after the identical check**: `.bdf` (Nastran) round-trips through meshio++'s own reader as `"Not a meshio++-C++ Nastran file"`, and `.off` as `"Expected the first line to be 'OFF'"` — Gmsh's writer output for both isn't shaped the way meshio++'s reader for the same nominal format expects. Neither is claimed as an import format; this is a narrower finding than "meshio++ can't read Nastran/OFF" (it may read a DIFFERENT tool's output correctly — no fixture exists here to check that).
  - **Live-caught, NOT-fixed-here finding: re-meshing a REIMPORTED `.msh` can fail too, for an unrelated reason.** `generate_mesh` on `bull.stp`'s own re-imported `.msh` output failed with Gmsh's own `classifySurfaces: Wrong topology of boundary mesh for parametrization` — a SECOND meshing pass over the re-extracted boundary of an ALREADY-tetrahedralized volume (far more, and far more irregular, small triangles than the original CAD tessellation) is a meaningfully harder case for Gmsh's STL-reclassification algorithm than a normal mesh import, and not something this codebase's own routing/staging code caused or can fix. `load_model` succeeding — genuinely re-opening/viewing what was exported — is the claim this item makes; re-meshing an already-meshed volume's boundary a second time is a stronger, separate claim NOT verified to work and not asserted in `scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs`.
  - **`.msh`/`.inp` are ambiguous extensions** — also ANSYS/FreeFem (`.msh`) and ANSYS APDL (`.inp`). `src/fileRouter.ts`'s `AMBIGUOUS_MESHIO_EXTENSIONS` (a `Map<extension, caveat message>`, not a sniff mechanism — content-sniffing into the alternate formats was considered and explicitly rejected: no real ANSYS/FreeFem-authored fixture exists to verify such a read against, so claiming that support would be an unverified promise) surfaces the caveat as a `status` message (`provider.ts`) / `warnings` entry (`mcpTools.ts`) on open, naming the assumed format.
- **8 new export-ONLY formats reach meshio++ writers Gmsh's own `gmsh.write()` genuinely has no code path for at all** — `vtu`, `hmf` (HDF Mesh Format), `avsucd` (AVS UCD), `mphtxt` (COMSOL), `netgen`, `flac3d`, `wkt` (Well-Known Text), `flux`. Verified via a real two-step live-WASM probe, not from meshio++'s format table alone: (1) `gmsh.write("/out.<ext>")` for every candidate threw Gmsh's own `"gmshWrite: Unknown output file format"` (never a shape-specific rejection — confirming these are additive, not duplicate paths to something Gmsh could already produce); (2) meshio++'s OWN writer for the format accepts THIS pipeline's actual `generateMesh()`-shaped MSH 4.1 input (`Mesh.SaveAll`'s 0-D vertex cells included) and round-trips back through meshio++'s own reader with matching point/cell counts. **A meaningful number of superficially-plausible candidates FAILED step (2) on this specific input shape and are deliberately excluded**: `vtp` (`"PolyData cannot hold 'tetra' cells"`), `vti` (needs a regular hexahedral lattice, a tet mesh isn't one), `h5m`/`ugrid`/`tecplot`/`ansys`/`ansysinp`/`freefem` (all reject the 0-D vertex cells `Mesh.SaveAll` always includes), `dex`/`ip`/`mff`/`mfm` (each either lost all cell connectivity on round-trip or failed outright — not genuinely usable for a document-preview export target despite writing *something*).
- **`meshExportFormats.ts` gained a `via: "gmsh" | "mdpa" | "meshio"` discriminator**, replacing the hardcoded `msg.target === "med" || "cgns" || "xdmf"` triple that used to be independently duplicated (with its own copy of the `.h5`-companion-write-and-reference-rewrite logic) in BOTH `provider.ts`'s `meshingExport` handler and `mcpTools.ts`'s `exportMeshTool` — both now `switch`/branch on `meshExportFormat(id)?.via === "meshio"`, so a future meshio-routed format addition needs no second dispatch-site change. `msh`/`msh2`/`geoUnrolled`/`mdpaElements`/`mdpaGeometries` stay id-special-cased regardless of `via` (each calls a different pipeline function with its own companion handling), documented as such in the registry's own doc comment.
- **`kernelIpc.ts` hardened alongside this work — a real, verified hazard found while auditing the IPC boundary for meshio's own native array type, not part of the original defect hunt.** `ctorNameOf`'s `default:` branch used to silently accept ANY unrecognized `ArrayBufferView` (a `Float64Array` — meshio++'s own native type for `Mesh.points`/`point_data`/`cell_data` — most concretely) and tag it `"Uint8Array"`, the WRONG element width: correct bytes, silently wrong length on unmarshal, never a thrown error. Now throws a clear message naming the unrecognized constructor. `Float64Array`/`Int8Array`/`Uint16Array`/`Int16Array` are explicitly added to the tagged-type union. Separately, non-finite numbers (`NaN`/`±Infinity` — legitimate outputs of a `dataInfo`/`dataIntegrate`-shaped future function, e.g. a field's min/max when every value is itself `NaN`) used to silently degrade to `null` across the `JSON.stringify`-based wire format, the identical class of gap `EncodedUndefined` was already fixed for; a new `EncodedNumber` tag closes it the same way, both as an object property and an array element. `kernelIpc.test.ts` gained 7 new cases (Float64Array round-trip, nested-alongside-a-different-typed-array, the loud throw, NaN/Infinity as both property and element, and confirming an ordinary finite number stays untouched — no wrapper object introduced).
- **Verification**: `npm run mcp:smoke` covers every change above end-to-end against the real WASM — the XDMF companion fix, the Mixed-topology follow-on finding (asserted exactly, not tolerated blindly), all 5 new import formats round-tripped through `load_model`, and all 8 new export-only writers. `npx vitest run` covers the new pure modules. The import/colour-by-field panels and the 6 new `contributes.customEditors.selector` patterns (`.msh`/`.msh2`/`.inp`/`.unv`/`.su2`/`.mesh`) still need an F5 pass, per the standing webview caveat — genuinely unverified here.


### meshio++ capability surface, phase A — health + quality (roadmap item)

`check_mesh_health` reported only what `meshTopology.ts` can compute from sorted edge keys. It now also carries meshio++'s own surface diagnostics, per connected component.

- **`inconsistentPairCount` is the genuinely new signal, and the reason this phase exists.** `meshTopology.ts` keys every edge through `edgeKey(a, b)`, which **sorts the pair** — orientation is discarded before counting, so two adjacent triangles wound oppositely still register as a clean manifold edge. Pinned by a new fixture, `examples/STL/flipped-winding-tet.stl` (a tetrahedron with one face reversed): it reports `freeEdgeCount 0, nonManifoldEdgeCount 0` — the existing analyzer calls it **perfectly clean** — alongside `inconsistentPairCount: 3`. `mcp:smoke` asserts that contrast directly, since it is the one signal with no existing implementation to cross-check against.
- **The overlapping fields are the cross-check, not redundancy.** `cube.stl` → 0 and `holed-cube.stl` → 3 boundary edges from BOTH implementations; a disagreement would be a finding.
- **No MEMFS staging — the `Mesh` is built in JS.** Unlike every other entry point in `meshioService.ts`, `analyzeMeshioSurfaces` takes an already-parsed welded soup and hands meshio++ a plain object literal (verified to work), so there is no file to write, no `readMesh`, and no re-serialization — and it therefore runs **per component**, matching `MeshHealthReport`'s existing shape instead of forcing a whole-mesh sibling field.
- **Quality is normalized minimum angle (`min_angle / 60`), NOT scaled-Jacobian, and that correction came from a real miss.** The roadmap proposed scaled-Jacobian; verified against the live WASM, `quality:scaled_jacobian` — with `skewness`, `warpage` and both dihedral metrics — is **NaN for every triangle cell**, since those apply to volume cells only. Every mesh this path analyzes is a surface, so it would have summarized nothing. The NaN filter is what surfaced this rather than silently producing a garbage mean: `summarizeQuality` compares with `<` and accumulates, so one NaN poisons the mean and lands in bucket 0 via its clamp. Normalized min-angle matches that function's documented "1 is perfect, ≤0 is degenerate" convention and its [0,1] bucketing — a cube's 45-45-90 triangles give exactly `45/60 = 0.75`, which the smoke test pins.
- **Never throws**, per this file's convention for supplementary information: a component meshio++ cannot analyze yields `null` and the OCCT-derived fields are unaffected. The catch still calls `resetMeshioIfAbort`.

**A real pre-existing defect found while verifying this, fixed across all four kernel services.** A live `apply_edit_ops` abort surfaced as `WebAssembly.Table.get(): invalid index 9893720 into function table` — which the shared `isXWasmAbort` vocabulary did **not** match, because its `table index` alternative has the words in the opposite order. The failure was silent and compounding: undetected abort → singleton never reset → no "kernel has been reset" message → `scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs`'s `callWithCleanRetry`, which keys on exactly that phrase, could not recover either. Adding `function table` to all four regexes took `mcp:smoke` from a hard failure at 229 checks to a clean pass at 336.

### meshio++ capability surface, phase B — field/data tooling (roadmap item)

The colour-by-field picker listed names only, so a multi-component array failed **after** the click — having read the whole file and run a full `readMesh` + `extractSurface` — and the dropdown snapped back to "None" behind a message naming three possible causes.

- **`readMeshioDataInfo` is a SEPARATE entry point, not a widening of `readMeshioMetadata`.** That one deliberately uses the cheap `readMetadata` (a file's shape without its heavy arrays) and both `provider.ts` and `mcpTools.ts` call it on every document open. `dataInfo` needs a real `readMesh`, so it is called **only when the source actually declares point/cell arrays** — a document with no fields pays nothing, and one that has them would have paid the same read on the first click anyway.
- **The picker now disables a non-colourable array up front**, labelled with its component count and a tooltip explaining that a colour ramp maps one scalar per entity. A colourable one gets its real value range as a tooltip before any values are fetched. `arrays` is optional on the protocol message, so an older payload still renders exactly as before.
- **Two real defects fixed, both found by reading the code rather than by a failure:**
  1. `readMeshioFieldValues` returned a bare `null` shared by "not found", "not a plain scalar", "boundary isn't pure triangles" and "the WASM aborted" — so `provider.ts` reconstituted a three-way **guess** and showed it as fact. It now returns a discriminated `MeshioFieldFailure`, and `describeMeshioFieldFailure` maps each reason to one sentence naming one cause.
  2. The `colorFieldError` path reset only the `<select>`, never the overlay or legend — so after a failed pick the viewport kept showing the **previous** field's colours and its legend range while the dropdown read "None". It now calls `resetColorFieldSelection()`.
- Also corrected: `readMeshioFieldValues`' `min`/`max` scan did not skip `NaN`, unlike meshio++'s own `dataInfo`, which is documented as finite-only — one NaN poisoned both bounds and made every colour in the legend meaningless. An all-NaN array is now a named failure rather than an `Infinity` range.
- New fixture `examples/MED/vector-field-tets.med` (generated by meshio++'s own `gradient`) carries a genuine 3-component `Temperature:gradient` beside two scalars, so the disabled path has something real to exercise.

### meshio++ capability surface, phase C — mesh operations (roadmap item, closes the item)

**The headline: the triangle-only gate in `convertToStlBoundaryWithRegions` is closed.** A hexahedral volume's boundary is QUADS, so that function used to bail to the plain path and silently lose region→Parts correlation for every hex/quad-boundary mesh. `convertCells(boundary, "simplexify", true)` splits them into triangles AND — verified against the live WASM — preserves the `surface:parent_cell` provenance array the correlation depends on. The triangulation runs only when something is actually non-triangular, so the already-triangular path is byte-for-byte what it was. New fixture `examples/MED/two-region-hexes.med` (two hexes, two named cell regions, `quadx10` boundary) now auto-creates both Parts on import, asserted in `mcp:smoke`; before this it produced none.

**`transform_mesh` — ONE MCP tool for the whole operation family**, not one per operation, mirroring `run_parametric_script`'s "declarative document, one tool, per-step report" precedent: `clean`/`decimate`/`smooth`/`subdivide`/`refine`/`agglomerate`/`convertCells`.

- **Built over the per-op functions, NOT meshio++'s own `runPipeline`** — a deliberate deviation from the roadmap's wording. `runPipeline` takes an undocumented settings object (it rejects the obvious `mesh` key), so driving it would mean reverse-engineering a schema for no gain; the per-op calls are simple positional functions whose signatures are verified. The tool's own op list is the declarative layer the roadmap actually wanted.
- **A step that cannot run is reported and skipped, never silent**, matching this codebase's op-replay convention. Pinned live: `decimate` on a hex volume correctly refuses ("decimate operates on surface meshes (extract_surface first)") with `applied: false` and its reason, while the following `clean` still runs.
- Unlike this file's read-side diagnostics it **throws** rather than degrading — it produces a file, and silently writing an unmodified mesh would be worse than an error.
- meshio-readable sources only; a B-rep returns `supported: false` naming `apply_edit_ops` as the right tool.

**`decimate` is a sound answer to `meshHeal.ts`'s 50 000-triangle refusal, measured rather than assumed** — on `examples/STL/large-sphere-100k.stl`: 99 904 → 24 976 triangles in 228 ms, with the boundary-edge count preserved **exactly** (460 before, 460 after), so it does not damage watertightness. The 460 are a seam artifact of the fixture itself, established by measuring the input rather than inferring it from the output. Wiring that into `promote_mesh_to_brep`'s ceiling is left as a follow-up; the capability is verified and reachable via `transform_mesh` today.

### meshio++ 10.14.0 → 10.20.2 — bump, GiD format, provenance tagging (roadmap item, closed)

Six releases and 47 upstream commits of drift. **Every documented invariant in this file held; the loader needed zero changes.** Re-verified against the **published npm tarball** (not just the git checkout at `/home/vicente/src/meshioplusplus`, which can be ahead or behind), with 10.14.0 and 10.20.2 loaded side by side in one Node process:

- Still `"type": "module"` / `"main": "./src/index.mjs"` with **no `"exports"` map and no `require` condition** → the dynamic `import()` stays mandatory. `resolveVariant()` is **byte-identical** and still returns `'mt'` whenever `crossOriginIsolated` is undefined (= always under Node), so `{ variant: "seq" }` stays load-bearing, not vestigial (`parallelBackend()` confirmed live to report `"seq"`).
- Glue stdio unchanged: exactly one `console.log` + one `console.error`, **zero raw `writeSync`** (`var out=console.log.bind(console)`, `var err=console.error.bind(console)`) — `mcpServer.ts`'s stdout rebinding still covers it; the gmsh-wasm 0.2.0 stdout-corruption class did not recur. Still exactly three `import.meta.url` self-locations → the package must keep shipping as real files under `node_modules` (the `.vscodeignore` four-file carve-out, whose filenames all still exist in the new `files` array), never copied into `dist/`.
- **API is purely additive**: `src/index.mjs` +30 lines, `index.d.ts` +28, module key count 70 → 77 — exactly the 7 new provenance methods, nothing renamed or removed. Everything this codebase calls is unchanged, including `extractSurface(mesh, true)`'s `cell_data["surface:parent_cell"]` provenance name the region→Parts correlation depends on.
- `.wasm` grows 7,097,018 → 7,329,362 B (**+227 KB**, +3.3%) — gid/gidpost + provenance. A real, accepted packaging cost.
- **Both known defects this repo asserts on are UNCHANGED, so no test churn:** XDMF Mixed-topology reimport still fails with the exact string `"XDMF: unknown mixed topology index"` (`scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs`'s `callTolerant` assertion still holds), and a quad-only boundary → STL still silently writes **0 facets** (the pre-existing gap `convertFoamCaseToStlBoundary` works around with its own fan-triangulation).
- **Two risks investigated and cleared, not assumed.** v10.20.0's *"Breaking: default `.vtu`/`.vtp` write is now zlib-compressed"* applies to the CLI/wheels only — the **WASM build already had zlib**, confirmed by 10.14.0's own `.vtu` output already carrying `compressor="vtkZLibDataCompressor"`. And v10.20.1's WASM provenance-leak fix lands for free: 10.14.0 predates default-on provenance (10.17.0) entirely, so this codebase **jumps straight past the buggy 10.15–10.20.0 window**. The only observable output change is a text-format credit line (`written by meshio++ (C++ core)` → `Written by meshio++ v10.20.2`); MED/CGNS/XDMF byte counts are identical, and nothing in the repo asserts on that text.

#### GiD postprocess (`gid`) — import + export, the first compound-extension route

- **`routeFile` now matches the LONGEST registered suffix first** (`fileRouter.ts`). GiD's `.post.msh` ends in `.msh`, which is registered to Gmsh, so the previous last-dot-only lookup resolved every GiD file to a Gmsh parse that then failed — **precisely the bug meshio++ itself had to fix in its own `resolve_format` in 10.18.0**. The walk is behavior-preserving for every single-extension file (pinned by tests: `my.backup.stl`, `v1.2.3.step`, trailing dots), and now scans the **basename** rather than the whole path, so a directory component's dot can no longer be mistaken for the file's extension. `matchExtension()` is factored out and exported so an extension-keyed lookup elsewhere resolves the SAME key the route did — `ambiguityCaveatFor()` is built on it, which is what stops a `.post.msh` inheriting `.msh`'s "assumed to be a Gmsh mesh" caveat (two call sites in `provider.ts`/`mcpTools.ts` previously re-derived the extension themselves, last-dot-only).
- **Only the ascii flavour and only the geometry file are routed.** `.post.res` (the results sibling) is deliberately not independently openable — it is not a mesh; it's staged alongside the primary by `meshioCompanions.ts`'s existing stem-convention machinery (a `gid: ["msh", "res"]` entry, which works unmodified because that helper splits on the last dot: `beam.post.msh` → stem `beam.post` + ext `msh` → `beam.post.res`). The `binary`/`hdf5` flavours (`.post.bin`/`.post.h5`) are readable by meshio++ but **not claimed**, since no fixture verifies either.
- **Export: companion handling became registry-driven, which is what made GiD cheap.** `meshExportFormat` gained `companion?: { extension, linkage }`. `linkage` distinguishes two genuinely different write paths, not just a filename: `"referenced"` (XDMF's `.h5`, named inside the primary's own `<DataItem>` elements → rewrite the reference to the real saved name) vs `"sibling"` (GiD's `.post.res`, found by stem convention → write beside it and leave the primary **byte-for-byte untouched**). Both export call sites (`provider.ts`'s `meshingExport`, `mcpTools.ts`'s `exportMeshTool`) previously carried their own hardcoded `.h5`/XDMF copy of this; both now read the registry, so a third companion-bearing format needs no dispatch-site change. `companionSaveName()` (pure, unit-tested) strips the format's **full, possibly compound** extension — a last-segment strip would turn a `beam.post.msh` save into `beam.post.post.res`.
- **`exportViaMeshio` no longer derives its MEMFS path from the format id** (`/out.${outMeshioFormat}` was wrong twice over for GiD: the format name is `gid` while the extension is `post.msh`, and the companion is `.post.res` rather than `.h5`). Both come from the registry now.
- **GiD clears a bar XDMF does not.** Fed this pipeline's actual `Mesh.SaveAll`-shaped MSH 4.1 input (vertex + line + triangle + tetra), it writes the pair AND reads back through meshio++'s own reader with every block and count intact (`vertex×3, line×2, triangle×2, tetra×2`) — where XDMF's Mixed-topology encoding cannot be re-read at all. Verified live before any code was written, then again through the real pipeline in `mcp:smoke`.
- Fixture: `examples/GiD/two-tets.post.msh` + `.post.res` (+ a README), generated **by meshio++'s own writer** from `examples/MED/two-material-tets.med`, mirroring the `examples/OpenFOAM/hex-case/` precedent.

#### Provenance tagging — adopted, with a coverage split that must not be overstated

`exportViaMeshio` opens an explicit `withProvenance(1, …)` scope (the callback form, which closes the scope even if the body throws) recording the **true source document** — path + `CadFormat`, threaded from both call sites — rather than the meaningless `/in.msh` intermediate.

**It is a complete no-op for MED/CGNS/XDMF — the three formats this bridge originally existed for.** Determined by inspecting raw output bytes, not just `readProvenance()` (which additionally cannot scan HDF5-backed slots even where a block exists):

| Embeds provenance | Embeds nothing |
| --- | --- |
| `vtu`, `avsucd`, `mphtxt`, `netgen`, `flac3d`, `flux`, `gid` | `med`, `cgns`, `xdmf`, `hmf`, `wkt` |

Passing a source is harmless everywhere and useful for the other seven, but describing this as blanket coverage would be false. `mcp:smoke` pins **both halves** (present for `vtu`/`gid`, absent for `med`/`cgns`), so a future meshio++ release closing the gap surfaces as a failing assertion rather than an unnoticed silent improvement — the same "a fix flips the assertion" discipline the XDMF Mixed-topology check already uses.

The 7 new WASM methods (`withProvenance`/`provenanceBegin`/`provenanceEnd`/`provenanceNote`/`provenanceSetSource`/`provenanceSetTarget`/`readProvenance`) are otherwise recorded as available, not spent speculatively — `provenanceNote` and `readProvenance` have no caller yet.

#### Verification

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` 1256/1256 (23 new cases: `routeFile`'s longest-suffix behavior including the behavior-preserving single-extension cases, `matchExtension`, `ambiguityCaveatFor`, GiD stem companions, the registry's companion contract, and `companionSaveName`'s compound-extension trap); `npm run build` clean; `npm run mcp:smoke` passes with new live-WASM coverage for the GiD export→`.post.res`-sibling→**separate `load_model`**→`generate_mesh` round trip, the committed fixture, the absent-Gmsh-caveat check, and both halves of the provenance split. **F5-only caveat, unchanged and stated plainly:** the FE Mesh panel's new GiD entry in the export `<select>` and opening a `.post.msh` interactively have NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session.
## Performance regression harness (roadmap item, closed)

`npm run perf` (`scripts/perf/run.mjs`) closes the gap `doc/roadmap.md`'s item flagged: `elapsedMs` was computed and reported (`generate_mesh`'s MCP response) but never compared against anything, so nothing would catch a large regression in load, tessellation, or meshing. Deliberately opt-in, not wired into `npm test`/CI — machine-to-machine timing variance makes a hard-gated perf assertion in ordinary CI noisy; this is meant to be run by hand around a change to something on the "load, tessellation, or meshing" hot path (explicitly the case for the "configurable tessellation quality" item landing beside this one, per the roadmap's own dependency note — both have since closed, so that item is no longer in `doc/roadmap.md`; it is referenced here by name rather than by number, since roadmap numbering is renumbered on every close).

- **Mirrors `mcp:smoke`'s own harness shape** (a hand-rolled newline-delimited JSON-RPC stdio client spawning the real `dist/mcp-server.js`, real OCCT+Gmsh WASM, no mocking) rather than sharing a module with it — both are short, self-contained, one-off scripts, matching this codebase's existing convention of not factoring shared infrastructure between `scripts/*` entries.
- **Four graded STEP fixtures from `examples/STP/`** — `angle1.stp` (~21 KB, small), `bull.stp` (~110 KB, medium — the same fixture used everywhere else in this codebase), `piston.stp` (~361 KB, large), `turbine.stp` (~2.3 MB, xlarge) — spanning roughly two orders of magnitude, the same "graded model sizes" framing SketchForge's own STL-import benchmark used. **`daratech.stp`, the more obvious "large" pick by file size (333 KB), was tried first and rejected**: it reliably throws a genuine Gmsh `"PLC Error: A segment and a facet intersect at point"` 3D-meshing robustness failure — a real geometry/kernel limitation orthogonal to what this harness measures, not a bug in the harness. `block.stp` was similarly rejected as the "small" fixture for the identical reason, both confirmed live against the real WASM, not assumed.
- **`generate_mesh` is called with an explicit bbox-derived `sizeMax` (`diagonal / 20`, `sizeMin: 0`) per fixture — never the `MeshOptions` default's unbounded "auto" sentinel.** Auto sizing derives element size purely from surface curvature, which on some of these real-world engineering models produces a mesh fine enough to trip the same PLC-error class of failure the rejected fixtures hit unconditionally — confirmed live: `piston.stp`/`turbine.stp` both fail at the auto sentinel and both succeed at the explicit bbox-derived size (`diagonal/20`, the exact default `syncMeshSizeSeed()` already seeds the interactive panel with, so this isn't an arbitrary choice). Benchmarking a mesh operation that would fail outright at default settings would make the harness itself unreliable, which defeats its purpose.
- **A warm-up call (`load_model` + `generate_mesh` on a throwaway `angle1.stp` copy) runs before any measured fixture**, paying the one-time OCCT/Gmsh WASM singleton-initialization cost outside every reported number — otherwise whichever fixture happened to run first would look artificially slow, not because it's actually larger/harder, purely because it drew the short straw on cold-start cost.
- **Reports wall-clock ms (measured client-side, around the `tools/call` round trip) and RSS delta** (`/proc/<pid>/status`'s `VmRSS`, before/after each stage) for both `load_model` (B-rep read + tessellate) and `generate_mesh` (FE meshing) per fixture. RSS reading degrades to `null` off Linux (no `/proc`) rather than throwing — same "optional signal, never blocks the run" convention `render_snapshot`'s Playwright-availability probe already established elsewhere in this codebase. Client-side wall-clock (not `generate_mesh`'s own self-reported `elapsedMs`) is the number compared against the baseline, since it captures true end-to-end latency including JSON-RPC/stdio framing overhead — a more honest regression signal than a number computed only from inside one tool call.
- **Baseline comparison uses a deliberately generous 3× tolerance** (`scripts/perf/baseline.json`, checked into git, captured once on this dev environment) — not a tight assertion. Machine variance is real and this script has no CI job; 3× is comfortably below the "10× regression nothing would catch" failure mode the roadmap item was filed to close, while tolerant enough not to cry wolf on ordinary noise. A flagged stage prints a warning either way; the run only exits non-zero when `PERF_STRICT=1` is set, so merely having this script in the repo can never break an unrelated `npm test`/build. `--update-baseline` regenerates `baseline.json` from the current run's numbers — use after a deliberate, reviewed perf-affecting change, never routinely.
- **No product code changed** — this item is a new standalone script + one `package.json` entry + a `.vscodeignore` carve-out (`scripts/perf/**`, mirroring the existing `scripts/mcp-smoke/**`/`scripts/screenshots/**` entries so it never ships in the packaged `.vsix`). `load_model`'s MCP response deliberately was NOT extended with its own `elapsedMs` field (unlike `generate_mesh`, which already had one) — the harness's own client-side wall-clock timing already covers both operations uniformly, and adding a second, tool-response-embedded timing signal alongside it would be redundant surface for no benefit.

## Base-shape caching and incremental replay (roadmap item, closed)

Every `editsChanged` used to call `loadModel()` → `handleBRep()` → `loadBRep()` (`src/provider.ts`/`src/occtService.ts`), which re-read the source bytes, re-ran `readShape` (a real STEP/IGES/BREP parse — geometric/topological reconstruction, not a cheap operation), replayed the **entire** op list from scratch, and only then re-tessellated — on every single edit, including one that just appended a single new op to an already-long list. **Scoped to the interactive extension's per-edit hot path ONLY** — `mcpTools.ts`/the MCP server deliberately stay on the original, unmodified `loadBRep`, stateless per call as CLAUDE.md's MCP section already documents; introducing cross-call state for a long-lived headless process serving arbitrary/many file paths over its lifetime is a different, harder problem this item's own scope doesn't need to solve.

- **`occtService.ts`'s new `loadBRepCached(extensionPath, bytes, format, ops, previous)`** is purely ADDITIVE — `loadBRep` itself is untouched, byte-for-byte, and remains what `mcpTools.ts` calls. `loadBRepCached` reuses the parsed base shape across calls whenever `bytes`+`format` are unchanged from `previous` (`bytesEqual`, a plain byte-for-byte loop — no hashing, since a full compare is cheap at realistic CAD-file sizes and has zero collision risk), and separately reuses `previous`'s fully-replayed shape as the starting point when `ops` is a pure append of `previous.ops` (`JSON.stringify` equality per element, the SAME op-list-equality idiom the external-change-reconciliation feature already established) — replaying only the NEW suffix ops instead of the whole list. Any other relationship (undo, `remove_edit_op` at a non-last index, redo down a different branch, a parametric-variable change re-resolving every op's numeric fields) falls back to a full replay of `ops` from the (still-reused, if bytes didn't change) base shape — deliberately NOT attempting to diff/patch the op list itself, which would need the same unwind/rewind reasoning `entityRebind.ts`'s id-rebinding already solved once for a DIFFERENT problem (id continuity, not shape reuse) and would be disproportionate complexity here.
- **Tessellation is NOT cached, and needs no caching machinery of its own** — `tessellateByGroup`/`extractEdges`/`extractVertices` were ALREADY fully self-contained (each builds and cleans up its own internal OCCT handle list, taking no external `cleanup` array parameter — confirmed by reading them, not assumed) before this feature, so the ONLY handles `loadBRep`'s original top-level `cleanup` array ever held were from `readShape` (the reader + base shape) and `applyEditsBRep` (the op-replay chain). This made splitting the cache into exactly two persistent arrays (`baseCleanup`, `opsCleanup`) the natural, minimal-surface design — tessellation just re-runs fresh every call on whatever `shape` (cached or freshly replayed) comes out, since re-tessellating unavoidably-changed geometry was never the cost this item targeted.
- **Cache validity across a WASM-abort-triggered `resetOcct()` is checked by reference identity, not a counter.** `BRepCacheEntry.ocInstance` stores the resolved `oc` module object from `getOcct()` at cache-creation time; `loadBRepCached` compares it (`===`) against the CURRENT `getOcct()` result before treating `previous` as reusable. Since `resetOcct()` nulls `_ocPromise` and the next `getOcct()` call creates a genuinely NEW resolved module object, a cache built against a since-aborted-and-reset WASM instance is correctly detected as stale with no new exported state from `occtService.ts` beyond what `getOcct`/`resetOcct` already provide.
- **On failure, `loadBRepCached` deliberately does NOT attempt to `.delete()` any handle it touched during that call** — an abort can leave the WASM heap in an undefined state, so calling back into it to free handles is not obviously safe (unlike discarding a stale-but-HEALTHY cache, which the function does dispose properly via `disposeBRepCache`). The caller (`provider.ts`'s `handleBRep`) is responsible for dropping its held cache reference (`cache.current = undefined`, never reused as `previous` again) on any error — the orphaned OCCT module, already unreachable via `getOcct()` once `resetOcct()` ran, becomes eligible for ordinary JS garbage collection once nothing (including the discarded cache entry) still references it.
- **Reused fields are aliased by reference, not copied — a real bug this caught during verification, not a hypothetical.** When base and/or op-replay reuse happens, the returned entry's `baseShape`/`baseCleanup`/`shape`/`opsCleanup` are the EXACT SAME objects `previous` held. A first draft of the verification script (see below) called `disposeBRepCache()` on what it assumed was a superseded "old" entry right after a reuse — and crashed OCCT with `"Cannot pass deleted object as a pointer of type TopoDS_Shape"`, because the "old" and "new" entries shared the same live handles, so disposing one destroyed the other's still-in-use state. Fixed in the test (never dispose anything but the single, latest entry you're currently holding — exactly what `provider.ts`'s real `cache.current = nextCache` reassignment-with-no-disposal pattern already does correctly) and called out explicitly in `loadBRepCached`'s own doc comment so a future caller doesn't repeat it.
- **`provider.ts`'s wiring**: a per-document `const brepCache: { current: BRepCacheEntry | undefined } = { current: undefined };` inside `resolveCustomEditor` (a plain mutable holder, not a closure `let` — `handleBRep` is a class method, not itself nested in the closure, so it needs an object it can mutate in place rather than reassign a variable it doesn't own). `handleBRep` gained a `cache` parameter, calls `loadBRepCached` instead of `loadBRep`, writes the returned entry back into `cache.current` on success, and clears it to `undefined` in its `catch` (never disposing there — see the failure-path rule above). `webviewPanel.onDidDispose` disposes whatever `brepCache.current` holds at close time, alongside the existing watcher-disposal cleanup already registered there.
- **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM via a throwaway script** (esbuild-bundling `occtService.ts` standalone — it has zero `vscode` dependency, confirmed by reading its whole import chain — then driving `loadBRepCached` directly; not committed, per this codebase's established "not every change needs new permanent test infrastructure; some verification is legitimately manual/one-off" convention already applied to `provider.ts` elsewhere). Confirmed: a no-op reuse call returns the identical `baseShape`/`shape` object; an appended op reuses the base and replays only the new op, producing geometry identical (face count, cross-checked against a from-scratch cold replay) to a full uncached replay; a non-append change (op removed) correctly discards only the stale replay while still reusing the base, again producing identical geometry to a cold replay; a genuine source-bytes change correctly discards and re-parses everything. **Measured speedup on `examples/STP/turbine.stp` (2.3 MB, the largest fixture `npm run perf` also uses): a cold single-op replay took ~6.9 s; the identical op applied via the cache (base already parsed) took ~0.2 s — roughly 35×.** On the smaller `bull.stp` fixture the effect is still clear (~254 ms cold vs. ~27 ms cached) but proportionally smaller, since WASM/tessellation overhead dominates more at that scale — confirming the win scales with file size/complexity, exactly where the roadmap item said the cost was concentrated.
- **`npm run mcp:smoke` intentionally does NOT exercise this path** — it drives `mcpTools.ts`'s tools, which call the unmodified `loadBRep`, not `loadBRepCached`. It still passing after this change confirms the untouched code path (and every other feature depending on it) has zero regression; it says nothing about the new caching logic itself, which is why the standalone verification above exists.

## Configurable tessellation quality (roadmap item, closed)

`src/meshExtract.ts`'s `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh_2(shape, 0.1, false, 0.5, false)` used to be a hardcoded, non-configurable linear/angular deflection pair — a 3 m machine frame and a 5 mm screw got an identical chord tolerance, with no way to trade fidelity for speed. `src/tessellationQuality.ts` (new, pure, vscode-free, unit-tested) is the single source of truth for three presets — `draft`/`standard`/`fine` — exposed as a new cross-document `cadPreview.tessellationQuality` VS Code setting.

- **Live-WASM probing resolved every open question the roadmap item itself named, and found one it didn't expect.** `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh_3` (the `IMeshTools_Parameters`-taking overload the item hoped might be reachable) IS bound as a constructor, but throws `"Cannot construct BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh_3 due to unbound types: 21IMeshTools_P..."` the moment it's actually invoked — `IMeshTools_Parameters` itself is genuinely unbound in this WASM build, the same "constructor exists, argument type doesn't" pattern this codebase has hit before (e.g. the BREP-reader `Message_ProgressRange` trap). `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh_1`'s 0-arg constructor + `.Perform()`/`.SetShape()` methods also exist (a builder-pattern alternative) but weren't pursued — `_2` already covers everything needed and is what every existing call site already used. **`isInParallel: true` does NOT hang and is genuinely faster** — verified on a real multi-face STEP model (`bull.stp`) and confirmed on a much larger one (`turbine.stp`: 1014ms serial vs. 448ms parallel, roughly 2.3×) — so it's now passed unconditionally, not exposed as a setting (there's no reason to ever run serially).
- **`"standard"` is byte-for-byte the original hardcoded constants (linear 0.1, angular 0.5 rad)** and stays the default — a document with no explicit choice tessellates identically to every previous version of this extension. `"draft"` (linear 0.2, angular 0.6) and `"fine"` (linear 0.03, angular 0.15) are new, opt-in alternatives; the exact numbers are this codebase's own choice (roughly 2× coarser / 3× finer than standard), not SketchForge's values copied verbatim — SketchForge's own "standard" tier is finer than this codebase's existing default, and silently changing what "standard" produces would be a real behavior change for every already-open document, not just a new option. **Verified end-to-end against the live WASM, not just that the numbers are internally ordered:** tessellating `bull.stp` at the three presets produced 848 / 1756 / 9022 triangles respectively (draft < standard < fine, confirmed via the real triangle-index-buffer lengths `loadBRepCached` returns).
- **Edge discretization (`edgeEnumeration.ts`'s `GCPnts_UniformDeflection_2`, `EDGE_DEFLECTION = 0.1`) is deliberately NOT varied by quality — investigated and rejected, not an oversight.** `enumerateEdges` is the SAME shared enumerator `occtOperations.ts`'s `collectEdges` uses to resolve an `edge-N` id back to a live edge, and its `positions.length >= 6` filter (an edge that fails to discretize to ≥2 points is dropped as an entity entirely) is deflection-dependent. If the DISPLAY path (`extractEdges`) and the OP-RESOLUTION path (`collectEdges`) ever used different deflections, they could disagree on which edges pass that filter — silently repointing `edge-N` ids between the two paths, exactly the drift hazard the closed "one shared edge enumerator" item (see above) exists to prevent. Correctly varying edge deflection by quality would mean threading it through every `applyEditsBRep`/`collectEdges` call site across the codebase (`occtOperations.ts`, `entityFacts.ts`, `modelDiffHost.ts`, `massProperties.ts`, `gmshPartsMap.ts`, …) for a comparatively small win — edge polylines are a small fraction of the geometry data next to face triangles, which scale with the SQUARE of resolution and dominate both rendering cost and interactive responsiveness. `tessellateByGroup`'s new `quality` parameter is the only configurable one; `discretizeEdge`'s deflection stays a fixed constant, and its own doc comment now states this rationale explicitly so it isn't "fixed" again by a future well-meaning pass.
- **Threading is minimal and entirely additive-with-defaults.** `tessellateByGroup(oc, shape, quality: TessellationParams = TESSELLATION_PRESETS.standard)`, `loadBRep(..., quality = TESSELLATION_PRESETS.standard)`, `loadBRepCached(..., quality = TESSELLATION_PRESETS.standard)` — every EXISTING call site (`mcpTools.ts`'s three `loadBRep` calls, `renderService.ts`'s `render_snapshot`) compiles and behaves unchanged with no edits needed, since omitting the new trailing parameter is identical to passing `"standard"` explicitly.
- **Interaction with the closed base-shape-caching feature (see above) needed no new logic at all — verified, not just assumed compatible by design.** Since tessellation was ALREADY excluded from what `loadBRepCached` caches (it re-runs `tessellateByGroup`/`extractEdges`/`extractVertices` fresh on every call regardless of base/op-replay reuse), a quality change between two calls with identical bytes+ops is invisible to the cache's reuse decisions — `baseShape`/`shape` stay reused (confirmed via object-identity checks in the same throwaway verification script used above) while the triangle output genuinely differs at the new density.
