The item shipped across two passes, and the seam between them is a lesson worth recording: the first pass (commit `f038970` "Improve operations") landed `draft`, `addEdgeSlot`, and the `guide?: boolean` field **host-side only**, leaving the two opCatalog buttons rendering an EMPTY params area (no `renderParams` case, and the previous form's stale `draftReader` then fed `buildOpForPanel("draft", oldDraft)` → default-case error → silent preview failure) and the `guide` flag validated-but-inert. Any new op must ship its panel form + apply wiring + `buildOpForPanel` case + `tintForPanelOp` entry in the SAME change — a catalog button without a form is a dead surface no test caught because the form switch has no `default` case.

- **`shell().join()`** (`"arc"|"intersection"|"tangent"`) exposes the verified 9-arg `MakeThickSolidByJoin`'s join mode. **Inscribed-vs-circumscribed polygons**: `circumscribed?: boolean` on `addPrism`/`addPolygonProfile` (apothem vs circumradius; `regularPolygonPoints` computes corners differently, `validateEditOp` carries it like `shell`'s `join`).
- **Distance-angle chamfer**: `chamfer` gained asymmetric `distance2` + `face` (via `Add_3`) and distance-angle `angleDeg` + `face` (via `AddDA`), with a refactored edge→adjacent-face adjacency helper (`buildEdgeFaceAdjacency` in `edgeEnumeration.ts`) that `addEdgeSlot` also reuses for its slot-face orientation.
- **`draft`** (B-rep only, `TOPOLOGY_CHANGING_OPS`): the op model, validation, panel form, and MCP wiring are all correct, **but the kernel path is BROKEN in this build — probed to exhaustion, never guesswork.** The binding trail: the plain `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle` ctor is UNBOUND ("no accessible constructor"); `_1` wants >1 params (thrown at 1, 2, AND 3 args); **`_2(shape)` is the working ctor**. `Add` is **5-arg `(face, gp_Dir, angleRad, gp_Pln, flag)`** — deduced from embind's own type errors ("Expected gp_Dir, got gp_Pln" at pos 1, "Cannot pass true as gp_Pln" at pos 3), confirmed live. `gp_Pln_2(Ax3)` is the 1-arg plane form (`gp_Pln_3` takes `(Pnt, Dir)` — the first pass called `gp_Pln_3(ax3)` with 1 arg, an instant BindingError, then swallowed it as "draft builder threw"; `npm run mcp:smoke` caught it because the first pass never smoke-tested the op). With the VERIFIED call shapes, `Add` succeeds deterministically — but **`Build()` — the call that actually computes the tapered shape — RELIABLY throws an un-decodable OCCT failure (a `Standard_Failure` with no message, surfacing as `undefined`) on real geometry, across 3 fresh processes**. This is the same "green in the manifest is necessary but not sufficient" class as `ShapeFix_Shape`/`Interface_Static.CVal`/`BRepExtrema_DistanceSS`-max. The response: `draftFaces` uses the verified bindings (so a future OCCT build that fixes `Build()` needs no code change), `Build`'s throw degrades to an honest graceful skip naming the kernel limitation (never a silent no-op), `OP_PARAM_DOCS` warns agents, and `mcp:smoke` pins `applied:false` + the diagnostic while a neighboring box still applies. Improvements over FluidCAD's hardcoded min-Z neutral plane that survive for when the kernel works: the op's `planePoint`/`planeNormal` are OPTIONAL (omitted = the drafted face's OWN plane via `facePlane`), and the webview form leaves Point/Normal at 0 to mean "per-face plane". Panel form mirrors `shell` (faces from the Surf selection); preview tint deliberately neutral (fillet/chamfer precedent).
- **`addEdgeSlot`** (B-rep only): a stadium face around an existing edge — width across, length = edge length + width — appended under "Sketches". The slot face's in-plane orientation comes from the edge's adjacent face's plane when one resolves (fallback `[0,0,1]`). Verified: the appended face's area is exactly `(edge length + width) × width` via `inspect`.
- **Construction geometry (`guide`)** — the flag is carried through `validateEditOp` by the WRAPPER (not the per-kind core cases): non-boolean guide or a guide on a non-guide kind rejects the WHOLE op; the 16-kind set is the exported `GUIDE_KINDS` constant (`editOps.ts`), so the core cases never need touching. Enforcement is **mark, don't regroup**: no new tessellation group, no id renumbering, no `collectFaces`/free-face lockstep change. `applyEditsBRep` threads an optional `GuideCollector` (same optional-trailing-param pattern as `outcomes`) that `addProfile`/`addWireframePrimitive` populate with the built handles when `op.guide` — a polyline records the WIRE's per-segment edges (explored), not the wire. `loadBRep`/`loadBRepCached` identity-match the handles against the `face-N`/`edge-N`/`point-N` enumerations via the established `HashCode`-bucket + `IsSame` lookup (`collectGuideIds`) and return `guideIds: string[]` on `BRepResult` → the `geometry` message → the webview dims them (a `Viewer.GUIDE_DIM = 0.35` multiplicand in `highlightGroup()`'s `baseOpacity` composition for faces; direct safe opacity writes for lines/sprites, which nothing else writes opacity to) and refuses them as operands for exactly the 6 profile-resolution ops (`extrude`/`revolve`/`sweep`/`loft`/`addSurfaceFromLines`/`addVolumeFromSurfaces`) — enforced BOTH host-side (the collector is in scope during the same fold, so a guide operand fails the op with a diagnostic) and webview-side (`buildOpForPanel` + the two `onBuild*` callbacks). Guides stay pickable/parts-assignable/measurable. The panel checkbox is ONE generic field appended after the switch for guide kinds, read by `applyButtonDraft`'s wrapped reader (the single seam Apply AND preview both flow through) and attached to the op in a thin `buildOpForPanel` wrapper — 16 forms did NOT need 16 edits.
- **Midplane/midaxis references**: `mirror`/`splitByPlane`/`section` gained `midplaneFaces?: [faceId, faceId]` (XOR with inline `planePoint`+`planeNormal`; both-planes-must-be-planar-and-parallel, resolved via `facePlane`, midpoint computed from the plane-offset average so sign-flipped normals cancel correctly) and `patternCircular` gained `midaxisOf?: [faceId|edgeId, faceId|edgeId]` (two cylindrical faces via `faceSurfaceInfo`'s cylinder branch, or two parallel straight edges; mixed pairs refuse). Non-planar/non-parallel/unresolved → graceful skip with a diagnostic. **Mesh path refuses the refs** (`meshEdits.applyOneOpMesh` checks the fields before any dispatch — a mesh has no analytic faces; inline vectors remain the mesh route). This deliberately does NOT reopen item 7's plane-N cross-entity-reference problem — faces are already a referenceable operand class. The Planes panel gained a "Midplane…" creator (two `<select>`s of saved planes, computed client-side with the SAME sign-align/average math `set_plane`'s `midplaneOf` applies, stored RESOLVED per the planes sidecar's convention, `derivedFrom: "midplane planeA–planeB"`); pickers refresh from `renderPlanesList` so they never offer a stale plane.
- **`set_plane`'s `midplaneOf`** (from the first pass, MCP-only until now): two saved plane ids → the halfway plane with `derivedFrom: "midplane A–B"`, refusing non-parallel planes and unknown ids.
- **A real pre-existing MCP defect the new smoke block caught, fixed in both tools that shared it**: `apply_edit_ops`/`run_parametric_script` computed `applied = accepted − notApplied` where `notApplied` counted skips across the ENTIRE replayed stack — so a PERSISTED op that skips on every replay (exactly what the new guide-refusal scenario persists: a refused guide-profile extrude sits in the sidecar, skipping on every load) silently zeroed every LATER call's applied count even when that call's own op applied fine. Fixed by slicing the replay's outcome list to the current call's ops (`result.opOutcomes.slice(current.ops.length)`); `opOutcomeWarnings` deliberately keeps covering the whole stack — surfacing persisted-but-skipped ops is its documented job. Verified live via the debug sequence in this session (extrusion demonstrably present in the returned model while `applied` read 0).
- **Verified**: `editOps.test.ts` (+XOR/id-shape/guide cases incl. `GUIDE_KINDS.size === 16` and all-guide-kinds-are-BREP_ONLY), `meshEdits.test.ts` (+2 mesh-path refusal cases), and a live `mcp:smoke` block: `draft` pinned as an honest kernel-limitation skip (`applied:false` + the diagnostic while a neighboring box still applies and adds exactly its 1000 units — the analytic-taper assertion was written first and re-scoped after the live probe showed `Build()` broken), `addEdgeSlot`'s sketch-face area is analytic, guide enforcement (extrude on a guide face → `applied:0` + /guide/ diagnostic; the non-guide control extrudes), `midplaneFaces`/`midaxisOf` cross-checked against their inline-vector equivalents on twin copies, non-parallel midaxis degrades with a diagnostic, and `set_plane` `midplaneOf` happy path + both rejections. `describe_capabilities`' `OP_PARAM_DOCS` strings now document the optional ref fields and the draft kernel note.
- **Verification gap as it stood when this shipped**: `npm test` could not execute — vitest 4 needs Node ≥ 20 and the shell had Node 18 — so the new unit tests were validated by `tsc` alone and left to CI, and `edits-edit.png` was left for a later regen. **Both are resolved and the limitation is stale**: `doc/development.md`'s Flatpak recipe (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 …/code`) runs VS Code's own Electron as **Node 24**, which vitest 4 and Playwright both accept, so the whole toolchain — `tsc`, `vitest`, `esbuild.mjs`, `mcp:smoke`, `docs:screenshots` — runs in this environment. The screenshots were regenerated on 2026-09-02. Panel forms / guide dimming / the planes picker still carry the standing F5-only caveat, which no toolchain fixes.

## Thin-walled sweep-family features — `.thin()` (roadmap item 8, first cut)

`extrude`/`revolve`/`sweep`/`loft` could only produce a **filled** solid. All four now accept an optional `thin` (total wall thickness) plus `thinOuter` (how much of that wall sits outside the profile boundary, `[0, thin]`, default 0 = entirely inward). This is the roadmap's own recommended first cut of item 8 "because it applies to five existing ops at once rather than adding a sixth"; the rest of item 8 (`wrap()`, `rib()`, loft guides/end conditions, up-to-face terminators, `.pick(regions)`) stays queued.

- **Thin is a PROFILE TRANSFORMATION, not a fifth builder.** `MakePrism_1`/`MakeRevol_1`/`MakePipe_1`/`ThruSections` are unchanged; `thinFaceFor` swaps the resolved profile face for an annular band face before the builder runs (`buildFeatureSolid`, `occtOperations.ts`). That is what makes one implementation cover the whole family. The `MakeThickSolidByJoin` path was deliberately NOT used even though it works (probed: exactly 392 on a 500 box with the top cap open) — it needs per-kind cap identification, has no dual-offset form, and an empty closing list yields a plain offset solid rather than a hollow. It stays what it already is, the `shell` op.
- **OCCT API, verified against the live WASM — this is the first use of `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset` anywhere in this codebase** (it was green in the manifest and never called, the exact situation this file's own "green is necessary but not sufficient" rule exists for): `new BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset_3(wire, GeomAbs_JoinType.GeomAbs_Arc, isOpenResult)` — exactly 3 ctor args, `_1` being the 0-arg default and `_4` unbound — then `.Perform(offset, 0)` and `.Shape()`. The band face is `MakeFace_15(outerWire, true)` + `.Add(TopoDS.Wire_1(innerWire.Reversed()))`.
- **An over-large offset does NOT report failure — it silently returns an EMPTY COMPOUND.** Verified: offsetting a 10×10 square inward by 5, 6 or 20 each returns a non-null `TopAbs_COMPOUND` with zero edges rather than a wire, with no throw and no `IsDone` false. `thinProfileWires` therefore checks `ShapeType()` is actually `TopAbs_WIRE` before using the result; without that check the caller builds a degenerate solid from nothing and reports a confidently-wrong volume. This is the single most important guard in the feature.
- **Every one of the four kinds is verified against an ANALYTIC expectation through the real modules** (a throwaway bundle of `applyEditsBRep`, not a reimplementation), on a 10×10 profile with a wall of 2 (band area 64): extrude ×5 → **exactly 320**; revolve 90° about an axis 20 away → **2010.619308** vs Pappus's 2010.6192982975, a **relative** error of 4.8e-9 (the adaptive integrator's own eps — an absolute 1e-6 tolerance on a value of ~2010 wrongly flags this, which cost one confusing "MISMATCH" during development); sweep along a straight length-20 path → **exactly 1280**, and along a 90°/R20 **arc** spine → 2010.6193, again Pappus-exact; loft between two profiles 10 apart → **exactly 640**. Dual offset (`thin: 2, thinOuter: 1`) → 395.708 against a 400 sharp-corner ideal, the gap being `GeomAbs_Arc` corner rounding, so dual-offset assertions must be bounded rather than exact.
- **`loft` is the one kind that needed more than a swapped face.** `ThruSections` is WIRE-based and consumes `BRepTools.OuterWire(face)`, discarding inner wires — so handing it a band face silently lofts a FILLED solid (verified: 813.33 for a case whose thin answer is 560). The loft branch instead lofts the outer band wires and the inner band wires as two separate `ThruSections` runs and `BRepAlgoAPI_Cut_3`s them, reusing the cut call shape `booleanSolids`/`cutHole` already use — verified exact (outer 1000, inner 360, cut 640). `sweep` needed no such fallback: `MakePipe_1` handles an annular profile on both straight and curved spines, and the arc case is the one that matters (a straight spine can succeed by degenerating to a prism).
- **A thin feature does NOT consume its profile sketch, unlike a plain one** — `Copy=false` reuses whatever face it is given, and for a thin build that is the freshly-made band, so the original sketch survives in `"Sketches"`. This is user-visible and is documented in `doc/file-formats.md` beside the existing "extruding a sketch consumes it" note.
- **That non-consumption broke the `opBuckets` `startCap` role, and the first fix was wrong in an instructive way.** `collectBucketForOp` identifies `startCap` as "the profile face still present in `after`" — which for a thin extrude finds the **leftover sketch**, a free face that is not part of the new solid at all, while the solid's real annular start cap gets misfiled under `side`. The first attempt kept that match and added a min-dot rule for the start cap; it picked the leftover sketch anyway, because the sketch and the real cap are **geometrically coincident** (identical bbox, both at the extrusion's start) so a dot product cannot separate them. The fix is two parts: skip the `IsSame(profileFace)` match entirely when the op is thin, and require a thin start-cap candidate to be **annular** (`wireCountOf > 1`) — only the caps have an inner wire; the walls and the sketch have one wire each. Verified on the real fold: `startCap` = the annular cap, `endCap` = the far cap, `side` = exactly the 4 outer + 4 inner walls, and the leftover sketch correctly excluded.
- **Op model: two FLAT fields, and that is forced rather than stylistic.** `paramExpr.ts`'s `parseFieldPath` is `/^([A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]*)((?:\[\d+\]){0,2})$/` — an identifier plus up to two NUMERIC indices, with no dotted form. A nested `thin: {outer, inner}` would make these the only numeric op fields in the codebase a parametric variable cannot drive (`sanitizeExprs` would silently drop the entry). `editOps.test.ts` pins this executably: `exprs: {thin: "wall"}` survives, `exprs: {"thin.inner": "wall"}` is dropped.
- **Validation follows `chamfer`'s optional-numeric shape** (`asThinSpec`, called from the four per-kind `case` blocks — `thin` is kind-restricted, so it takes `planeId`'s per-kind precedent rather than `guide`'s wrapper-level one): rejects a non-positive/non-finite `thin`, `thinOuter` without `thin`, and `thinOuter` outside `[0, thin]`.
- **"Closed (face) profiles only" was this cut's stated scope and is now CLOSED — see the next section.** The open-profile half shipped as its own item; what is recorded here is the finding that motivated it, and one correction. This section originally claimed a probed open wire's band came back with **negative volume**, which is why `orientPositiveVolume` exists. Re-probing while building the operand shows that measurement came from the WRONG offset mode: `isOpenResult = true` returns a ONE-SIDED offset, not a band, and closing that open wire into a face is what produced the reversed orientation. The band from `isOpenResult = false` extrudes positive without any flip. `orientPositiveVolume` is kept regardless — it also catches a collapsed band and a revolve that crosses its own axis, it is cheap, and it is the only reason a reversed result could never ship silently.
- **A profile that already has holes is refused** (`wireCountOf !== 1`) rather than silently losing them: `BRepTools.OuterWire` drops inner wires, so thin-extruding a washer face would produce a solid annulus with the hole gone — wrong geometry, not a skip.
- **Verification**: `editOps.test.ts` grew 6 cases (accept on all four kinds, the field-omission default, the full `thinOuter` range including fully-outward, both rejection families, and the flat-field expression test), bug-injected by weakening the `thinOuter <= thin` bound — confirmed to land and to fail exactly that test before restoring. `npm run mcp:smoke` carries the analytic live-kernel block: all four exact volumes, the Pappus relative tolerance, the bucket role split, the bounded dual-offset case, the over-thick graceful skip with its diagnostic, and a plain-extrude regression pinned at 500.
- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every other webview-touching feature**: the panel's new Wall/Outward fields have NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session. See "Verify a change".

## Open-profile (wire) operand for the sweep-family ops (roadmap item 8, second cut, closed)

`.thin()` shipped able to hollow a profile but not to consume an open one — `addPolyline { closed: false }` was authorable, renderable and pickable, and nothing could use it. All four ops now take their profile in one of **two mutually exclusive forms**: `profile`/`profiles` (a `face-N`, unchanged) or `profileEdges`/`profileEdgeSets` (a set of `edge-N` ids assembled into one wire). This closes `.thin()`'s own stated scope and removes the op-model half of `rib()`.

- **The roadmap's kernel note was imprecise, and getting it backwards would have shipped the wrong shape silently.** It recorded that "a raw open wire offsets correctly with `isOpenResult=true`". Probed directly against the live WASM: `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset_3(wire, GeomAbs_Arc, /* isOpenResult */ true).Perform(d, 0)` on an open spine returns a **ONE-SIDED offset** — an open wire on whichever side the sign of `d` selects (for an L-shaped spine, 4 edges all on one side). The **closed band** comes from `isOpenResult = false`: a single closed wire of half-width `|d|`, symmetric about the spine, with **semicircular end caps**, sign of `d` irrelevant. Verified exact against `2·d·L + π·d²` — a length-10 spine gives 23.141593 at `d = 1` and 52.566371 at `d = 2`, and a quarter-circle spine of radius 10 gives 34.557519. Since `thin` is a TOTAL thickness, it maps to `d = thin / 2`. Feeding the one-sided wire to `MakeFace_15` is also what produced the negative-volume prism the `.thin()` section recorded; the real band extrudes positive.
- **`thinOuter` is REFUSED for an open profile, and that is a semantic decision, not a kernel limitation.** An open wire has no inside or outside, so "how much of the wall sits outside the boundary" names nothing — an asymmetric band would put the extra material on whichever side the kernel's own sign convention happens to pick, which the user cannot predict. `openThinOuterOk` therefore reads the RAW `thinOuter` field (not `thinSpecOf`'s, which defaults an absent one to 0 and would wrongly refuse the common case) and accepts only "absent" or "exactly `thin / 2`" — the latter *is* the symmetric band, so the rule is "no meaning", not "never allowed". Anything else is skipped with a diagnostic rather than silently ignored.
- **A wire that is a single straight edge throws, and is repaired rather than refused.** `MakeOffset` on a lone line raises `___cxa_can_catch is not defined` — an Emscripten exception-machinery failure surfacing as an ordinary JS `ReferenceError`, which the `try`/`catch` catches and the module survives (verified: later calls in the same process succeed). A lone **arc** offsets fine, so this is specific to a straight line having no defined offset direction. `splitLoneLine` splits it at its midpoint into two collinear edges, which the probe confirms gives the byte-identical exact area — a two-point polyline is far too natural a profile to refuse over a kernel quirk. The smoke suite pins the split result against the two-segment spine's, so a regression here shows as a divergence rather than a skip.
- **Closedness is computed from vertex degree, because no API answers it.** `addSurfaceFromLines`' own doc already records that `BRepTools.IsReallyClosed`/`DetectClosedness` need arguments this binding doesn't expose and that `ShapeAnalysis_Wire.CheckClosed` did not discriminate — it accepted a best-effort face from an open chain for exactly that reason. Here the answer picks between two completely different band constructions, so `wireIsClosed` tallies each vertex's degree across the wire's own edges using the same `HashCode(1<<30)` bucket + `IsSame` dedup `enumerateEdges`/`buildEdgeFaceAdjacency` already rely on. Verified: a 1-edge open wire reports 2 free ends of 2 vertices, a 2-edge chain 2 of 3, a 4-edge rectangle 0 of 4.
- **`resolveProfileSection` + `profileFaceFor` are the whole feature, as four quadrants.** A **face id** stays byte-for-byte the old path (its inner wires survive a plain feature; a thin one still refuses a holed profile). A **closed edge wire** behaves identically to the equivalent face — filled via `MakeFace_15`, or banded through the same two-offset `thinProfileWires`, which was refactored to take a WIRE rather than a face (the `wireCountOf` hole check moved up into the face branch). An **open edge wire** is refused without `thin` (it encloses no area) and otherwise gets the symmetric band. `loft` resolves one section at a time and requires them all to agree on closedness — the closed path lofts an outer and an inner boundary and cuts, while an open section's band is a single boundary that lofts directly with no cut at all.
- **Offset direction needs no orientation fix-up for a hand-assembled wire** — probed, because a user-picked edge set has no reason to wind the way `BRepTools.OuterWire` does: a rectangle wire built clockwise and one built counter-clockwise both grow to 192.566 at `+2` and shrink to 36 at `-2`. So `+d` is outward regardless of winding, and no area comparison is needed to tell outer from inner.
- **Diagnostics became specific in the same pass.** `buildFeatureSolid` previously returned a bare `null` for every failure and `featureModel` chose one of two generic messages from `thinSpecOf(op)` alone — there was no way to tell "face-9 doesn't exist" from "MakePipe threw". `fail` is now threaded into `buildFeatureSolid` and called at each profile-resolution branch (unresolved face, unresolved edge, disconnected edge set, open-without-thin, `thinOuter` on an open profile, mixed loft sections, unresolved sweep path). `fail` is first-call-wins, so the old generic messages remain as fallbacks and no existing diagnostic changed.
- **A pre-existing gap fixed alongside: `sweep`'s PATH edge was never guide-checked** — only its profile was, so a guide `addLine` could still be used as a spine. `featureModel`'s guide loop now covers both operand forms and both operands, faces and edges alike.
- **Panel: `Set path` (sweep) and `Add section` / `Clear` (loft), following `Set A`.** A sweep's profile and its path are both Line-mode picks once open wires are profiles, and a loft's sections cannot be told apart in one flat selection — both are the same disambiguation `booleanA` already solves, so both reuse its shape (module-level capture state in `main.ts`, a small status label in the form). With nothing captured, both forms behave exactly as before. `extrude`/`revolve` need no capture at all: a Surf selection is a face profile, a Line selection is a wire profile. Captures are dropped after a successful Apply (`resetFeatureCaptures`), because the op that just applied renumbers the very ids they name — the same reason `onApplyBoolean` drops A.
- **Verification.** Three bug injections, each confirmed to land and to change the live-kernel result before being reverted: forcing `isOpenResult = true` in `openBandWire` collapses **every** open-profile case to no new solid at all; making `wireIsClosed` always report closed sends the 2-segment spine down the two-offset path and yields **262.83 instead of 115.71** — a plausible-looking wrong number, exactly the class the cross-check exists for — while also turning the open-without-`thin` refusal into a silently zero-volume solid; and disabling `splitLoneLine` turns the single-straight-edge case into a skip. `editOps.test.ts` +5 cases (both forms per kind, neither/both rejected, the `^edge-\d+$` element check — a `face-N` in `profileEdges` is a silently-wrong operand, not a resolve-time miss, which is why it is refused up front like `asMidaxisPair` — loft's nested arity, and composition with `thin`/`exprs`); `opCatalog.test.ts` +2 (`referencedEntities` and the `edge-2+edge-3` label). `npm run mcp:smoke` carries the analytic live-kernel block, whose headline is a **cross-check**: the same rectangle extruded via `profile: "face-6"` and via its own four edges gives byte-identical volumes (320), which a resolver that quietly built a different wire could not do. Plus the open-spine extrude/revolve (Pappus)/sweep/loft against `thin·L + π·(thin/2)²` products at **relative** tolerance (the adaptive integrator's own eps puts them a few parts in 1e8 off, which an absolute 1e-6 would flag — the same trap the thin block already documents), the lone-straight-edge split matching the two-segment spine exactly, and four refusals each asserted by name. Full run: 563 checks, 0 failures.
- **Deliberate scope line**: an open profile with no `thin` is refused rather than extruded into a shell. `featureModel` appends solids, and `finishThin`/`opBuckets` both assume one; a ruled surface would land in the free-face pass under "Sketches" instead. Recorded as a possible follow-up, not a gap.
- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every other webview-touching feature**: the `Set path` / `Add section` buttons and picking a profile in Line mode have NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session. See "Verify a change".

## Region pick + drill (roadmap item 1, `.pick(regions)` + `.drill()`, closed)

The last shippable cut of the FluidCAD item: `pick` narrows WHICH enclosed regions of a multi-region profile a feature consumes, and `drill` cuts the picked regions through target solids. What remains of that item after this is loft guides/conditions (probed-red — see the probe record in `doc/roadmap.md`) and `wrap()` (probed-blocked).

- **Orientation is load-bearing and was probed live, not assumed.** `MakeFace_15(outer).Add(inner)` keeps hole-ness for an enumerated inner wire added as-is (20×20 with a 10×10 hole rebuilds to exactly 300.000); the same wire reversed adds an island (500.000). So `regionFacesFor` (`occtOperations.ts`) never reads wire orientation at all — `TopoDS.Orientation` is unbound in this build (`Orientation is not a function`, another green-insufficient instance) — it reuses enumerated wires as-is for holes (via `OuterWire` + `IsSame` to find region 0) and builds picked islands as standalone forward faces. Each enumerated wire alone builds its enclosed disk (400.0/100.0), and region prism minus region prism via the verified `Cut_3` is exact (3000.000).
- **Region semantics, stated once**: wires enumerate in `TopExp` order (`wiresOfFace`); region 0 is the outer boundary, 1..N the inner loops. Absent/`"outer"` = the face as modeled (holes preserved — the byte-identical old path). `"all"` or any list containing 0 = outer boundary with every hole filled (picked inners merge into the slab, so `[0,1]` ≡ `[0]` on a single-hole face — verified at identical 1334.3363). A list without 0 = one island face per picked loop, fused via the existing `combineSolids`. Out-of-range indices skip at replay like unresolvable ids (validation cannot know the loop count); malformed picks reject the whole op up front (`asPick`, the `asThinSpec` shape — empty/non-integer/negative/unknown-word all null).
- **Two deliberate refusals, both semantic rather than kernel limits.** An explicit `pick` refuses `thin` (thin walls build from the outer boundary alone — drop one of them; absent pick + thin is the unchanged old path). `pick` on `loft`/`rib` rejects at validation (a per-section selector needs a parallel array the op model does not have — silently ignoring it would lie about which regions were lofted). Edge profiles expose a single region (`[0]`/`"all"` coincide; anything else names regions a wire does not have).
- **`drillCut` is the `cutHole` skeleton with a region-built tool** — targets resolve, picked regions prism (`prismsForFaces`, the exact `MakePrism_1(face, vec, false, true)` call shape extrude uses), fused when several, `Cut_3` against the compounded targets, leftovers carried over. Length is explicit-only (no `upToFace` variant — a through-cut covers the use case). B-rep only (the mesh engine's blanket `BREP_ONLY_OPS` skip diagnoses it, zero mesh code), topology-changing (buckets record `wall`, like the hole family), queryable `targets`/`profile`/`profileEdges`, guide-checked profile like every other feature operand.
- **Pick-free replay is byte-identical by construction**: `featureProfileFaces` returns the old single-face path untouched when no explicit pick is present, and the extrude/revolve/sweep branches build the same single prism through `prismsForFaces`. Proven, not asserted — the full `mcp:smoke` run (including the smoothing block, whose absolute pin predates this change) is green with zero non-pick behavior shifts.
- **Panel: a `Regions` row on extrude/revolve/sweep plus a Drill composer in Modify.** One shared `pickField()` (blank = as modeled) with the parse in `main.ts`'s `pickOf` (single choke point, so preview ≡ Apply — an unparseable value is an error there, never a silent drop, unlike `thinOf`'s silent-drop). Drill reuses `profileOperandFromSelection` (Surf face or Line wire) + Vol targets, the same selection mapping extrude uses. The drill icon went through the real TikZ→SVG pipeline (`icons/tikz/drill.tex`, even-odd compound, explicit filldraw arrowhead per the 1em rule); the targeted rebuild touched exactly 3 lines (`git diff --stat`: the tex, the svg, one `opIcons.ts` line).
- **Verification.** `editOps.test.ts` +7 cases (omission, all four acceptions, dedupe, malformed family, loft/rib rejection, drill operand requirements, edge form); live-kernel scratch first (detector loop: `pick=[1]` applies only on a multi-region face — found face-2/face-4 of the holed box, pinned analytically like the tutorial's edge-13 precedent), then `npm run mcp:smoke` analytic blocks: no-pick/fill/island extrusions at 1284.0708/1334.3363/984.6018, drill at exactly 700.0000, drill-with-pick removing the filled slab at 584.6018, and four refusals each asserted by name. Plus three pre-existing defects fixed alongside (each blocking verification, each verified pre-existing at HEAD, not caused here): the `tools/list` count (44→46, two selector tools never bumped it), the rib miss-case missing required `thin` (tested validation-rejection instead of the behind-plane path), and the stale smoothed-loft pin (1457.0866→1457.9374 with a relational guard; plain still matches bit-for-bit).
- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every other webview-touching feature**: the Regions row and Drill composer have NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session. See "Verify a change".

## Build-time classification buckets (roadmap item 7 Phase 1, closed)

`doc/roadmap.md` item 7 ("Selector synthesis") Phase 1: every topology-changing edit op records, at the moment it runs, which `face-N` ids it produced and what role each plays — a per-op namespace strictly better than positional ids alone for exactly the entities that drift most, and the raw material Phase 2's synthesis ladder consumes. Deliberately scoped to **record + expose** (user decision this session): nothing consumes buckets in `entityRebind.ts`, nothing persists them to a sidecar (derivable by replay), and no selector AST/ladder/induction/oracle machinery ships yet — roadmap item 7 stays open for those three pieces.

- **Mechanism: a hybrid before/after face-set diff + per-kind role names, NOT a collector threaded through 15 op functions.** `occtOperations.ts`'s `collectBucketForOp()` runs inside `applyEditsBRep`'s fold at each op boundary: enumerate faces of the shape before and after the op, `HashCode`-bucket the before set, and every after face with no `IsSame` partner is "produced" (the established dedup technique). The produced set is then named from `opBuckets.ts`'s `PRODUCED_ROLE` table (`band`/`inner`/`wall`/`cutFace`/`sectionFace`/`copies`/`body`, generic `produced` otherwise) — one mechanism, zero per-op instrumentation. `applyEditsBRep` gained a 7th optional trailing param (`opBuckets?: OpBucket[]`, the same optional-collector pattern as `outcomes`/`guideCollector`); `loadBRep`/`loadBRepCached` always pass it.
- **The `TOPOLOGY_CHANGING_OPS` gate is load-bearing for CORRECTNESS, not just cost.** Rigid transforms (`translate`/`rotate`/`scale`/`mirror`/`align`) run `BRepBuilderAPI_Transform_2(..., copy=true)`, which creates genuinely new TShapes for every face — an ungated diff across one would report the ENTIRE model as "produced". Verified live in the throwaway probe (a translated box: 6/6 faces produced, 0 preserved). Pattern ops stay gated IN: their internal copies transform only the copies while the original shape is kept by reference, so the diff correctly finds just the copies' faces (`copies` role).
- **Role semantics, live-verified against the real WASM before writing the code** (throwaway esbuild-bundled probe, this repo's standard recipe — three call-shape errors caught and fixed by it: `BRepBndLib.Add` is a static, `BRepAdaptor_Surface_2` needs a `TopoDS.Face_1`-cast face, `MakeFillet.Add_2` needs an `Edge_1`-cast edge):
  - **`extrude`/`revolve` record `startCap` via `MakePrism_1`/`MakeRevol_1`'s `Copy=false` identity reuse** — the profile face object IS the start cap, so `IsSame` hits in the after enumeration (probe-confirmed for both). **A shared-TShape nuance the probe caught**: with `Copy=false` that face is the SAME TShape as the operand it came from, so it appears at TWO enumeration positions (the original body's and the new solid's) — first match wins; both ids resolve to the same live face.
  - **`extrude` additionally splits `endCap` (the produced face farthest along the extrusion direction — probe: the cap centre sits at the profile plane + length) from `side`** (the rest — the cylinder/planes connecting the caps). `revolve`'s produced set keeps the generic `produced` role: its caps can't be split without unverified geometry heuristics, and the honest generic name beats a plausible wrong one.
  - **`band` (fillet/chamfer/draft) and every other role include REBUILT faces, not just brand-new ones — a real finding, not an implementation shortcut.** Probe: filleting ONE box edge reports 5 produced faces, not 1 — the two adjacent planar faces and the top/bottom faces are REBUILT (shrunken → new TShapes) plus 1 new cylinder. Those rebuilt faces drift positionally exactly like new ones, so recording them is the point; the role name is the op's dominant effect, never a claim that every listed face is new. Same for booleans (mixed provenance → generic `produced`: a cylinder cut through a box reports the new wall AND the two rebuilt hole-punched faces).
  - Wireframe ops (`addLine`/`addArc`/…) and `addPoint` make no faces → no bucket at all. A gracefully-SKIPPED op → no bucket (a skip never fabricates a classification — pinned in `mcp:smoke` via the existing doomed-fillet fixture).
- **The staleness contract, stated once and honored everywhere**: recorded ids are the AFTER enumeration's positions **valid against the model state at that op's own step** — later topology-changing ops renumber them. Nothing re-resolves them automatically; a future accessor re-derives by prefix replay (Phase 2's job, and the reason the query form is worth building). The webview chip tooltip says so explicitly.
- **Wiring is the `guideIds` template end to end**: `BRepResult.opBuckets` (+ `BRepCacheEntry.opBuckets`, with prefix+suffix merge on incremental append exactly like `opOutcomes` — the reused prefix ops aren't re-run, and their recorded ids describe their own prefix states, identical in the append case) → `provider.ts`'s `handleBRep` includes it in the `geometry` post → `main.ts`'s `lastOpBuckets` state (`rebuildMeshModel()` clears it — mesh sources have no host replay) → `editsPanel.render(…, opBuckets)`. `mcpTools.ts`'s `entitySummary` gained `opBuckets: result.opBuckets ?? []`, so `load_model`/`apply_edit_ops`/`render_ops_prefix` responses all carry it with zero per-tool changes (kernelIpc passes the plain-JSON record untouched).
- **Webview consumer: a `+N` chip per producing history row** (`editsPanel.ts`, `.edit-bucket` in `viewer.css`, hover-revealed like the ✕ remove button): tooltip = `bucketSummary(roles)` (e.g. `start cap ×1, end cap ×1, side walls ×4`) + the ids + the staleness note; click = transient highlight of those faces via the new `onHighlightBucket(ids | null)` callback — `main.ts` maps ids to `{entityType: "surface"}` entities and goes through `viewer.renderSelection()` directly, **never** into the `SelectionSet` (the selection-groups context menu's hover-preview precedent), with `renderHighlight()` restoring the real selection on clear. Click-toggle, one chip active at a time, reset on every render (most triggers coincide with a model rebuild + `refreshColors()`, which already restores the real selection's rendering). `stopPropagation()` on the chip so highlighting never also jumps the timeline (the ✕-button precedent).
- **Pure-module tests** (`opBuckets.test.ts`): JSON round-trip (kernelIpc/postMessage-safe by construction), role-vocabulary completeness — every face-producing kind in `TOPOLOGY_CHANGING_OPS` has a `PRODUCED_ROLE` entry or the extrude/revolve special case, rigid transforms deliberately have none — every role has a display label, and `bucketSummary`'s fixed-order rendering. Validated by `tsc` alone when they landed (Node 18 blocked vitest 4 then — no longer true, see the Node-version note above); they run under `npm test` now.
- **Verified end-to-end via extended `npm run mcp:smoke` (502 checks, up from 490)**: the first-flow addBox classifies as exactly one bucket with `body` ×6 over `face-N` ids; the guide-fixture flow's control extrude gets the canonical split (`startCap` = the profile face's id via Copy=false identity, `endCap` farthest-along, `side` = the cylinder, roles disjoint) while `addLine` contributes no bucket; the doomed-fillet fixture proves a skipped op contributes no bucket. **`npm run perf`**: zero-ops fixtures mean the collector never runs on the benchmarked path (the fold body never executes), so the feature adds nothing there — but perf DID surface a real pre-existing defect, below.
- **Verification gap, stated plainly, same caveat as every webview-touching feature**: the chip rendering, click-to-highlight feel, and tooltip layout have NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session — see "Verify a change" below.

### A third wasm-abort signature, found by the perf harness at clean HEAD (defect fix shipped with this change)

`npm run perf` failed on the medium fixture with `wasmTable.get(...) is not a function` — reproduced deterministically at **clean HEAD** (stashed, rebuilt, re-ran: identical failure), so pre-existing and environmental, not caused by this change. The sequence matters: warmup + small (angle1 meshed twice in the same worker) + medium (bull) is what trips it; any shorter sequence passes, and a standalone direct `generateMesh` call with the same options succeeds — it is an accumulated-state abort class, sibling to the documented accumulated-heap-pressure aborts elsewhere in this file.

- **The real defect: this third signature matched NONE of the four kernel services' abort vocabularies.** The recorded history already had two misses (`table index` vs `WebAssembly.Table.get(): invalid index … into function table` → fixed by adding `function table`); this third surface is a JS TypeError from emscripten's glue (`wasmTable.get(...)`) when the corrupt table entry resolves to a non-function — a different message shape entirely. Undetected = singleton never reset = every later call keeps failing with no "kernel has been reset" message for `mcp-smoke`'s `callWithCleanRetry` to key on. **All four vocabularies (`occtService`/`gmshService`/`meshioService`/`ftetwildService`) gained `wasmtable` (case-insensitive) together** — the distinctive token, not a generic `is not a function`, which is a common JS bug phrase that would misclassify ordinary application errors. Regression-tested in `occtService.test.ts` (recognizes the real message + a case-variant; does NOT match a plain `foo.bar is not a function`).
- **The perf harness gained a single clean retry** on the `/kernel has been reset/i` signature — mirroring `mcp-smoke`'s `callWithCleanRetry` precedent but WITHOUT a state-reset callback, for a stated reason: the harness's two call types (`load_model`/`generate_mesh`) are read-only benchmarking and never persist sidecar state, so the aborted attempt cannot leave a partial write the retry would double-apply (the exact failure that made smoke's naive blind retry need `resetState`). With the vocabulary fix + retry, `npm run perf` completes all four fixtures (the retry fired once, mid-generate_mesh, exactly as designed).
- **Perf numbers this session are environmental, not a code regression — and the baseline was deliberately NOT updated.** All four fixtures came in ~3–8× baseline uniformly, including `load_model` paths this change provably never touches; the sandbox was measurably slower this session (the smoke suite also took ~25 min). `--update-baseline` is reserved for deliberate, reviewed perf-affecting changes, never routinely — masking an environment slowdown would blind the harness to future real regressions.

## Selector synthesis (roadmap item, closed in full — ladder, induction, and both persistence halves)

`doc/roadmap.md`'s old item 1: persist a **re-executable query** instead of a positional entity id, so a selection survives the renumbering every topology-changing op inflicts (FluidCAD's answer: never persist a reference at all — convert the pick into a query that re-derives it). Shipped in five layers, each independently verified; the pieces compose with (and never replace) `entityRebind.ts`'s after-the-fact repair.

- **Phase 1 — build-time classification buckets** (see this file's own section below): the per-op `face-N` namespaces every later layer consumes.
- **Ladder rungs 1–3 — `resolve_selector`** (`selectorQuery.ts` + `entityFacts.ts`'s `resolveBucketSelector`): resolves `{version: 1, source: {kind: "bucket", op, role}}` (rung 1), with an optional induced `filter` (one leaf or an AND-list: planar/surfaceType/normal/area thresholds over exact current-shape facts; rung 2, `selectorPredicate.ts`) plus `rank` (`{by: "area", order, n}` with a deterministic face-N tie-break, null areas last), or `{kind: "scene", filter?, rank?}` with no anchor at all (rung 3; bare scene queries rejected — naming the whole model is never meant). Mechanism: prefix replay re-derives the reference ids (never trusting caller ids), full replay + `rebindEntities` at the shared `1e-3·bboxDiagonal` tolerance — **the returned `matches` ARE the oracle** (trustworthy only at ~0 `centreDistance`); resolve-THEN-filter ordering is load-bearing (filtering on prefix facts would promote faces that only matched old geometry). An induced selection of zero is an honest empty, never a whole-bucket fallback. The bottom rung (bucket indices) needs no build — positional ids + rebind already are the fallback.
- **Induction — `synthesize_selector`** (`selectorInduce.ts` + `synthesizeSelector`): given a picked `entityId` + producing op/role, induces the query naming exactly that entity — bare bucket if targets span the universe, else single qualitative leaves → rank → exact normal → greedy pairs (openers by gain, exhaustive trimmer: intersections only remove, so "extend by uncovered gain" is the wrong criterion) → leaf+rank → area literals dead last — accepted only on exact re-execution plus `centreDistance ~ 0`. Honest nulls with reasons; pattern producers return `bindable: false`. Live probe finding recorded: opposite box faces report the SAME plane normal (orientation-ignorant `faceSurfaceInfo`, pre-existing) — the probe pivoted to a fillet band face.
- **Phase A — `Part.selector`** (`partsSidecar.ts` tolerant parse + `entityFacts.ts`'s `resolvePartSelectors`): annotation+cache (`surfaces` stays the cache; consumers keep reading plain ids), resolved on open and after every op-list change — one full replay shared by all parts, one prefix replay per distinct bucket op — overwriting only on oracle-clean results. Stale-index guard via the server-derived `selectorOpKind` tag (a splice that moves a different kind under the stored index freezes instead of repointing); `selectorQuery.ts`'s kind tags are derived at write time, never caller-supplied. `partsModel.clone()` had to learn the field — the same clone-drops-a-field defect class `AnnotationsModel.clone` hit.
- **Phase B — per-op `targetQueries`** (`editOps.ts` validation + `opQueryResolve.ts` resolution): the id-field counterpart of `exprs`. `targetQueries` maps operand fields (`QUERYABLE_OPERAND_FIELDS`, locked 1:1 with `referencedEntities` by `opCatalog.test.ts` — a queryable field the UI never reads would be a hover-tooltip lie) to queries, sanitized per-entry in `validateEditOp`'s wrapper (unknown field / malformed query / dangling tag dropped; scene queries need no tag — no producer exists). **Resolution is fused into a sequential fold** (`resolveOpOperandQueries`), not a map: op N resolves against `shape(ops[0..N-1])` with earlier queries already resolved — a parallel map would resolve against never-applied geometry. Bucket queries replay the resolved prefix to re-derive the reference bucket and re-match via the shared machinery; freeze semantics are replay-only (frozen field keeps its cache, loses its query in the replay copy, sidecar untouched, warning surfaces via `BRepResult.queryWarnings` → `load_model` warnings / viewer status line). Guards: forward references (producing op not strictly before the consumer), kind-tag mismatch, pattern producers, no bucket, unresolved/empty, and scalar slots (`SCALAR_OPERAND_FIELDS`) resolving to anything but exactly one id — never "first of many". `occtService` gates on `hasTargetQueries(ops)` (query-free documents pay nothing) and **skips append-reuse for query documents** (a query's index addresses the full list; the suffix fold's base is the previous shape — resolving mid-list queries against it would read the wrong geometry). Mesh sources never resolve (cached ids); queries inside `repeat` bodies are refused (indices are baked, not replayed).

**Import-graph rule this feature established**: `opQueryResolve.ts` imports `occtOperations`/`editOps`/`selectorQuery`/`entityRebind` but NEVER `occtService` (which calls it) or `entityFacts` (which imports `occtService`) — the face-fact read and predicate application are shallow local mirrors kept in lockstep by the smoke suite, the same acyclicity discipline `brepGProp.ts`'s extraction established.

**Verified end-to-end against the live WASM** (`npm run mcp:smoke`, not just unit tests): bucket re-resolution to identical ids at ~0 oracle distance (with and without an unrelated append); scene largest/smallest/conjunction cross-checked via `inspect`; pattern-instance refusal; induction producing a coordinate-free query that survives an unrelated append and re-hits the same cylinder; Phase A's splice freeze; Phase B's happy path (3000-volume extrusion on a queried 600-area face — an unresolved cache could never produce it), forward-reference freeze, and kind-tag mismatch freeze, all with named warnings; and the repeat-body refusal. Standing F5-only caveat: the panel-side query surface (history label, hover) is display-only.

### Interactive half — pin-as-query row (follow-up, closed)

Phase B shipped headless-only: queries were MCP/sidecar-hand-authored, with no webview surface. The Edits panel's Extrude/Revolve/Shell/Draft forms now carry a **Pin query** row (`editsPanel.ts`'s `queryRow`, `QUERYABLE_PANEL_FORMS` in `opCatalog.ts`): bucket + role selects populated from the latest `geometry`'s `opBuckets`, a Synthesize button, and a result line. The panel owns only bucket coordinates; `main.ts`'s `onSynthesizeQuery` reads the live selection through the same mapping `buildOpForPanel` uses, posts one `selectorSynthesizeRequest` (`protocol.ts`, new pair + `SelectorSynthesizeResultEntry`), and stages the reply as a `PendingOpQuery` (one per form+field — replace, never stack). `buildOpForPanel` attaches staged entries (the single choke point, so preview ≡ Apply), but only while the field's current ids still equal the synthesized set — otherwise both paths surface a `queryNote` instead of silently attaching to moved-on ids. `pushResolvedOp` is the single commit helper (consumes attached entries, surfaces the note); a new `geometry` load clears all pending state. The provider handler loops `synthesizeSelector` per id (cap 25) and stamps `kind` from the producing op itself — server-derived, the `set_part` precedent.

- **Two deliberate scope lines, both kernel-dictated, both stated in the UI.** Face operands only (`synthesizeSelector`'s universe is `faceFilterableFacts` — an edge/volume pick could never induce, so the row refuses it up front with a reason instead of paying a doomed round trip), and exactly one face (joint multi-entity induction doesn't exist — `synthesizeSelector` accepts only on exact-singleton re-execution — so attaching a singleton query over a wider pick would silently narrow the operand). Edge/volume predicates and joint induction each unlock more forms with a one-line `QUERYABLE_PANEL_FORMS` change once the kernel grows them.
- **A real pre-existing regression found and fixed in the same pass, not a hypothetical.** Commit `3ee2963` (live preview) dropped the `f.appendChild(...)` wrapper from every `applyButtonDraft` call site at once — since then NO Edits form rendered an Apply button (forms opened, preview worked, nothing could commit), and nothing caught it because no test asserted the button exists. Fixed by centralizing the append (`applyButtonDraft` stashes the row, `renderParams` appends it after every param row — order-identical to the pre-regression layout, including the guide-checkbox tail), which makes the failure class unrepeatable. The new `test:webview` pin-as-query case asserts the button's presence (without it the click silently no-ops and no `editsChanged` is ever posted — the exact false-pass shape this repo documents).
- **A second real bug, caught by the new test, same session.** The first version of the `selectorSynthesizeResult` handler nulled the stale-response latch BEFORE checking the id — a stale reply consumed the latch and the real reply was then ignored. Fixed to the established check-first idiom. Both bugs were found by the harness test failing against real code, not by synthetic injection — which is the stronger evidence, and is why the test's failure modes are documented here instead of an injection log.
- **Verified**: `test:webview` case drives the real bundle end-to-end minus the kernel (bucket injection by re-posting the harness's own captured geometry, since the shared fixture's rigid-transform ops record no buckets): row renders, picker lists the bucket, Synthesize posts a well-formed request (op/role/single face id), a stale reply stages nothing, the real reply stages a summary, Apply attaches `targetQueries`/`targetQueryKinds` to the pushed extrude (read back off `editsChanged`). `tsc`/`vitest`/`build` clean; `docs:screenshots` regenerated (the churn also picks up several features' drift since the last regen — Region fit panel, clip Face/3Pts row, macro/standard-parts panels — verified non-regressive by inspecting `viewer-main.png`: correctly framed, no misframing class).
- **Verification gap, stated plainly**: the host handler (`provider.ts`, no unit-test path) and the human feel of the row are F5-only — same caveat as every other webview-touching feature. The test fakes the kernel reply; `mcp:smoke` covers the kernel side.
## Narrative tutorials + executable doc examples (roadmap items 11 + 12, Phase 1 of each, closed)

Taken together because each is worth much more with the other, exactly as the roadmap's own sequencing note said: item 11's tutorials end in op-list JSON that item 12's gate executes, and without the tutorials that gate had **one** block in all of `doc/` to gate (`doc/mcp-server.md`'s bolt circle — the only fully-formed compilable `{variables, steps}` document in the tree; the sibling `jsonc` macro block has its `steps` elided as a comment). Before this, `doc/` had **no narrative content at all** — `getting-started.md` is a panel-by-panel reference, and `index.md` called it "a walkthrough", which it was not.

- **` ```parametric ` is the per-block opt-in marker, and it accepts BOTH shapes the docs naturally use** — a `{variables?, steps}` script (used as-is) or a bare op array (normalized to `{steps: arr.map(op => ({op}))}`), which is literally an `apply_edit_ops` `ops` payload and is what a tutorial's "Full operation list" is. Both compile through the one existing `compileParametricScript`. A ` ```json ` block stays illustrative and is never executed — `doc/protocol.md`'s message envelopes and `doc/file-formats.md`'s sidecar-file examples are *not* op payloads and must not be compiled as if they were.
- **`doc/.vitepress/config.mts` aliases `parametric → json` via `markdown.languageAlias`** — without it Shiki has no such language and every one of those blocks renders as **plain unhighlighted text**. `docs:build` only warns ("The language 'parametric' is not loaded, falling back to 'txt'") and still exits 0, so this is another instance of the standing "inspect the output, not the exit code" rule: it was caught by reading the build log and then confirming real token spans in `doc/.vitepress/dist/tutorials/bracket.html`.
- **`src/docExamples.ts` (pure, test-only) tracks EVERY fenced block, not just the parametric ones.** A first version only tracked `parametric` fences, so a ` ```parametric ` line nested inside a longer outer fence — which any page documenting this convention necessarily contains — would wrongly open a block. Caught by its own unit test. It lives in `src/` beside every other pure module (no bundle entry point imports it, so esbuild never sees it) specifically so item 12 Phase 2's coverage gate can share the same tree walk.
- **The gate is compile-only, and that is deliberate.** "Zero rejected steps" is `issues.length === 0 && !truncated && every report[i].rejected === 0 && ops.length > 0` — the last conjunct catching the "compiles but produces nothing" rot class. It does **not** check that `edge-13` resolves against real geometry: `validateEditOp` only checks an id's *shape*. That gap is closed once, separately, by the smoke block below.
- **Failure messages name the expected shape.** `parametricScript.ts`'s own reason is the generic `"invalid op"`; the test rewrites it through `mcpTools.ts`'s exported `explainEditOpRejection`, so a failure reads `Unknown op kind "addCylinderX". Did you mean "addCylinder"? Expected shape: {center: [x,y,z] (base), axis: …}`. A `repeat` step reports one reason **per iteration**, so a single bad op in a `times: 8` loop yielded eight identical messages until they were deduped after stripping the `iteration N: ` prefix.
- **Verified by bug injection, not by a green run** — two injections (a renamed op kind, a renamed op field), each **confirmed to have landed before being trusted**: the first `sed` targeted the wrong line and silently changed nothing while the suite stayed green, the exact silent-no-match failure this file already records. Both then failed with a precise `mcp-server.md:210 — …` message and were reverted.
- **Every tutorial's op list was probed against the live kernel, not guessed** (throwaway script driving the real `dist/mcp-server.js` over stdio, not committed). Findings that shaped the pages: `examples/STP/block.stp` is confirmed **3 × 4 × 5 mm centred on the origin** (x ±1.5, y ±2, z ±2.5 — an exploration pass had misread the Z extent as `0→2.5`); a `boolean` rebuilds the compound as **result first, then untouched solids**, so the union in the bracket tutorial moves the seed from `solid-0` to `solid-1` — real id drift, documented in the page rather than hidden; the bracket's inside corner is `edge-13` (length 60, centre `[0,−14,3]`); and `extrude`'s own bucket reports `startCap`/`endCap`/`side`, which the enclosure tutorial reads directly.
- **A genuine behavioural finding the flange tutorial had to state honestly: a plain op's `exprs` resolve on the NEXT read, not within the `apply_edit_ops` call that created it.** Probed directly — `count: "N"` with `N = 8` returned 6 tools in the applying call's own `model`, then 8 on the following `load_model`. This is the documented resolve-on-read design (`editVariables.ts`'s two resolution sites), not a bug, and the tutorial says so. The literal `45`/`8` written beside the `exprs` in that page's block are the last-computed-value caches, deliberately kept consistent with `R = 30`/`N = 8` so the block stands alone.
- **`scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs` pins the flagship tutorial for good**: `doc/tutorials/bracket.md`'s six ops are applied in ONE call (as a reader would paste them) and every one must **apply**, not merely validate, plus assertions on the seed bbox, the finished extents, and the 18-face count the page claims. Applied as one call rather than a per-op loop so a `callWithCleanRetry` retry can never double-append; its `resetState` re-copies `block.stp` and drops the edits sidecar.
- **Per-step generated screenshots (roadmap Tier 3 item 4, closed).** `fixtures-entry.ts` tessellates cumulative op-list prefixes from `block.stp` via the already-4-parameter `loadBRep` (fused + finished bracket, tooled + finished flange, sketch + extruded + shelled enclosure — the ids are the smoke-pinned ones above, inherited not re-derived) plus one `exportBRep`-baked bracket meshed at the fea-prep page's own `Size max = 4`; `capture.mjs` gained 8 purely-additive full-page `SHOTS` entries reusing `populate()`'s exact `geometry → viewState(null) → sleep → tree → sleep` ordering. Verified by face counts (17/24, 33/17, 7/12/17 meshes) and by inspecting every new PNG (framing correct, no misframed-close-up class). Re-running `capture.mjs` re-renders the 20 pre-existing shots with environment-dependent byte churn — those were reverted, keeping only the 8 new files.

- **No verification gap.** Nothing here touches the webview, the kernel, or `provider.ts`, so unlike essentially every other feature in this file there is no "not exercised in a real Extension Development Host session" caveat. Verified with `npm run build`, `npm test` (1594 + 9 new), `npm run docs:build`, `npm run mcp:smoke`, and the two bug injections.

### Phase 2 — a coverage gate with three failure modes (closed)

`src/docOpCoverage.ts` (pure, test-only, shares `docExamples.ts`'s `walkMarkdownFiles` — the reuse Phase 1's own doc comment promised) checks the live catalog (`allOpKinds()`, i.e. `describeCapabilities()`'s list, no second registry) against `doc/**`: **missing** (a live kind named nowhere), **stale** (an op-position snippet claiming a removed kind — the rename-catcher), **unused-allowlist** (an opt-out for a removed or newly-documented kind). `src/docOpCoverage.test.ts` runs all three over the real tree in `npm test`, so it gates CI.

- **A mention is deliberately narrow**: an exact kind token inside a backtick span or a single/double-quoted string (line-bounded, so a prose apostrophe can never open a span that swallows the paragraph), or an `"op"` / `{ op:` JSON value. Bare prose never counts — `section`, `scale`, `mirror`, and `boolean` are all ordinary English words that would match everywhere. A trailing `()` is stripped, so `` `rib()` `` counts.
- **Stale comes ONLY from op-position channels** (`"op": "X"`, `{ op: 'X'`), never prose backticks: `` `wrap()` ``, `.guides()`, and `.drill()` are legitimate discussions of unshipped concepts, and flagging them would be a false positive. A rename surviving only in prose is still caught — via `missing`, since the new name appears nowhere.
- **The gate's first run found a real gap, not a green pass**: `rib` (live since the rib cut shipped) was named nowhere user-facing — only in `doc/roadmap.md`'s own Tier-1 text. Fixed by documenting it where it belongs (`doc/getting-started.md`'s EDIT table gained a **Rib** row), not by allowlisting it. `doc/op-coverage-allowlist.txt` therefore starts empty (comments only); each entry must carry its `# reason`, and the gate fails on an entry for a removed or newly-documented kind.
- **Adjacent fixes in the same pass**: `doc/mcp-server.md`'s skill paragraph still said "roadmap item 5" after the screenshots close renumbered the coverage gate to item 4; `doc/development.md`'s CI step list now includes the new `docs:build` step.
- **`npm run docs:build` now also runs in `ci.yml`** (one step after unit tests) — the known gap recorded above is closed rather than left queued: the only dead-link check over `doc/**` no longer waits for the push-to-master `docs.yml` run. `docs.yml` itself is unchanged (deploy still lives there).
- **Verified by bug injection, not by a green run**: a renamed kind and a renamed field in a temp copy (each confirmed to have landed before trusting the red), plus the gate green over the real tree. Full suite green; `npm run docs:build` green.

**Two pre-existing defects were fixed in the same pass, both unrelated to the item and both blocking its verification:**

- **`editOps.ts`'s `asMidaxisPair` accepted a MIXED `["face-0", "edge-1"]` pair**, which this file's own "Cheap thin-wrapper ops" section says must be refused (`resolveMidaxis` reads a face via its cylinder axis and an edge via its chord — a mixed pair has no coherent mid-axis). It validated each element independently, so `editOps.test.ts` had a **failing test at HEAD**. Now both elements must match the same prefix.
- **`scripts/mcp-smoke/run.mjs`'s hostile-Part-name `apply_edit_ops` aborted reproducibly** with `OCCT crashed (memory access out of bounds)` — the accumulated-heap-pressure class already documented above, at a call site still using a plain `call`. Given a clean-retry guard with a real `resetState`, matching every other site of this class.

## OpenSCAD .csg import (roadmap Tier 2 item 2, path (a), closed)

Path (a) only — path (b) (shell out to an installed binary) has since closed too (next section); path (c) (bundle `openscad-wasm`) is rejected to Non-goals as a GPLv3 one-way door. No new dependency, no license change, no new `EditOp` kind, no new MCP tool, no new kernel `Pipeline` key.

- **Opaque base, not op lowering — a deliberate pivot mid-session, and the reasons are load-bearing.** The first implementation lowered the CSG tree to `EditOp`s replayed over an empty compound. It was replaced before wiring because lowering needs three things this codebase cannot supply cheaply: positional `solid-N` index simulation across nested booleans (fragile under any graceful skip — the same drift semantics every `.edits.json` list already has, but manufactured by the importer itself); a shear decomposition (`multmatrix` is not guaranteed rigid and no shear op exists — importing children untransformed would silently misplace them); and a brand-new `addPolyhedron` op kind complete with panel button + TikZ-generated icon (blocked: no TeX toolchain in this environment, and `opCatalog.test.ts` locks every kind to ≥1 button). The opaque base needs none of it: `src/csgImport.ts` parses text into an AST (`CsgNode`), `src/csgModel.ts` walks it with LIVE handles — a `.csg` document is exactly like a STEP import (user edits layer on top via the sidecar; the parsed structure is not itself a history). `multmatrix` applies raw via `gp_GTrsf` (shear included, no decomposition); polyhedron sews natively.
- **Every OCCT call shape was probed live before writing** (throwaway esbuild-bundled scripts, not committed): empty-compound + two primitives (volume exact at 1523.60), boolean union (exactly 1500), `gp_GTrsf_1` + `BRepBuilderAPI_GTransform_2` for near-identity float noise / ×2 scale (exactly 2000) / mirror (stays positive), pyramid sew to 0 free edges, N-gon prism exact vs analytic `(N/2)r²sin(2π/N)h`. Two probe findings shaped the code: the `gp_GTrsf` constructor is SUFFIXED (`gp_GTrsf_1` — bare `gp_GTrsf` throws "no accessible constructor", the same class as `Message_ProgressRange_1`), and a CCW-wound pyramid sews to volume **−266.67**, so every polyhedron passes through the same flip-if-negative rule `occtOperations.ts`'s (private) `orientPositiveVolume` enforces.
- **One choke point covers everything downstream.** `readShape()` takes an untyped `format: string`, so a single `"csg"` branch (MEMFS bytes → UTF-8 → `parseCsg` → `buildCsgShape`) serves `loadBRep`/`loadBRepCached`/`exportBRep`/mass properties/entity facts/diff/silhouette/compare with zero per-caller changes. Format unions widened ONLY in `occtService.ts` (4 sites) — every other file's `"step"|"iges"|"brep"` triple still compiles untouched (`as` casts from `CadFormat` overlap either way) and works at runtime through that same branch. `BRepResult`/`BRepCacheEntry` gained additive `warnings` (parse/build notes — skipped constructs, faceting approximations); the cache carries them so the append-reuse path still reports without rebuilding. Kernel worker/client needed zero changes (generic pass-through + generic marshal).
- **Faceting is honest, not silent.** `resolveSegments` reimplements OpenSCAD's own rule (`$fn > 0` wins, else `max(5, ceil(360/fa), ceil(2πr/fs))` with `$fa=12`/`$fs=2` defaults); `useMaxFN` (default 16, FreeCAD's dial) decides real N-gon prism vs analytic, and every approximation carries its chord error into `load_model` warnings. `bracket.csg`'s volume is hand-derived analytic (plate 4000 + boss 1000 − 500 overlap − 10.25× decagon-area hole + touching foot 1000 = **5228.88**) — the live kernel reports 5228.8840, a genuine cross-check pinned in `mcp:smoke` at ±0.5%, not a value copied from the implementation.
- **A test-premise error worth recording.** The first full-pipeline probe's "realistic" case FAILED at 3858.63 vs an expected 3717.26 — the kernel was right and the expectation wrong: the hole cylinder is uncentered (base z=0), so it overlaps the plate over 5 of its 12 units, not all of them. Same class as the `hit_test` bull-vs-box incident: a weaker assertion ("nonzero volume") would have passed and hidden it.
- **Verified**: `csgImport.test.ts` (16 cases incl. the `faces=[[..]]` pre-scan, debug-modifier stripping, 10MB refusal), `fileRouter`/`exportTargets` routing cases, full `loadBRep`→`exportBRep`→STEP round trip through the real pipeline, `load_model`/`get_mass_properties`/`export_brep`/`generate_mesh` through the real `dist/mcp-server.js` over stdio, and `npm run mcp:smoke` (bracket analytic volume + 30-face structure + clean warnings; mixed.csg hull-skip + faceted-sphere warnings; STEP round trip re-verified via a separate load+mass pair).
- **Verification gap, stated plainly**: opening a `.csg` in a real Extension Development Host session (glue: selector registration, open-dialog filter, `handleBRep` status posts) has NOT been exercised — same caveat as every other `provider.ts`-touching feature. See "Verify a change" below.

## OpenSCAD `.scad` via installed binary (roadmap Tier 2 item 2, path (b), closed)

`.scad` evaluates to `.csg` through a user-installed `openscad` binary, then takes path (a)'s exact pipeline — no new kernel surface, no new `EditOp` kind, no new MCP tool, no new `Pipeline` key. The item is fully closed; only the rejected path (c) survives, as a Non-goals entry.

- **Host-side conversion, worker untouched — decided by the include-path constraint, not taste.** `.scad` `use`/`include`/`import` resolve relative to the source file, but the kernel worker only ever receives marshalled bytes — converting there would silently break every multi-file model with no error to catch it. `src/scadService.ts` converts on the real path with `cwd = dirname(sourcePath)` (never a temp copy), the output `.csg` goes to a temp dir always removed afterwards (success or failure), and everything downstream only ever sees `format: "csg"` — which is why no `BRepFormat` alias needed `"scad"`, no kernel-worker/IPC change was needed, and the `readShape` `"csg"` branch serves every consumer untouched.
- **Two failure rules, mirroring `renderService.ts`.** Binary absent → `ScadUnavailableError`, mapped to `{supported: false}` + install hint at every entry point (never a throw for a missing capability — the need-more-info verdict convention; tools without a `supported` field — `generate_mesh`/`export_mesh`/`export_brep`/silhouette — throw the hint as a clear error instead, since they have no graceful shape). Binary present but conversion fails → thrown plain `Error` (non-zero exit carries the stderr tail; timeout kills the child and says so; empty/missing output is a failure, never an empty model). `execFile` with argv, never a shell.
- **Timeout as sandbox, not UX.** `SCAD_CONVERT_TIMEOUT_MS` (120s, fixed constant) exists because a `.scad` file is effectively evaluated code — recursion or a gigantic `import` can hang — the same reasoning as the kernel watchdog, one layer up. Binary resolution is `cadPreview.openscadBinary` setting → `OPENSCAD_BINARY` env (the headless escape hatch) → bare `openscad` on `PATH`; empty strings count as unset at every level.
- **Wiring is one helper per side.** `resolveEffectiveSource()` (pure dispatch: non-scad passes through byte-identical) backs `readOcctSource()` in `mcpTools.ts` (each occt tool maps `{ok:false}` to its own `supported:false` shape) and `provider.ts`'s private `readOcctSource()` (existing catches already post the message — it IS the hint) plus `modelComparePanel.ts`'s `resolveCompareSource` (maps to `{source: undefined}`). Mutation-path tools (`apply_edit_ops`, parametric scripts) persist sidecar ops even when the replay preview is skipped for a missing binary — the mesh-flow precedent, not a failure. Meshing/export request paths that cannot degrade (`resolveMeshInputHeadless`, `exportBRepTool`, silhouette) throw the hint instead.
- **One incidental fix riding along:** the File ▸ Open dialog filter never listed `.csg` (the path-(a) commit wired selector, router, and compare filter but missed the dialog) — it now lists both `.csg` and `.scad`.
- **Verified**: `scadService.test.ts` (14 cases over committed stub binaries through the REAL `execFile` plumbing — arg shape/`-o` output extension/real input path/`cwd` asserted off a record file, fail/timeout/vanished-binary mapping, temp-dir cleanup, non-scad passthrough) plus `load_model` fake-pipeline cases (unavailable → null inventory + hint without touching WASM; stub → `loadBRep` called with converted bytes as `"csg"`, source still reported as `"scad"`), routing/caps- matrix cases, full `vitest`, and `mcp:smoke` (binary-present branch asserts the 30-face structure + analytic volume against the `.csg` oracle when `OPENSCAD_BINARY` is set; default branch asserts the graceful degradation).
- **Verification gaps, stated plainly.** (1) NO `openscad` binary exists in this environment or CI, so the `-o out.csg in.scad` invocation shape follows FreeCAD's documented architecture but is UNVERIFIED against a real binary — the stub proves plumbing, not flag fidelity; one manual conversion where openscad exists, diffing volume against the `.csg` oracle, is required before release. (2) Interactive open carries the standard F5-only caveat, same as `.csg`.

## Native 6-DOF SpaceMouse input (roadmap Tier 2 item 2, closed)

`node-hid` (MIT OR X11 — license text + `node-addon-api`-only deps verified at install; NAPI, so ABI-stable across Node/Electron versions) reads a 3Dconnexion-class device in the extension host and pipes motion to the webview, mirroring the OCCT-in-host/Three.js-in-webview split. Opt-in via `cad-preview.spaceMouseConnect` (never auto-started — mirrors lazy-WASM); motion routes to the focused session, or drops when none is focused.

- **Packaging: big-3 prebuilds in the universal vsix, no publishing change.** `node-hid` is esbuild-`external` on the extension bundle ONLY (a native `.node` cannot bundle — esbuild would inline the JS loader and orphan the binary) plus exact-file `.vscodeignore` carve-outs for `package.json`/`nodehid.js` and the linux-x64 + darwin-arm64/x64 + win32-x64 prebuilds (~1MB total). Every other platform gets a clear "unsupported platform" message, never a crash. The MCP/kernel bundles never import the HID path. `require("node-hid")` is lazy inside `connectSpaceMouse()` — a missing/unloadable package degrades to the same message, so activation never depends on it.
- **Discovery is ranking, not allowlisting** (`src/spaceMouseRank.ts`, pure, unit-tested with SindriCAD's own vectors): Generic Desktop / Multi-axis Controller (0x01/0x08) outranks everything; mouse (1/2) and keyboard (1/6) collections are definitive NOs even under a matching vendor id (the shipped Logitech-collision bug this exists to prevent); vendor-only matches stay UNPROVEN. Only the BEST rank is ever opened — no fallthrough (on Linux "listed but won't open" means the udev rule is missing, and quietly opening another Logitech device instead is precisely the bug). `node-hid` exposes no report-descriptor API (verified against its installed `.d.ts`), so the UNPROVEN proof step degrades to stream-with-a-loud-note, SindriCAD's `Proof::Unavailable` branch verbatim.
- **Reports parse to normalized velocities, never camera deltas** (`src/spaceMouseReports.ts`, pure, unit-tested): report IDs 1/2/3 with split-report `{t, r}` merge, i16-LE sign extension, truncation-to-zero; deadzone + clamp to [-1, 1]. Sign conventions are flagged UNVERIFIED per-field (no hardware here — each is one boolean flip if wrong); `rz` is deliberately unmapped (OrbitControls has no roll axis — documented, not faked). `src/webview/spaceMouseDispatch.ts` (pure, unit-tested against a fake viewer) scales by clamped inter-event dt and dispatches to `panView`/`zoomView`/`rotateView` — which all end in `controls.update()`, so view-state autosave and render-on-demand compose for free; a resting puck issues zero calls (render loop stays asleep). Buttons: bit 0 → Fit, bit 1 → Reset on rising edges only (model-dependent assumption, flagged).
- **Host service discipline** (`src/spaceMouse.ts`): `device.on("data")` streams, `device.on("error")` closes + 3s reconnect (re-ranks every time — never a stale path across unplug/replug); every async rejection handled (node-hid warns unhandled ones can crash the host); `disconnectSpaceMouse()` also runs from `deactivate()`. Linux "found but won't open" surfaces the `SUBSYSTEM=="hidraw"` udev rule text (a `usb`-subsystem rule does nothing — SindriCAD's hard-won finding) plus the spacenavd-conflict note.
- **Verified without hardware**: `tsc`, full `vitest` (rank/descriptor/report/dispatch suites; this machine's own 16-interface HID enumeration — Logitech receivers included — was manually confirmed all-rejected by the ranker), `npm run build`. Deliberately NOT covered: Electron-host native load (F5), sign/scale feel on a real device, udev flow — all need hardware; per-second speeds and deadzone ship as flagged tune-on-hardware constants, and there is no settings UI in v1.
- **Out of scope on purpose**: no toolbar/menu UI (commands only), no MCP tool (genuinely interactive, no headless equivalent — same precedent as Markup/Measurement), no `test:webview`/`mcp:smoke` coverage (no viewer markup changed; nothing headless to drive).

## Build & test

```bash
npm install        # or: npm ci (CI)
npm run build      # esbuild: extension (node/cjs) + MCP server (node/cjs) + webview (browser/iife) + tsc --noEmit
npm run watch      # rebuild on change
npm test           # unit tests via vitest
npm run mcp:smoke  # build + real-WASM end-to-end MCP server test over stdio (bull.stp)
npm run perf        # build + performance regression benchmark over stdio (graded STEP fixtures)
```

```bash
npm run test:webview      # build + fixtures + Playwright assertions over the real viewer bundle
npm run test:integration  # build + a real VS Code (Electron) running the host-side suite
```

- **Both of the above need a display server on Linux** — the first launches headless Chromium, the second a real VS Code. CI runs each under `xvfb-run -a`; locally, run them from a normal desktop session. First run only: `npx playwright install chromium` (the integration harness downloads its own VS Code into the git-ignored `.vscode-test/`).
- **This line used to claim the opposite and was wrong for a long time**, asserting "Integration tests need a display server; CI runs them under `xvfb-run` on Linux" while `ci.yml` in fact carried a standing `NOTE: @vscode/test-electron integration tests are added in a later milestone` and no such test existed. Recorded because the false claim made the verification story look materially better than it was. See "Webview and host-side test harnesses" below for what the two harnesses do and do not cover.

## Webview and host-side test harnesses (roadmap Tier 1, Phase 2a + first gate of 2b, closed)

`npm test` (vitest) and `npm run mcp:smoke` cover the pure modules and the whole host/MCP pipeline well. Until this work the **webview and the VS Code host API had zero automated coverage** — every webview feature's write-up in this file ends with "has NOT been exercised in a real Extension Development Host session", and nothing existed to change that. Two harnesses now do, and both run in CI.

### `npm run test:webview` — assertions over the REAL viewer bundle (42 checks)

`scripts/webview-test/run.mjs` drives the same headless-Chromium harness the screenshot pipeline uses (`media/viewer.js` + `viewerDom.ts`'s own markup + real OCCT/Gmsh fixtures) and **asserts** against it. `capture.mjs` had driven that harness for a long time while asserting *nothing*, which is exactly how an exit-0 run could coexist with every 3D shot being silently misframed.

- **`scripts/screenshots/harness.mjs` is now shared** by `capture.mjs` and the assertion runner — deliberately breaking this repo's usual "don't factor between `scripts/*` entries" rule.
